    8  # v3.22: THE BRACKET LAYOUT DISCLOSES ITS POST-HOC AND ITS CORRECTION.
    9  #        AUTHOR RULING, 16 August 2026. Ruling 1b gave the MATRIX layout a
   10  #        sub-line naming the post-hoc test and, on the parametric arm, saying
   11  #        where its family-wise control comes from. The BRACKET layout was left
   12  #        with nothing — and the brackets are the layout that puts the p-values
   13  #        and the stars directly ON the picture, so it is the one a reader is
   14  #        most likely to read a claim off. The figure carried three stars over
   15  #        a pair and did not say which test produced them or whether anything
   16  #        had been corrected.
   17  #        WHAT THE AUTHOR ASKED, AND THE ANSWER THAT SET THE SHAPE. SPSS states
   18  #        "Adjustment for multiple comparisons: Bonferroni" beneath its
   19  #        pairwise-comparisons display. Base R states nothing, but every R
   20  #        package that DRAWS significance brackets discloses:
   21  #        ggpubr::stat_compare_means prints the method on the plot by default,
   22  #        and ggstatsplot carries the pairwise test AND the adjustment method
   23  #        in a caption on every figure. The caption is the closest precedent
   24  #        and it is what this is.
   25  #        [1] Two new globals, @emlBridgeGroupComparison writes both:
   26  #            annotBracketPosthoc$ names the pairwise test, annotBracketAdjust$
   27  #            says what was done about multiplicity. THEY ARE STORED AS TWO
   28  #            HALVES, not as one sentence, and that is not tidiness — it is
   29  #            what lets @emlDrawBracketCaption break the caption at its seam on
   30  #            a narrow figure without doing string surgery on a sentence, and
   31  #            it is what makes "the two arms say different things" a property
   32  #            a validator can read rather than infer.
   33  #        [2] THE TWO ARMS SAY DIFFERENT THINGS, and the asymmetry is the
   34  #            content. Tukey's p comes from the studentized range distribution
   35  #            and is already family-wise over the set of pairwise comparisons,
   36  #            so the parametric arm STATES that and claims no further
   37  #            adjustment. Dunn's z-tests carry no family-wise control of their
   38  #            own, the form still offers the menu there and @emlDunnTest still
   39  #            honours it, so the nonparametric arm NAMES the method the user
   40  #            chose — the same token the Info-window report prints — and
   41  #            claims nothing about what it achieves. One sentence covering both
   42  #            would be false on one of them whichever way it was written.
   43  #        [3] PLACEMENT IS A CAPTION UNDER THE FRAME, not a second box inside
   44  #            it. annotTextX/annotTextY is a single slot with an alpha
   45  #            background already carrying the omnibus line, and a second box
   46  #            inside the frame would collide with the very brackets it
   47  #            describes — the brackets occupy the top of the plot by
   48  #            construction and @emlPlaceElements penalises the top quadrants
   49  #            for exactly that reason. The caption takes its own band below
   50  #            emlSetAdaptiveTheme.outerBottom and reports that band to
   51  #            @emlExpandDrawnExtent, the mechanism @emlDrawMatrixPanel already
   52  #            uses, so @emlAssertFullViewport widens the saved image to include
   53  #            it instead of cropping it off.
   54  #        [4] IT FITS OR IT SHRINKS, AND IF IT STILL DOES NOT FIT IT WRAPS. The
   55  #            caption is measured with Text width (mm) against the band it has,
   56  #            scaled down to a 5 pt floor, and split onto two lines at the seam
   57  #            between the two halves if one line still overruns. A disclosure
   58  #            that runs off the canvas is not a disclosure, and it is the
   59  #            failure mode a check anchored on where the FIRST ink falls cannot
   60  #            see: clipping takes ink off the RIGHT and leaves the left edge
   61  #            exactly where it was.
   62  #        [5] The two-group arms deliberately write empty strings. Two groups
   63  #            is one comparison: there is no post-hoc and no family to correct,
   64  #            so this ruling, which is about a post-hoc method and a
   65  #            correction, has nothing to say there, and a caption would be a
   66  #            claim about a correction that never happened.
   67  #        [6] AND ONE THING THIS CHANGE FOUND AND DID NOT CLOSE, because
   68  #            closing it is a ruling and not an implementation detail. Neither
   69  #            two-group arm sets annotTextN -- only the k >= 3 arms do -- so
   70  #            the form has no omnibus line to route into the corner box and a
   71  #            two-group BRACKET figure names no test anywhere on it at all.
   72  #            harness/bracketcap/out/welch_two.png shows a bracket, a star
   73  #            triple and a Cohen's d, and nothing that says a Welch t-test
   74  #            produced them. That is the same defect this ruling is about, one
   75  #            arm further down, and it is recorded as an attestation in
   76  #            validate/v69 rather than repaired on this pass.
   77  #
   78  # v3.21: THE ANNOTATION BRIDGE NOW DECLARES. @emlReportBridgeStats ran the
   79  #        analysis, printed it and filled the legacy CSV buffer, but never
   80  #        called the @emlDeclare*Result family -- so an annotated graph's
   81  #        export could only ever be the old single long-format file, for an
   82  #        analysis the stats menu exported as tidy/glance/augment. Added the
   83  #        same declaration calls the orchestrators make, for all three families
   84  #        the bridge produces: two-group, one-way ANOVA with Tukey, and
   85  #        Kruskal-Wallis with Dunn. Extras staged FIRST in every case, because
   86  #        staging reuses the one tidy collector and the model's own tidy has to
   87  #        be what is left in it when @emlResultWrite runs. No new reporting
   88  #        code: these are calls to the procedures the stats path already uses.
```

   91  # v3.20: THE LEGEND IS AN ANNOTATION, AND NOW PAYS FOR ITS OWN ROOM.
   92  #        eml-draw-procedures.praat has said since 5 Aug that "any extra room
   93  #        a figure needs is a property of what is drawn on it ... and is
   94  #        supplied by @emlComputeAnnotationHeadroom at the annotation stage".
   95  #        Only the significance bracket honoured it. A legend drawn inside the
   96  #        plot is an annotation box by the same definition and contributed
   97  #        NOTHING to the axis, so the figure was never given room for it and
   98  #        it landed on the data — 13145 data pixels covered on a five-group
   99  #        line chart at 6 x 4, measured on the rendered PNG, 9 Aug 2026, by
  100  #        differencing it against the same figure on the same axis with the
  101  #        legend suppressed. The worst of the six types measured 23702.
  102  #        [1] @emlComputeAnnotationHeadroom takes two more arguments — the
  103  #            laid-out legend height in inches (from @emlMeasureLegendPanel,
  104  #            the same measurement the renderer lays itself out with, so the
  105  #            two cannot disagree) and the corner it will occupy — and returns
  106  #            .footroom beside .headroom, because a legend in a bottom corner
  107  #            is not helped by room above the data. Both bands go into ONE
  108  #            algebraic solve: the expansion is not linear in the band, so
  109  #            solving twice and adding would be wrong. With no legend the
  110  #            outputs are bit-identical to v3.19, and .overflow still means
  111  #            "the brackets do not fit" and nothing else.
  112  #        [2] The legend band is capped at emlLegendHeadroomShare (default 0.5)
  113  #            of the panel, so a 24-entry key cannot leave a figure with no
  114  #            plot. A capped band is NOT silently swallowed: .legendOverflow
  115  #            goes up and the caller names the shortfall in the Info window.
  116  #        [3] @emlPlaceElements is UNCHANGED, deliberately — see the note in
  117  #            its header for why penalising quadrants by legend size does not
  118  #            make the corner choice and the headroom agree, and what does.
  119  #
  120  # v3.19: D124 — @emlDrawAnnotationBlock wraps.
  121  #        [1] The box was sized to its longest line exactly as handed in, so
  122  #            one long disclosure line made it wide enough to sit over the
  123  #            data and, on a narrow figure, to run off the canvas. Lines are
  124  #            now wrapped through @emlWrapText to a budget taken from the
  125  #            frame (emlAnnotBlockWidthShare, 0.55 of the inner viewport less
  126  #            the box's padding and inset), never from a character constant —
  127  #            annotSize scales with the viewport, so a character is a near
  128  #            constant FRACTION of the panel and "keep it under 50
  129  #            characters" has always meant "up to seven eighths of the panel".
  130  #            Measured 8 Aug 2026 on a 3.6 x 3 inch 4-bar chart: box width
  131  #            fell from 1.79 to 0.52 of the axis range, and the rightmost
  132  #            inked pixel column from 1079 (the canvas edge, i.e. clipped)
  133  #            to 929, which is exactly where a figure carrying no box at all
  134  #            ends. On the real @emlDisclose path the box fell from 0.75 to
  135  #            0.49 of the frame and the data pixels it covered from 28 to 0.
  136  #        [2] Wrapping can only trade width for height, so the box is
  137  #            re-wrapped wider (to at most 0.72) when it would otherwise
  138  #            stand taller than the frame. Nothing is ever truncated.
  139  #        [3] Praat markup survives the break: a wrapped line's Draw string is
  140  #            cut at the same word boundaries as its Label, guarded by a word
  141  #            count check, so the omnibus "%H(3) = ..., %p = ..." keeps its
  142  #            italics on both halves. New helper @emlSpaceCount.
  143  #        [4] Comment corrections. The wizard pointer for the skew/kurtosis
  144  #            criterion was :2085 (a "Data column:" padding line) and is now
  145  #            :2170-2175. The D95 note claimed the gate's kurtosis threshold
  146  #            was 1; it is 7 (emlSkewThreshold 2 / emlKurtosisThreshold 7 in
  147  #            stats/eml-output.praat), which reverses the direction of the
  148  #            contradiction it describes — rewritten to match the code, the
  149  #            threshold itself untouched. Two eml-analysis.praat pointers had
  150  #            drifted (:2961 now sits in @emlDeclareKWResult, :2194 in
  151  #            @emlRMAnovaTest's arithmetic) and now name their anchors.
  152  #            @emlCSVAddRow, named as the reporters' CSV entry point, does
  153  #            not exist anywhere in the plugin — the real ones are @emlCSVAdd
  154  #            / @emlCSVAddStr / @emlCSVAddDescriptives. The Procedures index
  155  #            listed 15 of the file's 26. v2.7's "all 6 test paths" is 4,
  156  #            which is what its own list names and what the code has.
  157  #            annotBlockLabel$'s "max 20 lines" is @emlDisclose's disclosure
  158  #            cap, not the array's — the form appends a 21st.
  159  #
  160  # v3.18: Audit fixes (annotation/stats-bridge layer).
  161  #        [1] @emlClearAnnotations now clears annotMatrixLabel$[] over the

  362  # v1.3: @emlDrawAnnotationBlock and @emlDrawAnnotation: replaced charW
  363  #        estimation (length * fixedWidth) with exact text measurement via
  364  #        Text width (world coordinates): — queries the rendering engine
  365  #        for actual rendered string width in the current font/size/axes.
  366  #        Fixes box sizing for proportional fonts (the fundamental flaw in
  367  #        all prior charW-based approaches). Symmetric vertical padding.
  368  #        @emlDrawRegressionLine: white halo (width 3.5) drawn under the
  369  #        colored line for visibility against dense data.
  370  #        Semi-transparent backgrounds via alpha sprite for annotation block
  371  #        (falls back to opaque white if sprites unavailable).
  372  # v1.2: charW estimation attempts (superseded by v1.3).
  373  # v1.1: Spearman label split (Info window plain text vs Picture window
  374  #        markup). New @emlDrawAnnotationBlock for multi-line text boxes
  375  #        with background fill and corner positioning. New @emlOppositeCorner.
  376  #        annotBlockN/annotBlockLabel$/annotBlockDraw$ data structures.
```

    8  # v1.26: COMMENTS ONLY — no executable line changed. The D124 wave falsified
    9  #        this file's account of the annotation block; three statements fixed.
   10  #        (1) @emlDisclose's contract said "@emlDrawAnnotationBlock does not
   11  #        wrap: the box is as wide as its longest line" and "KEEP .short$ UNDER
   12  #        ABOUT 50 CHARACTERS". The block wraps now — every entry is broken
   13  #        through @emlWrapText against emlAnnotBlockWidthShare (default 0.55 of
   14  #        the inner viewport), with a height guard that re-wraps wider, to at
   15  #        most 0.72, only when the box would exceed emlAnnotBlockHeightShare
   16  #        (0.95) of the frame. The character rule was never a width guarantee
   17  #        even before that: annotSize scales WITH the viewport, so a
   18  #        48-character line took 0.68 of the inner width at 6 x 4 inches and
   19  #        0.88 at 3.6 x 3 — inside the old rule, over half the panel at both.
   20  #        The contract now states the share, what the 20-line budget actually
   21  #        counts, and why shorter is still better.
   22  #        (2) The LINE BUDGET note claimed @emlDrawAnnotationBlock's contract
   23  #        documents the 20-line maximum. It does not and never did — it points
   24  #        back here. Rewritten to say what the 20 is: a cap on @emlDisclose's
   25  #        own entries, not on annotBlockN (@emlDrawScatterPlot and
   26  #        eml-graphs-form.praat both append outside it) and not on drawn rows,
   27  #        which wrapping makes larger again.
   28  #        (3) The @emlDiscloseBegin counting rule said the indent excludes
   29  #        "the three mentions of it in comments"; there are eight, and the
   30  #        figure moves whenever one of these comments is reworded, so the rule
   31  #        no longer quotes it.
   32  # v1.25: COMMENTS ONLY — no executable line changed. Eight stale statements
   33  #        corrected: counts that disagreed with the list or the code under
   34  #        them, and pointers to things that do not exist. Three came from the
   35  #        7 Aug 2026 contradiction sweep
   36  #        (audit/reviews/CONTRADICTION_SWEEP_2026-08-07.md, C10); the rest
   37  #        from a file-wide sweep for the same class of error.
   38  #        (1) "Contains all 14 drawing procedures" (v1.0 entry below): there
   39  #        are 15. The count now carries the rule that derives it, under
   40  #        Procedures, so the next reader does not have to trust it.
   41  #        (2) The Procedures list itself was missing @emlDrawLMMForest, which
   42  #        is how the 14 went unnoticed: the list agreed with it.
   43  #        (3) "Real implementations for all 7 graph types" above the DRAWING
   44  #        PROCEDURES section: two different counts of one thing in one header,
   45  #        neither of them 15.
   46  #        (4) "the MAIN EXECUTION section below": there is no such section in
   47  #        this file or anywhere in the plugin. The dispatch it meant is the
   48  #        DISPATCH (DRAW) block of eml-graphs-form.praat, now named.
   49  #        (5) @emlDrawTimeSeries's header still described drawing modes B and
   50  #        C (spaghetti strands by ID, mean overlay) and a CI band. The
   51  #        procedure takes no .idCol$, no .showMean and no band columns — the
   52  #        banner immediately under it has said "reverted, modes A + D only"
   53  #        the whole time. The palette line was also pre-v1.24: point marks
   54  #        now cycle 8 hues x 3 marker shapes, not 8 hues.
   55  #        (6) @emlDrawHistogram's header called its second grouped mode
   56  #        "side-by-side". The code at Step 9-10 draws FACETED vertically
   57  #        stacked panels with shared axes; every other mention in the file
   58  #        says faceted. Also: eml-output.praat added to Dependencies (the
   59  #        spaghetti summary calls @emlReportHeader and four siblings) and
   60  #        @emlDrawGroupedBoxPlot's "See the note above Step 3's equivalent"
   61  #        repointed, that procedure having no numbered steps.
   62  #
   63  # v1.24: MARKER SHAPES for the four chart types that draw dots and lines.
   64  #        v1.23 gave the AREA marks a second dimension (8 hues x 3 fill
   65  #        patterns) and left the point marks where they were: a scatter, a
   66  #        line chart, a spaghetti plot and a time series have no area to fill,
   67  #        so all four went on cycling eight hues in silence above eight
   68  #        groups. Each now draws through @emlDrawMarker and takes its shape
   69  #        from emlSetColorPalette.marker[] -- circle, square, triangle, on the
   70  #        same slot arithmetic, so the ceiling is 24 for both families -- and
   71  #        each sets the legend's marker key so the key shows the shape and not
   72  #        merely the hue. @emlDrawTimeSeries and @emlDrawTimeSeriesCI drew no
   73  #        point markers at all before this and now place one at every plotted
   74  #        vertex that lies on the panel (not clamped: a clamped marker would
   75  #        claim an observation at a value nobody measured).
   76  #        @emlDrawScatterPlot additionally stands its alpha-sprite path down
   77  #        above eight groups -- the sprite set is circles only, so on macOS
   78  #        and Windows, where the sprites actually render, the shape would
   79  #        never have reached the page. Its B/W point colour moves from .fill$
   80  #        to .lightLine$, because the widened grey ramp takes .fill$ to 0.94
   81  #        and a 0.94 dot with no outline is not on the page at all.
   82  #
   83  # v1.23: The sub-group ceiling goes from 10 to 24. @emlSetColorPalette now
   84  #        defines a style as (one of 8 hues) x (one of 3 fill patterns:
   85  #        solid, diagonal hatch, dots), so 24 sub-groups can be drawn
   86  #        distinguishably where 10 could not — the old ten included two
   87  #        literal duplicates (D127) and refused eight styles that were
   88  #        genuinely available. @emlDrawGroupedViolin and @emlDrawGroupedBoxPlot
   89  #        pass the pattern through to the primitives, the legend carries it,
   90  #        and the over-cap disclosure names the 8 x 3 space rather than a bare
   91  #        count. Set on the author's ruling that the boundary be "in excess of
   92  #        what we think is reasonable" — 24 sub-violins are not readable on a
   93  #        default 6-inch figure and are perfectly readable on a wide one, and
   94  #        that is the user's call to make.
   95  #
   96  # v1.22: Three more silences, all found by the v1.21 disclosure work — a
   97  #        procedure that knew it had dropped something and did not say so.
   98  #        Same ruling, same three channels, same helpers.
   99  #        (1) THE OVER-CAP SCATTER. @emlDrawScatterPlot's grouped path writes
  100  #        a Pearson line, a Spearman line and a fitted-line formula PER GROUP
  101  #        straight into annotBlockN, bypassing @emlDisclose and its 20-line
  102  #        budget. At eight groups that is 24 lines: measured 7 Aug 2026, the
  103  #        box ran from the top of the panel, across the x-axis and off the
  104  #        bottom edge of the figure, and the dropped-row disclosure that
  105  #        followed was refused by the spent budget and never appeared. The
  106  #        lines are now buffered and committed ALL OR NONE — a box holding six
  107  #        of eight groups is indistinguishable from a complete one — and when
  108  #        they do not fit, every one of them is printed to the Info window and
  109  #        the figure says so in one line.
  110  #        (2) ZERO IS NOT "NO DATA". @emlMeasureBarData (v3.23, in
  111  #        eml-graph-procedures.praat) seeded emlBarData_mean[] and
  112  #        emlBarData_error[] to 0 rather than undefined, so this file's
  113  #        "<> undefined" guards for the bar and the whisker could never fire:
  114  #        .nSkippedBars and .nSkippedErrors were dead code and both
  115  #        disclosures reading them were unreachable. A group with no usable
  116  #        observation drew as a bar of height zero — the same mark a measured
  117  #        zero draws — and a whisker whose error was undefined simply did not
  118  #        appear. Fixed at the root; both guards now fire, the omitted bars
  119  #        are NAMED, and the v1.21 stopgap line "N group(s) shown at zero" is
  120  #        gone with the behaviour it described.
  121  #        (3) THE ELEVENTH SUB-GROUP. @emlDrawGroupedViolin and
  122  #        @emlDrawGroupedBoxPlot drop sub-groups past the tenth because
  123  #        @emlSetColorPalette declares ten fill/line pairs over eight hues.
  124  #        THE CAP IS LEGITIMATE AND IS NOT RAISED. It was silent, and the
  125  #        legend still listed sub-groups that had no violin or box anywhere on
  126  #        the figure. Now disclosed by name and row count, the legend is
  127  #        capped to what was drawn, and the slot geometry divides by the drawn
  128  #        count so the remaining violins stay centred.
  129  #        Verified by validate/v29_figure_disclosure.R.
  130  # v1.21: Figure disclosure unified across the ten Table-consuming draw
  131  #        procedures, to the author's ruling of 7 Aug 2026 — "Let's draw the
  132  #        image as the image unless someone asks to annotate."
  133  #        (1) Nothing in this file writes to emlSubtitle$ any more.
  134  #        @emlDrawTimeSeries appended " | Mean per time point" and
  135  #        @emlDrawBarChart appended an error-bar/truncation/omitted-bars
  136  #        caption; both saved and restored the global, so it was never
  137  #        permanently corrupted, but the DRAWN figure carried the user's own
  138  #        subtitle with a machine tail after " | ", and drew it whether or not
  139  #        Annotate was ticked. emlSubtitle$ is the user's field: the graphs
  140  #        form asks for it and persists it to config.
  141  #        (2) All ten disclose dropped rows, in one wording and one counter
  142  #        idiom taken from @emlDrawViolinPlot and @emlDrawBoxPlot, which were
  143  #        the only two that did. Silent before: histogram, bar, scatter,
  144  #        grouped violin, grouped box, spaghetti, time series; time series
  145  #        with CI had the count but in its own words and indentation.
  146  #        (3) The four procedures that plot a MEAN rather than raw values now
  147  #        all say so — @emlDrawTimeSeries already did, @emlDrawTimeSeriesCI,
  148  #        @emlDrawBarChart and the line chart (graph type 5 with CI off, which
  149  #        IS @emlDrawTimeSeries) did not.
  150  #        (4) New @emlDiscloseBegin / @emlDisclose / @emlDiscloseEnd carry all
  151  #        of the above: Info window always, figure only when the user ticked
  152  #        Annotate, through the existing annotation block. See the block
  153  #        comment on those procedures for why the gate could not simply be
  154  #        left to @emlDrawAnnotationBlock.
  155  #        Verified by validate/v29_figure_disclosure.R.
  156  # v1.20: @emlDrawLMMForest brought into line with the drawing standards.
  157  #        (1) Rule 1: the bare `Marks bottom: 5` is replaced by
  158  #        @emlDrawAlignedMarksBottom with theme-derived tick targets, so the
  159  #        12%-buffered coefficient axis gets nice-number labels instead of
  160  #        arbitrary quarter-range values. (2) Rule 2: the procedure now opens
  161  #        with the theme prologue (@emlSetAdaptiveTheme / @emlSetColorPalette)
  162  #        that the other 14 draw procedures already used — it was the only one
  163  #        without it, so its margins, and therefore its box, ticks and labels,
  164  #        previously depended on whatever ambient font size the Picture window
  165  #        happened to hold. (3) Because the LMM tool reaches this procedure
  166  #        outside the graphs form, the display-toggle globals and colorMode$
  167  #        are seeded through a variableExists guard — without it the new marks
  168  #        call aborts with "Unknown variable" on the shipped path. (4) Rule 34:
  169  #        line widths, marker size, tick colour, text colour and the series
  170  #        colour now come from the theme and palette rather than hardcoded
  171  #        constants and bare Grey/Black; the four inlined replace$ calls are
  172  #        replaced by @emlSanitizeLabel. Deliberately deferred: adopting
  173  #        @emlDrawTitle, which would require converting the figure from the
  174  #        outer-viewport layout model to the theme's inner-viewport model.
  175  #        No arguments or return variables change.
  176  # v1.19: Robustness and disclosure fixes. (1) @emlDrawHistogram ungrouped
  177  #        path no longer aborts on clean single-column data — the group loop
  178  #        is driven by .hasGroups instead of the always-1 .nGroups, and the
  179  #        dead else branch is removed. (2) @emlDrawScatterPlot (ungrouped and
  180  #        grouped) now draws the same estimator it reports, labels the drawn
  181  #        line, and discloses the estimator unconditionally instead of gating
  182  #        the disclosure on scatterAnalysisType. (3) @emlDrawBarChart guards
  183  #        every undefined mean/error before it reaches a drawing command — an
  184  #        unusable bar is skipped and recorded rather than aborting the
  185  #        figure — discloses the error-bar meaning (SE / SD / custom column)
  186  #        in the caption and Info window, and marks error bars truncated at
  187  #        the axis limits with outward arrowheads instead of clipping them
  188  #        silently. (4) Axis seeding in @emlDrawTimeSeries and
  189  #        @emlDrawSpaghettiPlot no longer takes row 1 on faith — ranges are
  190  #        folded from the first valid observation, with a 0..1 fallback and
  191  #        an Info-window note when nothing is usable. (5) Guard sweep over
  192  #        the aborting draw procedures, using @emlDrawGroupedViolin as the
  193  #        reference: @emlDrawTimeSeries, @emlDrawSpaghettiPlot,
  194  #        @emlDrawTimeSeriesCI (missing TIME cells no longer create phantom
  195  #        time points), @emlDrawViolinPlot and @emlDrawBoxPlot (undefined
  196  #        values filtered at accumulation so they never reach @emlPercentile's
  197  #        sort#, empty groups skipped, unit-axis fallback). Dropped rows and
  198  #        skipped groups are reported in the Info window.
  199  # v1.18: Graph correctness fixes. (1) Grouped scatter no longer prints a
  200  #        false "R² = 0.000" for Spearman/Theil-Sen fits — R² is emitted only
  201  #        for the OLS/Pearson line (guarded on annotCorrType$, mirroring the
  202  #        ungrouped path). (2) Bar chart y-range routed through
  203  #        @emlComputeAxisRange with the tracked data min, and bar baseline
  204  #        decoupled from yMin (bars emanate from 0) so all-negative means
  205  #        render. (3) Scatter axes use an adaptive @emlComputeNiceStep roundTo
  206  #        instead of a hardcoded 1, so fractional data is not over-ranged.
  207  # v1.17: Per-group scatter correlation output replaced with shared
  208  #        reporter (@emlReportCorrelationAnalysis) for rich Info window output
  209  #        including R-squared, effect size labels, and Why commentary.
```

## plugin/graphs/eml-draw-procedures.praat @ b4ca5e6

Lines 212-277 of plugin/graphs/eml-draw-procedures.praat at b4ca5e6.

```
  212  # v1.16: Group sort unification — replaced all inline group discovery
  213  #         with @emlCountGroups calls: @emlDrawViolinPlot,
  214  #         @emlDrawBoxPlot, @emlDrawGroupedViolin, @emlDrawGroupedBoxPlot,
  215  #         @emlDrawSpaghettiPlot (conditions and subjects). Legend labels
  216  #         sanitized in @emlDrawTimeSeries, @emlDrawTimeSeriesCI, and
  217  #         @emlDrawSpaghettiPlot (underscore→subscript fix). All draw
  218  #         procedures now use single source for group ordering.
  219  #
  220  # v1.15: Bug #3 — 1-group faceted histogram forced through ungrouped
  221  #        path (hasGroups=0, displayMode=1) to prevent viewport mismatch.
  222  #        Bug #11 — semitone auto-range minimum span enforcement now uses
  223  #        direct 6-st rounding instead of re-calling @emlComputeAxisRange.
  224  #        Prevents overshoot to ~30 st for constant-pitch input.
  225  #
  226  # v1.12: @emlDrawTimeSeriesCI: CI critical value changed from hardcoded
  227  #         1.96 (normal approximation) to invStudentQ(annotAlpha/2, n-1)
  228  #         for correct t-distribution intervals at any sample size and
  229  #         confidence level. Reads global annotAlpha (default 0.05 = 95%
  230  #         CI). At n=5 and α=0.05, gives t=2.776 vs the old z=1.96.
  231  # v1.11: Faceted histogram group labels moved from right margin to left
  232  #         margin (Text left:, rotated 90°). Truncation with binary search
  233  #         when label physical width exceeds 85% of panel height. Right
  234  #         margin reverted to standard (no longer widened for labels).
  235  # v1.10: @emlDrawBarChart refactored to pure renderer — all data extraction
  236  #         (unique groups, aggregation, SE/SD/custom error computation) removed.
  237  #         Now reads pre-computed emlBarData_* globals from @emlMeasureBarData
  238  #         (eml-graph-procedures.praat). Signature unchanged for dispatch
  239  #         uniformity.
  240  # v1.9: @emlDrawBarChart signature change — .errorCol$ replaced by
  241  #         .errorMode (0=none, 1=SE, 2=SD, 3=custom) + .errorCol$ (for
  242  #         custom only). SE and SD computed internally from value column
  243  #         per group using single-pass variance (sum of squares). Error
  244  #         bar drawing condition uses .errorMode > 0 instead of .hasError.
  245  # v1.8: Per-axis visibility — all 8 direct axis label draws (Text left/
  246  #        Text bottom) gated by emlShowAxisNameY/emlShowAxisNameX globals.
  247  #        Axis label sanitization removed from all 6 draw procedures —
  248  #        auto-generated labels sanitized at source (@emlCapitalizeLabel),
  249  #        user-typed labels pass through raw to support Praat formatting
  250  #        codes. Title sanitization preserved. Group label sanitization
  251  #        preserved. Scatter alpha sprites always on when available —
  252  #        removed group-column condition gate so ungrouped scatters show
  253  #        density transparency.
  254  # v1.6: Faceted histogram x-axis ticks — replaced Marks bottom: with
  255  #        @emlDrawAlignedMarksBottom for nice-number alignment consistent
  256  #        with gridlines and all other axis tick drawing.
  257  # v1.5: All 12 hardcoded "Helvetica" in Text special: calls replaced
  258  #        with emlFont$ global variable (set by main script font dropdown).
  259  # v1.4: Debug lines removed. All 7 legend callers updated to
  260  #        @emlPlaceElements (adaptive corner selection). Font state
  261  #        fixes: bodySize before all Select inner viewport calls.
  262  #        Faceted histogram restructured: symmetric right margin,
  263  #        gridlines inside branch, group labels outside box.
  264  # v1.3: All Draw inner box calls replaced with @emlDrawInnerBoxIf (font
  265  #        state assertion + boolean toggle). Gridline dispatch updated:
  266  #        continuous types now 4-way (Both/Horizontal/Vertical/Off),
  267  #        categorical types now 2-way (Horizontal/Off).
  268  #
  269  # v1.1: All 6 categorical label sections now use @emlFitCategoricalLabels
  270  #        for space-aware rotation + truncation. Faceted histogram uses
  271  #        per-facet tick count from panel height instead of full canvas.
  272  # v1.0: Extracted from eml-graphs.praat v2.28 to enable shared use by
  273  #        both the graph UI (eml-graphs.praat) and stats wrapper scripts.

## plugin/graphs/eml-graph-procedures.praat @ b4ca5e6

Lines 10-455 of plugin/graphs/eml-graph-procedures.praat at b4ca5e6.

```
   10  # v3.31: THREE RULINGS OF 15 AUGUST 2026, none of which moves a number.
   11  #
   12  #        RULING 7 — THE Y-AXIS NAME AND ITS TICK LABELS NO LONGER TOUCH.
   13  #        Praat's left-margin allocation is fixed: `Text left` anchors the
   14  #        rotated name to the inner frame at a distance set by the font size
   15  #        and by nothing else, tick numbers are right-aligned to that same
   16  #        frame, and about five characters fit between them. Six is the
   17  #        failure edge — a semitone axis reading "-33.08" left three pixels of
   18  #        gap at 300 dpi, and an explicit two-decimal dB axis put "100.10"
   19  #        into "Power (dB)" so that it read "Powe100.10". New
   20  #        @emlTickLabelWidth (what a tick will read as, and how wide) and
   21  #        @emlDrawAxisNameLeft (the name, moved only when the labels have
   22  #        taken the room). @emlDrawAlignedMarksLeft publishes the widest
   23  #        label it drew; @emlDrawAxes and @emlDrawAxesSelective place the
   24  #        name through the new procedure. WIDENING THE PLUGIN'S OWN MARGIN
   25  #        DOES NOT WORK and was measured not working — everything in the
   26  #        margin is anchored to the frame, and the frame moves with the
   27  #        margin. ORDINARY FIGURES ARE BYTE-IDENTICAL: all 39 of
   28  #        harness/stress_graphs.sh re-rendered and compared.
   29  #
   30  #        RULING 5 — Column_k NOW HOLDS SOURCE COLUMN k. @emlCleanConvertedTable
   31  #        numbered manufactured headers by TABLE position, and `To Table: "row"`
   32  #        has already put the label column in position 1 by then, so source
   33  #        column 1 was called "Column_2" and nothing was ever called
   34  #        "Column_1". Reached by both the graphs coercion and the describe
   35  #        wrapper, so one line repaired two doors.
   36  #
   37  #        RULING 8b — ONE PRESS, ONE DERIVED SOUND. The stereo gate keeps the
   38  #        extracted channel Sound on purpose, and kept one per press: three
   39  #        figures from one recording left three Sounds sharing one name. The
   40  #        derived object is now named with an "eml_" prefix — which the gate's
   41  #        header had promised since the gate was written — and new
   42  #        @emlDropStaleChannelSounds collects the previous press's at the top
   43  #        of the next one. Only ever a prefixed name: a channel the user
   44  #        extracted by hand is never touched.
   45  #
   46  # v3.30: THE LEGEND BOX IS SEMI-TRANSPARENT ON EVERY PLATFORM, NOT JUST THE
   47  #        TWO THAT HAVE ALPHA.
   48  #
   49  #        The legend's background is sprites/bg_white_a70_40.png, whose pixels
   50  #        MEASURE RGBA (255, 255, 255, 179) — a 70.2% white wash. Praat can
   51  #        only draw it on macOS and Windows, and @emlInitAlphaSprites is
   52  #        correctly gated to those two: re-confirmed 9 Aug 2026 by rendering
   53  #        the same script with and without `Insert picture from file:` on this
   54  #        Linux build, where `compare -metric AE` scores the two PNGs
   55  #        BYTE-IDENTICAL. Everywhere else the fallback was
   56  #        `Paint rectangle: "White"` — an OPAQUE box. Same script, same figure,
   57  #        and the data under the legend was visible to a mac reader and DELETED
   58  #        for everyone else, with nothing anywhere saying so.
   59  #
   60  #        New section SCREEN-DOOR TRANSPARENCY, with @emlScreenSetup,
   61  #        @emlPaintScreenRect and @emlPaintAlphaBox. Where there is no alpha
   62  #        the box is now a white LATTICE at 0.027 inch pitch — 70% ink, 30%
   63  #        holes — through which the data survives instead of being erased.
   64  #        Measured on rendered pixels: ink 0.740 at 300 dpi and 0.672 at 600,
   65  #        against the sprite's 0.702. The section header carries the two
   66  #        rejected designs (a 45-degree hatch and a hex dot field), the
   67  #        measurements that rejected them, and the finding underneath all
   68  #        three: PRAAT DOES NOT ANTIALIAS AND ROUNDS RECTANGLES OUTWARD BY
   69  #        ABOUT ONE DEVICE PIXEL, which is why the geometry carries a kerf.
   70  #
   71  #        Nothing changes on macOS or Windows. @emlPaintAlphaBox's sprite
   72  #        branch issues the identical `Insert picture from file:` call with the
   73  #        identical arguments and returns .viewportDirty = 1, which is the
   74  #        viewport restore v3.28 added, moved verbatim behind a flag.
   75  #
   76  #        NOT SILENT, either way. @emlPaintAlphaBox publishes emlAlphaBgMode$
   77  #        ("sprite" | "screen" | "opaque") for every box it paints, and puts
   78  #        one NOTE in the Info window per session the first time a figure gets
   79  #        the screen. The sprite path stays quiet: on the platforms where the
   80  #        figure was already right, a note about it is noise.
   81  #
   82  #        NOT APPLIED to @emlDrawAnnotationBlock's background, which has the
   83  #        same gate and the same opaque fallback in
   84  #        eml-annotation-procedures.praat. @emlPaintAlphaBox is written to be
   85  #        called from there unchanged — it takes world coordinates and scales
   86  #        through emlPatWorldPerInchX/Y, which that procedure's callers set —
   87  #        but that file is not this one's to edit.
   88  #
   89  # v3.29: THE LEGEND BAND NO LONGER LANDS ON THE COMPARISON MATRIX WHEN THE
   90  #        CALLER IS NOT THE FORM.
   91  #
   92  #        v3.28 gave placement 3 (Below plot) one way to find out what was
   93  #        already occupying the page under the plot: the global
   94  #        totalCanvasHeight, read through variableExists. Inside the graphs
   95  #        form that is exactly right — the form sizes the comparison-matrix
   96  #        panel before it dispatches the draw and leaves
   97  #        figure_height + matrixGap + matrixPanelHeight there — and rendering
   98  #        the two together confirms it: on a 6 x 4 figure with a four-group
   99  #        Tukey matrix the panel occupies 4.130 to 6.204 inches and the legend
  100  #        band starts at 6.344, one boxInsetInches below it.
  101  #
  102  #        BUT totalCanvasHeight IS A FORM LOCAL. @emlInitDrawingDefaults, the
  103  #        documented entry point for "standalone scripts or PraatGen companion
  104  #        files", sets emlLegendPlacement and does NOT set totalCanvasHeight.
  105  #        A caller outside the form that laid out its own matrix and asked for
  106  #        placement 3 therefore got a band starting at the plot's own bottom
  107  #        edge, drawn straight THROUGH the panel: measured on the same figure,
  108  #        band 4.140 to 4.566 against a panel at 4.130 to 6.204, with the
  109  #        omnibus line and the correction subtitle overprinted by the legend's
  110  #        entries. 11636 dark pixels of legend ink inside the matrix band.
  111  #
  112  #        FIXED by settling the page bottom ONCE, before the placement branch
  113  #        dispatches, from the larger of two sources: the form's
  114  #        totalCanvasHeight, and the matrix's own published measurement
  115  #        (annotMatrixN, emlMatrixLayout_suppressed, emlMatrixLayout_yMax,
  116  #        emlFitCategoricalLabels.overhangInches — all drawing-layer globals,
  117  #        and all necessarily already set, since @emlMeasureMatrixLayout is a
  118  #        precondition of @emlDrawMatrixPanel). Neither source can pull the
  119  #        band up; a form-driven figure is unchanged because the two agree
  120  #        there. Placement 4's park now reads the same page bottom instead of
  121  #        totalCanvasHeight alone, for the same reason.
  122  #
  123  #        Nothing else moved. Placements 1, 2 and 5 do not consult the page
  124  #        bottom, and a figure with no matrix publishes no measurement, so
  125  #        every render in harness/legend/ block 1 and block 2 is bit-identical
  126  #        to v3.28's. Driven and measured on the pixels in
  127  #        harness/legend/run.sh blocks 3 and 4; asserted in
  128  #        validate/v32_legend_geometry.R sections 8 and 9.
  129  #
  130  # v3.28: THE LEGEND STOPS BILLING ITS RENT TO THE PLOT (D136), and D135 —
  131  #        the label wider than the frame — closes with it.
  132  #
  133  #        THE OBJECTION. A user types 6 x 4 and means the DATA AREA. Until
  134  #        now the only place a legend could go was inside the plot, so the
  135  #        only way to give a legend room was to take room from the data, and
  136  #        "make my figure square" became unsatisfiable — a square canvas with
  137  #        a legend carved out of it is not a square plot. The answer is that
  138  #        the legend moves OUT and the EXPORT grows, rather than the plot
  139  #        shrinking IN. The plot rectangle is now identical in all five
  140  #        placements; what changes is how much of the picture is saved.
  141  #
  142  #        NEW: @emlDrawLegendPanel and @emlMeasureLegendPanel, a renderer that
  143  #        is handed a rectangle in inches and a measurer that reports what
  144  #        would fit in one. Both work in a viewport whose world unit is one
  145  #        inch, both set the font size BEFORE selecting it, and the renderer
  146  #        lays itself out by calling the measurer, so the two cannot disagree.
  147  #        The renderer MUST NOT draw outside its rectangle and MUST NOT call
  148  #        @emlExpandDrawnExtent — the caller reports, which is what lets one
  149  #        procedure serve a corner box (must not grow the export) and a side
  150  #        panel (must). Also new: @emlEllipsizeToWidth.
  151  #
  152  #        @emlDrawLegend keeps its signature and every behaviour of an entry —
  153  #        multi-column layout, "+N more" truncation with its Info-window NOTE,
  154  #        the swatch drawn as the mark, the viewport re-selected at legend
  155  #        font size — and becomes a placement dispatcher over the new panel.
  156  #        Placement is the global emlLegendPlacement, read through
  157  #        variableExists and defaulting to 1, so every existing caller (the
  158  #        seven sites in eml-draw-procedures.praat, every stress case, every
  159  #        PraatGen companion) draws exactly what it drew before.
  160  #            1 Inside plot (DEFAULT) — export = plot rectangle, unchanged
  161  #            2 Right of plot         — caller reports; export widens
  162  #            3 Below plot            — caller reports; export heightens
  163  #            4 Separate figure       — parked off-figure, saved as a 2nd file
  164  #            5 None
  165  #        The encoding, its registry and its dialog are in eml-graphs-form.
  166  #
  167  #        D135 CLOSED. "NOT HANDLED HERE: a single label wide enough that one
  168  #        column does not fit the frame width" was true of every revision up
  169  #        to v3.27, and it was structural: a legend that computed its own box
  170  #        had nothing to clamp against. A legend that is GIVEN its bounds
  171  #        does. When one column of full-width labels will not fit, the column
  172  #        is clamped to the width that IS available and @emlEllipsizeToWidth
  173  #        shortens each label to it, so the label comes out as
  174  #        "Extremely long gro..." inside the box instead of running off the
  175  #        canvas — and a NOTE says so, because a silent ellipsis is the same
  176  #        defect as a silent truncation.
  177  #
  178  #        @emlMeasureGraphLayout's legend estimate is no longer dead OR wrong.
  179  #        It now delegates to @emlMeasureLegendPanel instead of carrying a
  180  #        single-column stack measured at bodySize (the legend is drawn at
  181  #        annotSize at all seven call sites; measuring at the wrong size
  182  #        inflates every width — 0.4967" vs 3.6229" for "Group label",
  183  #        measured 8 Aug 2026 on Praat 6.6.30). New alongside the two
  184  #        existing globals: emlLayout_legendCols / Rows / Fits.
  185  #
  186  #        One latent defect fixed in passing: the alpha-sprite legend
  187  #        background called `Insert picture from file:`, which leaves the
  188  #        viewport set to the image's bounding box, and the old code restored
  189  #        the viewport only at the END of the procedure — after every swatch
  190  #        and label had been drawn through it. Restored immediately now. Not
  191  #        observable on Linux, where @emlInitAlphaSprites is gated off.
  192  #
  193  # v3.27: COMMENTS ONLY — no executable line changed. Five statements the code
  194  #        or the checks contradict, corrected.
  195  #        (1) Two places said "the annotation block does not wrap either" —
  196  #            the v3.26 entry below, and NOT HANDLED HERE in @emlDrawLegend's
  197  #            header. D124 closed the same day: @emlDrawAnnotationBlock now
  198  #            wraps every entry to emlAnnotBlockWidthShare (default 0.55) of
  199  #            the frame. The legend is now the ONLY element that does not, and
  200  #            both places say so instead.
  201  #        (2) The overhang itself is unchanged and still not fixed. Its
  202  #            documentation is now anchored on `if .colsMax < 1` rather than
  203  #            on a line number.
  204  #        (3) @emlSetColorPalette's B/W branch reported the measured minimum
  205  #            greyscale separation as 0.120, disagreeing with the v3.25 entry
  206  #            below (0.1176) and with the check that produces it. Run 8 Aug
  207  #            2026: validate/v29_figure_disclosure.R reports 0.1176. Two
  208  #            "See validate/v29." pointers were also completed to the real
  209  #            filename, and harness/stress_cases/legend_cap to legend_cap.praat.
  210  #        (4) The v3.26 entry, and the zero-category branch of
  211  #            @emlDrawCategoricalXAxis, both quoted a line of
  212  #            eml-graphs-form.praat as the home of `prev_gvAnnotStyle = 1`.
  213  #            That had drifted again within the day — it is ~250 lines further
  214  #            down now. Neither quotes a line number any more; the argument
  215  #            for the anchor was being made by a citation that could not hold.
  216  #        (5) @emlMeasureGraphLayout's note that nothing consumes
  217  #            emlLayout_legendWidthInches / HeightInches is TRUE, re-checked
  218  #            8 Aug 2026, and now carries the grep that shows it.
  219  #
  220  # v3.26: THE LEGEND IS LAID OUT TO FIT THE FRAME (D123), and one stale
  221  #        cross-reference corrected.
  222  #        (1) LEGEND. @emlDrawLegend stacked one row per entry and measured
  223  #            that stack against nothing. The palette holds 24 sub-group
  224  #            styles, so 24 entries is a figure the plugin is built to draw:
  225  #            at the annotation font on a 6 x 4 figure a row is 0.162", the
  226  #            panel is 3.11" tall, and 24 rows want 3.94". Rendered before the
  227  #            fix, the box overhung the frame by 304 px at 300 dpi and its
  228  #            last entries ran clean off the bottom of the PNG. The fix is a
  229  #            LAYOUT, not a cap: the frame is measured (rowsMax x colsMax =
  230  #            capacity) and the entries are poured down the fewest columns
  231  #            that fit the height, so 24 entries become two columns of 12 on
  232  #            a 6 x 4 figure and three of 8 on a 10 x 3 one. Hue order runs
  233  #            top-to-bottom down each column. A legend that fits in one column
  234  #            gets the identical single-column geometry v3.25 drew, to the
  235  #            last decimal — verified by rendering: a 3-entry legend's pixels
  236  #            are unchanged. Only when capacity is still exceeded (a very
  237  #            short frame, or labels wide enough that one column is all that
  238  #            fits) is anything dropped, and then the last cell reads
  239  #            "+N more" ON THE FIGURE and a NOTE naming both counts goes to
  240  #            the Info window. Nothing is dropped in silence. New reports for
  241  #            callers and fixtures: .nCols, .rowsPerCol, .shown, .hidden,
  242  #            .capacity. NOT fixed here: a single label wider than the frame
  243  #            still overhangs to the right. That is the legend's copy of the
  244  #            D124 defect — and D124 itself was closed the same day, so the
  245  #            annotation block now wraps to a share of the frame and the
  246  #            legend is the only element left that does not. See NOT HANDLED
  247  #            HERE in @emlDrawLegend's header.
  248  #        (2) The zero-category branch of @emlDrawCategoricalXAxis cited
  249  #            eml-graphs-form.praat by LINE NUMBER for the refusal that keeps
  250  #            an empty table out of it. The line it named held
  251  #            `prev_gvAnnotStyle = 1`, a grouped-violin persistence variable —
  252  #            the citation was ~750 lines stale (7 Aug contradiction sweep,
  253  #            C3), and the line number quoted in this entry had itself drifted
  254  #            again within the day, which is the whole argument. It now names
  255  #            the guard by its string, `exitScript: "Table has no
  256  #            rows."`, which cannot drift. Checking it also showed the same
  257  #            comment's other claim to be false: an all-blank category column
  258  #            does NOT reach that branch, because @emlCountGroups counts the
  259  #            empty string as a group (measured: nGroups = 1 on a 3-row table
  260  #            of blanks). Both statements are corrected in place.
  261  #
  262  # v3.25: POINT MARKERS, and a greyscale ramp that uses the page.
  263  #        (1) MARKERS. The v3.24 fill pattern needs an AREA. Scatter, line
  264  #            chart, spaghetti and time series draw dots and lines, so all
  265  #            four still cycled eight hues in silence above eight groups --
  266  #            D127 unfixed, in four more chart types. New @emlDrawMarker draws
  267  #            circle / square / triangle from NATIVE primitives (Paint circle,
  268  #            Paint rectangle, and a stack of horizontal slices for the
  269  #            triangle -- the same construction @emlDrawViolin uses for its
  270  #            body). New emlSetColorPalette.marker[] gives 8 hues x 3 shapes =
  271  #            24, the same arithmetic and the same cap as the fill patterns.
  272  #            @emlDrawLegend grew a marker key (legendMarkered,
  273  #            legendMarker[], legendMarkerLine) so the key shows the SHAPE and
  274  #            not merely the hue. Not sprites: plugin/sprites/ is real and has
  275  #            204 PNGs, but every one of them is a dot or a rectangle indexed
  276  #            by hue, and @emlInitAlphaSprites is disabled on anything that is
  277  #            not macOS or Windows because Praat has no cairo image branch.
  278  #            @emlDrawScatterPlot therefore drops the sprite path above eight
  279  #            groups, or on those two platforms the shape would never reach
  280  #            the page.
  281  #        (2) GREYSCALE. The v3.24 ramp ran 0.90 to 0.25, a span of 0.65, and
  282  #            what held it there was not the eye: the stroke was derived as
  283  #            fill - 0.30 and clamped at zero, so a fill below 0.30 collapsed
  284  #            the stroke ramp -- and at 0.25 it already had, leaving the two
  285  #            darkest strokes 0.043 and 0.000 apart and a greyscale LINE chart
  286  #            with two indistinguishable series at eight groups. The fill and
  287  #            stroke ramps are now independent: fills 0.94 to 0.10 (a span of
  288  #            0.84) and strokes 0.63 to 0.00. New @emlMarkInk pays the cost --
  289  #            a mark's outline, median, whiskers and pattern flip to white on
  290  #            a dark fill and black on a light one, per channel so that
  291  #            Okabe-Ito yellow (whose contrast is all in the blue channel) is
  292  #            not misjudged. New @emlParseRGB underneath it. Measured
  293  #            minimum greyscale separation on the rendered pixels: 0.1176,
  294  #            was 0.090.
  295  #
  296  # v3.24: FILL PATTERNS — the palette has eight colours, not ten, and now has
  297  #        a second dimension. @emlSetColorPalette declared ten fill/line pairs
  298  #        whose slots 9 and 10 were LITERAL DUPLICATES of 1 and 2, so a nine-
  299  #        or ten-sub-group figure drew two pairs of sub-groups in
  300  #        indistinguishable colours, on the figure and in the legend, with no
  301  #        disclosure (D127). Fixed by declaring the eight hues once and adding
  302  #        a fill-pattern axis: 8 hues x 3 patterns (solid, diagonal hatch,
  303  #        dots) = 24 distinguishable styles, hue cycling first so adjacent
  304  #        sub-groups differ in the stronger cue. @emlDrawViolin and
  305  #        @emlDrawBox take a .pattern argument and clip the pattern to the
  306  #        mark using the scanline structure they already build; @emlDrawLegend
  307  #        draws the swatch as the mark rather than as a block of colour, so
  308  #        the key cannot say "solid" where the figure says "hatched". The B/W
  309  #        branch's ten fills, which sat between 0.82 and 0.96, are replaced by
  310  #        the eight-step 0.90-0.25 ramp @emlOptimizePaletteContrast was
  311  #        already computing, so greyscale reaches the same 24.
  312  #        Also fixed here: @emlDrawLegend measured and drew itself at its own
  313  #        font size while the viewport had been selected at the body size, so
  314  #        the legend was rendered into a rectangle slightly smaller than, and
  315  #        offset from, the .boxLeft/.boxTop it reported to its caller. It
  316  #        looked right because the whole legend moved together, but the box
  317  #        the caller kept the disclosure block clear of was not the box on the
  318  #        page. The viewport is now re-selected at the legend's font size.
  319  #
  320  # v3.23: @emlMeasureBarData: zero is not "no data". emlBarData_mean[g] is now
  321  #        UNDEFINED when the group has no usable observation, and
  322  #        emlBarData_error[g] UNDEFINED when there is no error to compute,
  323  #        instead of both being seeded to 0. v3.22 seeded them at 0 to keep
  324  #        undefined out of the drawing commands; the side effect was that
  325  #        @emlDrawBarChart's "<> undefined" guards for the bar and the whisker
  326  #        could never fire, so a group with nothing in it drew as a bar of
  327  #        height zero — indistinguishable from a measured zero — and an
  328  #        undefined error bar vanished in silence. Both are now suppressed by
  329  #        the existing guards and disclosed. The visible-range fold at the end
  330  #        of the procedure substitutes 0 for an undefined error so the axis
  331  #        range is unaffected. See the invariant block on @emlMeasureBarData.
  332  #
  333  # v3.22: Undefined/validation hardening (audit items 1-3).
  334  #        (1) @emlCheckNumericColumn no longer samples the first 5 rows and
  335  #            no longer passes a column on a single parseable cell. It scans
  336  #            the whole column (capped at .maxScanRows), requires EVERY
  337  #            non-empty cell to be a strict numeric literal, treats
  338  #            empty/whitespace cells as missing rather than failures, and
  339  #            reports .nNumeric/.nMissing/.nCoerced/.nNonNumeric plus the
  340  #            first offending row and its literal text. Cells that only pass
  341  #            via number() coercion ("5,5", "30%", "1/2", "0x10", "2 3") are
  342  #            classified as coerced and REJECTED — a silent wrong number is
  343  #            worse than a rejected column. Parameter list and .isNumeric
  344  #            output name/meaning are unchanged.
  345  #        (2) @emlMeasureBarData no longer crashes on undefined values.
  346  #            Undefined observations and undefined custom-error values are
  347  #            skipped and counted; means, SE/SD and the visible min/max fold
  348  #            are guarded with explicit "<> undefined" tests; single-
  349  #            observation groups are handled explicitly (no undefined error
  350  #            bar). emlBarData_mean[] and emlBarData_error[] were made
  351  #            guaranteed-defined on return (SUPERSEDED by v3.23, which
  352  #            restores undefined as the "no measurement" sentinel and puts the
  353  #            burden of guarding back on the callers, where it already was).
  354  #            New disclosure globals: emlBarData_valid[],
  355  #            emlBarData_errorDefined[], emlBarData_skipped[],
  356  #            emlBarData_errSkipped[], emlBarData_nSkipped,
  357  #            emlBarData_nErrSkipped, emlBarData_nInvalidGroups,
  358  #            emlBarData_nSingleObs, emlBarData_nUnmatchedRows.
  359  #        (3) Swept the file for the same two patterns. Fixed:
  360  #            @emlComputeAxisRange, @emlComputeNiceStep,
  361  #            @emlSetAlphaDotGeometry (relational "guards" that are FALSE for
  362  #            undefined and therefore let undefined through to Axes:/
  363  #            Paint circle:), and @emlDrawViolin, @emlDrawBox,
  364  #            @emlDrawJitteredPoints (undefined observations reaching
  365  #            Draw line:/Paint rectangle:, or silently suppressing the whole
  366  #            glyph). @emlCheckPlausibility was already correct.
  367  # v3.21: Graph correctness fixes. (1) Box/violin quartiles now use the
  368  #        shared R-7 interpolated @emlQuartiles instead of nearest-rank
  369  #        floor(n*p), which biased the median low and collapsed it onto the
  370  #        minimum at small n (figure now agrees with the describe table).
  371  #        (2) Bar auto-range tracks a data minimum (emlBarData_visibleMin) so
  372  #        all-negative means are no longer clipped at 0. (3) B/W palette now
  373  #        recomputes sprite$ greys so alpha dots/overlap match the fill/line.
  374  # v3.20: Group sort unification — @emlExtractUniqueValues and
  375  #         @emlMeasureBarData now call @emlCountGroups instead of inline
  376  #         discovery. All group ordering flows through single source.
  377  #
  378  # v3.18: Stereo channel handling — @emlCheckChannels no longer silently
  379  #         converts to mono. Refactored to present user dialog via new
  380  #         @emlHandleStereo. New @emlApplyChannelChoice for batch scripts
  381  #         (applies pre-selected channel handling without dialog).
  382  #         Three-procedure architecture: @emlApplyChannelChoice (mechanical
  383  #         core), @emlHandleStereo (single-file UI wrapper),
  384  #         @emlCheckChannels (backward-compat thin wrapper).
  385  # v3.17: New @emlExpandDrawnExtent procedure — single source of truth
  386  #         for extent tracker bounding box expansion. Replaces inline
  387  #         pattern in @emlSetAdaptiveTheme; also called by
  388  #         @emlDrawMatrixPanel (eml-annotation-procedures.praat) to
  389  #         register the matrix panel viewport with the extent tracker.
  390  # v3.16: New @emlMeasureBarData procedure — extracts and aggregates bar
  391  #         chart data (unique groups, per-group means, SE/SD/custom errors,
  392  #         visible max) into emlBarData_* globals. Called once from
  393  #         pre-dispatch; results read by both headroom computation and
  394  #         @emlDrawBarChart. Eliminates DRY violation between pre-dispatch
  395  #         aggregation and draw procedure aggregation.
  396  # v3.15: Independent per-axis control — emlShowTicks/emlShowAxisValues
  397  #         split to emlShowTicksX/Y and emlShowAxisValuesX/Y. New
  398  #         emlShowAxisNameX/Y globals gate axis label drawing. New
  399  #         @emlExpandAxisControls procedure: 3 dropdown indices → 6
  400  #         per-axis booleans. All 3 aligned mark procedures,
  401  #         @emlDrawCategoricalXAxis, @emlDrawAxes, and
  402  #         @emlDrawAxesSelective updated. Categorical group names
  403  #         always visible regardless of X tick/value settings.
  404  #         Sanitization moved to source: @emlCapitalizeLabel now calls
  405  #         @emlSanitizeLabel (auto-generated labels). @emlDrawAxes and
  406  #         @emlDrawAxesSelective no longer sanitize axis labels — user-typed
  407  #         labels with Praat formatting codes (%italic, #bold, ^super,
  408  #         _sub) pass through raw.
  409  #         New .tickColor$ ("{0.35, 0.35, 0.35}") in @emlSetAdaptiveTheme
  410  #         — tick value numbers draw slightly muted vs axis name labels
  411  #         (textColor$). Applied in all 3 aligned mark procedures.
  412  # v3.14: New @emlDrawTitle procedure — centralizes title/subtitle drawing
  413  #         via Text special with 1:1 inch coordinate mapping. Title/subtitle
  414  #         anchored upward from inner box top, horizontally centered on inner
  415  #         box. Replaces 7 inline title blocks (1 in @emlDrawAxes, 6 in
  416  #         categorical procs via eml-draw-procedures). Clipping guard for
  417  #         small viewports.
  418  #         @emlSetAdaptiveTheme: symmetric margins (.marginRight = .marginLeft).
  419  #         New .bodyInch, .titleInch outputs (typography in inches). New
  420  #         .boxInsetInches output — unified physical inset for legend,
  421  #         annotation block, and comparison matrix boxes.
  422  #         @emlDrawLegend insetX/Y now use boxInsetInches for equal padding.
  423  # v3.13: @emlDrawLegend — colored line samples replaced with filled
  424  #         square swatches; legend text labels now use axis text color
  425  #         (textColor$) instead of group color. @emlDrawAxes title
  426  #         centering — asserts full outer viewport before title/subtitle
  427  #         so text centers on figure, not inner box. Subtitle support
  428  #         (global emlSubtitle$, drawn at bodySize in grey via Text top:
  429  #         "no"). Content-driven marginTop — computed from title/subtitle
  430  #         typography instead of viewport height cap.
  431  # v3.12: New @emlDrawAlignedMarksBottom procedure — mirrors Left/Right
  432  #         for bottom x-axis ticks. @emlDrawAxes and @emlDrawAxesSelective
  433  #         refactored to call aligned mark procedures (Left/Bottom) instead
  434  #         of inline loops — DRY, consistent guard logic. Show axis values
  435  #         toggle (emlShowAxisValues) added: all 3 aligned mark procedures
  436  #         now independently control writeNumber (emlShowAxisValues) and
  437  #         drawTick (emlShowTicks). @emlDrawAxesSelective per-axis params
  438  #         (.showXTicks/.showYTicks) remain as hard overrides wrapping the
  439  #         procedure calls.
  440  # v3.11: @emlSetAdaptiveTheme — bare Helvetica replaced with
  441  #         'emlFont$' global variable (set by main script font dropdown).
  442  #         Theme exposes .font$ output for documentation.
  443  # v3.10: @emlDrawLegend font restore moved before Select inner
  444  #         viewport (was after — caused margin mismatch). Show ticks
  445  #         toggle (emlShowTicks) guards added to @emlDrawAxes,
  446  #         @emlDrawAxesSelective, @emlDrawAlignedMarksLeft/Right.
  447  # v3.9: @emlDrawInnerBoxIf — wrapper for Draw inner box with boolean toggle
  448  #        and font size assertion (resolves BUG-007/008 tick displacement).
  449  #        @emlDrawVerticalGridlines — vertical-only gridlines for continuous
  450  #        axes. @emlDrawLegend now restores Font size: bodySize at end
  451  #        (font state management layer 1).
  452  #
  453  # v3.7: New @emlFitCategoricalLabels procedure — space-aware x-axis labels
  454  #        with automatic truncation via Text width (world coordinates) when
  455  #        labels exceed available slot width. Used by all categorical draw procs.
```
