## plugin/graphs/eml-graphs-form.praat @ b4ca5e6

Lines 7-189 of plugin/graphs/eml-graphs-form.praat at b4ca5e6.

```
    7  # v2.9: The post-draw dialog is three fixed buttons -- Done | Save | Redraw --
    8  #       and Save opens @emlSavePanel. The row used to be four or three
    9  #       depending on whether there were results to export, with the caller
   10  #       renumbering afterwards; a conditional button count means every
   11  #       keyboard walk has to know which variant is up, and harness/gui_e2e
   12  #       carried a retry for exactly that. The Exp CSV branch is gone: the
   13  #       panel offers whichever outputs exist.
   14  # v2.8: The post-draw "Exp CSV" button writes through @emlExportResultFiles,
   15  #       the shared migration fork, instead of calling @emlExportStatsCSV
   16  #       directly. It was the only export in the plugin that could not reach
   17  #       the fork, so it wrote the legacy single long-format file for analyses
   18  #       the stats menu exported in broom's three-file shape. The explicit
   19  #       @emlGenerateUniquePath call goes with it: @emlReportToFile has always
   20  #       uniqued the legacy file itself, and the declared arm does not unique,
   21  #       which is the behaviour @emlWrapperExportCSV has always had.
   22  # v2.7: A WRAPPER'S ANNOTATE PRESET NOW SURVIVES A BEGINNER DRAW. Beginner
   23  #       mode draws only what its own dialog offers, so the beginner commit
   24  #       setting annotate = 0 is correct and stays. What was wrong is what
   25  #       happened next: the preset is consumed once BEFORE the outer repeat,
   26  #       so Redraw does not re-apply it, and prev_adv_<type>_annotate is
   27  #       written in exactly one place -- the toggle to beginner -- which a
   28  #       preset never passes through. A user who asked a wrapper to annotate,
   29  #       drew in beginner mode, pressed Redraw and switched to Advanced found
   30  #       the box unticked, with nothing to say it had ever been set. All six
   31  #       annotate-capable restore branches now fall back to the live preset
   32  #       when there is no saved advanced state, and carry the preset's test
   33  #       type into the dialog index with it. Same shape as v1.6's Item 22,
   34  #       which preserved annotTestType$ and annotStyle$ across a beginner Draw
   35  #       and left `annotate` itself still being zeroed.
   36  # v2.6: A LEGEND INSIDE THE PLOT NOW GETS Y-AXIS ROOM MADE FOR IT.
   37  #       eml-draw-procedures.praat has stated since 5 August that "any extra
   38  #       room a figure needs is a property of what is drawn on it ... and is
   39  #       supplied by @emlComputeAnnotationHeadroom at the annotation stage".
   40  #       This file honoured that for significance brackets and for nothing
   41  #       else. @emlComputeAnnotationHeadroom had ONE call site here, gated on
   42  #
   43  #           (graph_type = 6 or 7 or 9) and annotate = 1 and annotBracketN > 0
   44  #
   45  #       and the six types that draw a legend are 5, 8, 10, 11, 12 and 13 —
   46  #       so a figure with a legend and no brackets never computed headroom at
   47  #       all and the legend landed on the data. Measured on the rendered PNG,
   48  #       9 Aug 2026, a five-group line chart at 6 x 4: **13145 data pixels
   49  #       covered -> 0**. Same measurement on the other five legend types:
   50  #       scatter 15096 -> 0, grouped violin 23702 -> 0, grouped box 15430 ->
   51  #       0, histogram 320 -> 0, 5 x 5 square 17218 -> 0.
   52  #
   53  #       * TWO PASSES. The figure is drawn, the axis it resolved and the
   54  #         corner @emlPlaceElements sent the legend to are read back from the
   55  #         draw procedure, and if the legend needs room the figure is erased
   56  #         and drawn again on the widened axis. Both facts are decided inside
   57  #         the draw procedure and neither exists before it runs. The
   58  #         alternative — this file re-deriving every legend-bearing type's
   59  #         auto-range with its own copy of that type's arithmetic — would
   60  #         change the axis of every figure whether or not it had a legend the
   61  #         first time a copy drifted by one rounding step.
   62  #       * NEW: @emlGraphsDispatchDraw (the DISPATCH block, lifted out
   63  #         verbatim so it can be called twice) and @emlGraphsDrawWithLegendRoom
   64  #         (the loop), both at file scope so a probe can drive the real code
   65  #         rather than a transcription of it.
   66  #       * NEW: @emlLegendHeadroomAfterDraw — the decision. Only placement 1
   67  #         (Inside plot) earns an axis change: 2 and 3 grow the saved image
   68  #         around an unchanged plot, 4 writes a second file, 5 draws nothing,
   69  #         and none of them takes a square inch of the data area.
   70  #       * NOTHING THAT CANNOT BE AFFORDED IS SILENT. A legend band capped at
   71  #         emlLegendHeadroomShare of the panel, a bottom-corner legend on a
   72  #         histogram (whose frequency axis has a hard zero floor), a
   73  #         user-typed axis that had to be widened, and the discarded first
   74  #         pass itself are each named in the Info window.
   75  #       * VERIFIED UNCHANGED, byte-identical PNGs: every graph type that
   76  #         draws no legend (bar, violin, box), a one-group line chart, and
   77  #         legend placements 2 and 5.
   78  # v2.5: D136 — LEGEND PLACEMENT. The user's width and height describe the
   79  #       PLOT, and until now a legend could only be drawn inside it, so the
   80  #       only way to give a legend room was to take room from the data and
   81  #       "make my figure square" could not be satisfied. New shared setting,
   82  #       one canonical encoding for every graph type:
   83  #
   84  #           config_legendPlacement
   85  #             1 Inside plot (DEFAULT — today's behaviour, auto-corner)
   86  #             2 Right of plot      3 Below plot
   87  #             4 Separate figure    5 None
   88  #
   89  #       THE PLOT IS THE SAME SIZE IN ALL FIVE. Placements 2 and 3 grow the
   90  #       SAVED IMAGE around an unchanged plot rectangle; placement 4 writes
   91  #       the legend as a second file. figure_width and figure_height are not
   92  #       adjusted anywhere for a legend, which is the whole point. The
   93  #       geometry lives in @emlDrawLegend / @emlDrawLegendPanel in
   94  #       eml-graph-procedures.praat v3.28; this file owns the encoding, the
   95  #       registry, the dialog and the second file.
   96  #
   97  #       * REGISTRY. New legendPlacementStyle[1..nGraphTypes], 5 = offers the
   98  #         menu / 0 = this type has no legend, beside gridModeStyle[] and in
   99  #         the same shape for the same reason (see the C1 block there: two
  100  #         encodings on one persisted key once left a dropdown blank and
  101  #         unusable, on disk, surviving a restart). Six types draw a legend —
  102  #         5, 8, 10, 11, 12, 13, which is exactly the set whose draw
  103  #         procedures call @emlDrawLegend. A missing or out-of-range entry
  104  #         fails at INCLUDE time from a file-scope loop, and
  105  #         @emlLegendPlacementStyle refuses an unregistered type by name at
  106  #         runtime. Adding a graph type without a legend entry cannot ship.
  107  #       * ENCODING. @emlLegendPlacementToMenu / @emlLegendPlacementFromMenu /
  108  #         @emlSeedLegendPlacement / @emlCommitLegendPlacement, mirroring the
  109  #         gridline set. There is only ONE encoding today, so the translations
  110  #         are identities; they exist so that a second one, if it is ever
  111  #         needed, has one place to go instead of fourteen. The clamp is at
  112  #         @emlLoadConfig, where the file is read, so an out-of-range value in
  113  #         a hand-edited config becomes the default rather than a blank
  114  #         dropdown and a form that refuses to close.
  115  #       * DIALOG. "Legend placement" optionmenu in the Advanced block of the
  116  #         six legend-capable pages only, seeded and committed through the two
  117  #         single-token procedures.
  118  #       * SECOND FILE. Placement 4's legend is drawn on a parked patch of the
  119  #         picture and deliberately not reported to @emlExpandDrawnExtent, so
  120  #         the figure still saves at its own extent; the Save branch then
  121  #         selects the parked rectangle and writes <name>_legend.png beside
  122  #         <name>.png — same folder, same base name, same DPI, same
  123  #         non-destructive @emlGenerateUniquePath. No new path convention.
  124  #         It inherits the figure's annotSize rather than recomputing a size
  125  #         from the legend canvas, so the two files' text matches when they
  126  #         are placed side by side in a manuscript.
  127  # v2.4: D3b — the graph type registry drops from 14 entries to 13. Type 13,
  128  #       "Time Series (with CI)", lost its form page when CI became a toggle on
  129  #       type 5 (Line Chart); the registry row and its dispatch branches stayed
  130  #       behind, unreachable (typeToMenu[13] was 0) and reading three column
  131  #       variables — ciTimeCol$ / ciValueCol$ / ciGroupCol$ — that nothing ever
  132  #       wrote. Spaghetti Plot moves 14 -> 13; nGraphTypes and all six parallel
  133  #       registry arrays, menuToType[16] and typeToMenu shrink with it.
  134  #       @emlDrawTimeSeriesCI is untouched and still dispatched from type 5.
  135  #       CALLERS: any script setting emlGraphsPresetType = 14 for a spaghetti
  136  #       plot must now set 13.
  137  # v2.3: D18/D65/D32/D60/D108.
  138  #       * D18 + D65 (filename half) — the Draw path's Export Results dialog
  139  #         proposed selected$ ("Table") + "_results" on originalSourceId, which
  140  #         named the transient `pairedLong` reshape on the paired workflow and
  141  #         carried no analysis identity anywhere. New @emlGraphsCSVDefaultName /
  142  #         @emlGraphsCSVRowAnalysis read the source table and the analysis off
  143  #         the CSV buffer that is about to be written, giving the same
  144  #         <table>_<analysis-slug> shape as @emlWrapperExportCSV. D65's other
  145  #         half (Draw exporting a different test family than the analysis that
  146  #         launched it) is a bridge design decision and is untouched.
  147  #       * D32 (remaining half) — declared emlGraphsPresetSubgroupCol$ so a
  148  #         wrapper can name its actual second factor, and consumed it in the
  149  #         Grouped Violin and Grouped Box preset branches ahead of the role
  150  #         guesser. A preset that collides with Category or Value is demoted and
  151  #         the guesser runs as before.
  152  #       * D60 — new @emlGraphsIsPercentageColumn, and the two
  153  #         @emlComputeAxisRange calls here now pass it instead of a literal 0,
  154  #         which is the first time that procedure's .isPercentage branch has
  155  #         ever executed. Detection needs BOTH a percentage-looking name and
  156  #         data inside 0-100.
  157  #       * D108 — the correction preset's string and its dialog index are now
  158  #         derived from one validated index via @emlAdjustMethodName, so they
  159  #         cannot disagree, and an unrecognised preset is reported where it was
  160  #         set. Consuming it on the parametric path is in
  161  #         eml-annotation-procedures.praat, not here.
  162  # v2.2: Item 8 — two further preset-plumbing defects. (a) The spaghetti-plot
  163  #       column-mapping dialog never consumed emlGraphsPresetGroupCol$; it
  164  #       relied on a name heuristic that runs only on the first spaghetti draw
  165  #       of a session, so a later Draw from a stats wrapper silently dropped
  166  #       the requested grouping. Added the spPresetHasGroup / spPresetGroupIdx
  167  #       sentinel, mirroring scatter and histogram. (b) emlGraphsPresetXCol$
  168  #       and emlGraphsPresetYCol$ were cleared only inside the scatter page,
  169  #       so they leaked into the next workflow call whenever the user chose a
  170  #       non-scatter graph type; both are now cleared at workflow end.
  171  # v2.1: Item 8 — histogram column-mapping dialog now honours an incoming
  172  #       group-column preset. The preset seeded histGroupIdx but left
  173  #       "Use group column" unchecked, so histGroupCol$ committed as "" and
  174  #       the grouped-histogram annotation route at the dispatch was silently
  175  #       skipped even though the caller had asked to annotate. Added the
  176  #       histPresetHasGroup sentinel, mirroring scatterPresetHasGroup.
  177  # v2.0: Item 6 — multiple-comparison adjustment is now user-facing instead
  178  #       of a hardcoded "holm". Added an "Adjustment method" optionmenu
  179  #       (Bonferroni / Holm / Benjamini-Hochberg, default Holm) to all six
  180  #       annotate-capable column-mapping dialogs, backed by the shared
  181  #       prev_annotAdjustIdx persistence variable and the @emlAdjustMethodName
  182  #       index-to-string helper. Added the emlGraphsPresetCorrection$ preset
  183  #       global so stats wrappers can carry their own adjustment choice in.
  184  # v1.9: Bar-chart negative-mean support in the annotated (bracket) path —
  185  #       auto-range now floors on emlBarData_visibleMin so all-/mixed-negative
  186  #       means are not clipped at 0 (positive data unchanged via the axis
  187  #       procedure's non-negative guard). Companion to eml-graph-procedures
  188  #       v3.21 / eml-draw-procedures v1.18.
  189  # v1.8: Post-draw dialog "Back" renamed to "Done".
```

```
  192  # v1.7: emlShowExplanations = 1 set in @emlGraphsWorkflow entry point
  193  #        (explanations on wizard + graphs paths, not wrapper output).
  194  #        Added emlGraphsPresetRegressionLine and emlGraphsPresetCorrType$
  195  #        preset globals — consumed after per-call reset, survive workflow.
  196  # v1.6: Item 22 fix — beginner Draw path no longer resets
  197  #        annotTestType$/annotStyle$ to defaults. These fields are
  198  #        only meaningful when annotate=1 (advanced mode). Resetting
  199  #        them in beginner mode destroyed preset test type on Redraw.
  200  #        Fixed across all 5 form types (bar, violin, box, grouped
  201  #        violin, grouped box).
  202  # v1.5: Scatter group column preset (item 21). Added
  203  #        scatterPresetHasGroup sentinel. Scatter form now consumes
  204  #        emlGraphsPresetGroupCol$ alongside X/Y presets, auto-checks
  205  #        "Use group column" checkbox when a group preset is provided.
  206  #
  207  # v1.4: Scatter preset globals (emlGraphsPresetXCol$, emlGraphsPresetYCol$)
  208  #        added for stats wrapper wiring. Consumed in scatter form section
  209  #        to auto-select X/Y columns from eml-correlate.praat presets.
  210  #
  211  # v1.3: Fix — group_order captures added to all 9 Draw branches (were
  212  #        only in toggle branches). Moved emlGroupSortAlphabetical set
  213  #        before annotation bridge (was after bridge, causing matrix to
  214  #        lag one draw behind graph ordering). Value column moved to
  215  #        first position in Grouped Violin, Grouped Box, and Spaghetti
  216  #        forms. Spaghetti: parenthetical field descriptions, boolean
  217  #        "Use group column" (replaces "(none)" option), spaghetti-
  218  #        specific prev_spGroupSort defaulting to table order. Legend
  219  #        labels sanitized in TimeSeries, TimeSeriesCI, Spaghetti.
  220  #
  221  # v1.2: Fix — group_order captures were in toggle branch (clicked=3)
  222  #        only; Draw branch (else) never committed the user's selection.
  223  #        Added prev_groupSort + config_groupSort captures to all 9
  224  #        Draw branches. Group sort now takes effect and persists on
  225  #        redraw.
  226  #
  227  # v1.1: Bug #14 — Save dialog replaced chooseWriteFile$ with folder +
  228  #        auto-generated filename (Appendix F §S9). Bug #15 — post-save
  229  #        dead end removed; save returns to main post-draw dialog so
  230  #        CSV export remains available after saving. Sub-dialog buttons
  231  #        use "Cancel" (returns to loop). PNG and CSV folder persistence
  232  #        tracked separately (config_lastPNGFolder$, config_lastCSVFolder$).
  233  #        Default folder is Desktop, not plugin directory. Backward-
  234  #        compatible config load (old lastOutputFolder key populates both).
  235  #        Group sort order dropdown on all 9 group-having graph types
  236  #        (Table order / Alphabetical). Config-persisted via groupSort
  237  #        key. Controls @emlCountGroups via emlGroupSortAlphabetical.
```

```
 3236      # Two things used to make that only accidentally true:
 3237      #
 3238      #   * the string and the dialog index were derived separately, so an
 3239      #     unrecognised preset set annotCorrectionMethod$ to the unrecognised
 3240      #     string but prev_annotAdjustIdx to Holm. In Advanced mode the form
 3241      #     then committed Holm over it silently; in Beginner mode the bad string
 3242      #     survived to the bridge, which warned and fell back. Same preset, two
 3243      #     behaviours, neither announced at the point the caller made the
 3244      #     mistake. Both now come from one validated index via
 3245      #     @emlAdjustMethodName, which is the same lookup the six column-mapping
 3246      #     pages commit through — they cannot disagree any more.
 3247      #   * an unrecognised value was never reported to the caller. It is now,
 3248      #     here, where the preset was set, rather than later from inside the
 3249      #     annotation layer.
```

```
   12  # v1.3: The two APPENDIX_D §7 (hard) rules this module had never implemented.
   13  #       GUARD BOTH ENDS OF EVERY BOUNDED RANGE — the filtered-autocorrelation
   14  #       range, the raw-cross-correlation range and the 60-330 Hz cepstral peak
   15  #       search now each warn when a measured F0 comes within 10% of either
   16  #       limit, which is the difference between a censored number and a
   17  #       measured one and is invisible in the value itself. CHECK THE USER'S
   18  #       STATED RANGE AGAINST THE MEASUREMENT — highest expected F0 derives
   19  #       both pitch ranges, so a measurement above it is reported with the two
   20  #       parameters it set. Info-window output and the warning count only: no
   21  #       Table cell, no column and no exported value changes, and
   22  #       harness/batch's seven result CSVs are byte-identical across the edit.
   23  #       This is the module's first version to carry a registered menu entry
   24  #       (plugin/setup.praat v1.7) and a GUI drive of its real dialog
   25  #       (harness/batchgui). validate/v72_batch_registration.R.
```

```
   10  # v1.1: NEW-G10-4 — file mode checked two things and reported on all of them.
   11  #        A CSV whose rows do not all carry the header's number of fields came
   12  #        back as "No import problems found", and Praat's own reader then
   13  #        refused the same file outright with "Row 3 incomplete" — the tool
   14  #        whose whole job is to say a file will not import said it would. Row
   15  #        lengths are now scanned (@emlCheckFileRowLengths), and the clean
   16  #        verdict enumerates the checks it is the verdict OF rather than
   17  #        speaking for the file as a whole.
   18  #        NEW-G12-4 — choosing Table mode with no Table selected refused
   19  #        through a raw exitScript, which Praat dresses in "Script exited.
   20  #        Script ... not completed. Command ... not executed." That refusal
   21  #        now comes through @emlErrorDialog like every other one, and Back
   22  #        returns to the mode choice instead of ending the session.
```

## plugin/scripts/eml-check-normality.praat @ b4ca5e6

Lines 9-30 of plugin/scripts/eml-check-normality.praat at b4ca5e6.

```
    9  # v2.2: D137 — per-group mode no longer carries its own normality rule. It
   10  #        had hard-coded thresholds of 1 and 3 against the shared constants
   11  #        of 2 and 7, and the pre-5-August `skKurtFail or swFail` gate, while
   12  #        the SAME script's overall mode called @emlRunNormalityAnalysis,
   13  #        which had neither. One wrapper, two answers for the same data
   14  #        depending on whether a grouping column was picked: a group with
   15  #        |skew| >= 1 that Shapiro-Wilk did not reject came out nonparametric
   16  #        grouped and parametric ungrouped. Both modes now reach the one
   17  #        shared rule, @emlNormalityRecommendation (stats/eml-analysis.praat).
   18  #        This CHANGES per-group verdicts, which is the point.
   19  # v2.1: Draw button on the completion dialog — a normal Q-Q plot for ONE
   20  #        column at a time, behind an explicit column picker. The picker is
   21  #        not optional: this checker tests every numeric column in one run,
   22  #        so a figure drawn from an assumed column would be a silently wrong
   23  #        figure. In grouped mode the picker asks for the group as well,
   24  #        because in that mode a column is not what was tested — a column
   25  #        within a group is.
   26  # v2.0: Full convergence — @emlWrapperInit for Table check,
   27  #        @emlRunNormalityAnalysis orchestrator for overall mode (fixes
   28  #        double-report bug and wrong-type 4th parameter in v1.0).
   29  #        repeat/until replaces goto/label.
   30  # v1.0: Initial release.
```

## plugin/scripts/eml-compare-paired.praat @ b4ca5e6

Lines 8-27 of plugin/scripts/eml-compare-paired.praat at b4ca5e6.

```
    8  # v3.2: NEW-G3-1 — "New" after a Draw is a fresh analysis on the user's own
    9  #        table again. The spaghetti plot is drawn from a wide->long reshape,
   10  #        and a reshape is a Table like any other: drawing it re-pointed the
   11  #        shared column-name array at IT, and the entry form — which is
   12  #        rebuilt from that array every pass — came back offering
   13  #        Subject / Condition / Value, the reshape's ROLE names, on a reshape
   14  #        that had already been removed. Run then died with "Column not
   15  #        found: Condition" and Back returned to the same form. The form now
   16  #        re-reads the user's table on every pass, so what it offers is a
   17  #        property of the form rather than a hope about what the last callee
   18  #        left behind. The reshape is also named after the user's table
   19  #        instead of "pairedLong", so the figure's automatic title and its
   20  #        save stem name something the user can recognise.
   21  # v3.1: D90 — the spaghetti plot's axis labels no longer come from the
   22  #        wide->long reshape's role names ("Condition", "Value"). The measure
   23  #        and the contrast are derived from the two column names and
   24  #        registered against the role names with the graph layer's D90
   25  #        label-override registry (@emlSetLabelOverride).
   26  # v3.0: Wrapper infrastructure refactor. repeat/until replaces goto/label.
   27  # v2.0: Full convergence — orchestrator + @emlGuessColumnRoles.
```

```
    8  # v3.5: D48/D49 — the per-group block is announced with its own header and
    9  #       counts, so it no longer reads as loose output past the end of the
   10  #       overall report, and the groups too small to analyse are named on one
   11  #       summary line inside the block rather than two orphan lines each.
   12  #       D104 — a grouped run exports ONE tidy frame in which every row is
   13  #       labelled in `term` ("(overall)" / "<group column> = <level>"),
   14  #       instead of dropping the per-group results from the export and
   15  #       accumulating them in the legacy buffer under a fabricated table name.
   16  # v3.4: D47 — the "Group column" menu is filtered: it now offers only
   17  #       columns with 2..(n/3) distinct levels, and never the columns
   18  #       currently bound to X and Y. D51 — Draw sets
   19  #       emlGraphsPresetRegressionLine so the scatter carries the line whose
   20  #       R-squared it annotates. D53 — the Test menu carries a one-line
   21  #       Pearson/Spearman assumption note.
   22  # v3.3: Missing-data fix (correctness). Per-group correlation now uses
   23  #       @eml_getGroupPairedData (row-wise complete-case within the group)
   24  #       instead of two independent @eml_getGroupData calls, which
   25  #       misaligned X and Y when cells were missing; excluded-row note added.
   26  # v3.2: Per-group correlation output replaced with shared reporter
   27  #       (@emlReportCorrelationAnalysis) for rich Info window output.
   28  # v3.1: Use emlGraphsPresetCorrType$ global instead of direct annotCorrType$
   29  #        set (survives per-call reset in graphs form).
   30  # v3.0: Wrapper infrastructure refactor. Shared @emlWrapperInit,
   31  #        @emlWrapperExportCSV. repeat/until replaces goto/label.
   32  # v2.0: Full convergence — orchestrator + @emlGuessColumnRoles.
```

```
   28  #        returned parametric. The gate now mirrors eml-analysis.praat's

   44  #        agree. Two-group parametric route derives wizEqualVar/wizTName$
   45  #        from the "Variance assumption" field and passes it to
   46  #        @emlRunTwoGroupAnalysis instead of hardcoding Welch. ANOVA and
   47  #        Kruskal-Wallis post-hoc plan strings now name the method actually
   48  #        dispatched, and no longer claim conditional ("if significant")
   49  #        execution for Tukey/Dunn, which run unconditionally.
   50  # v2.2: Item 1 — replaced the call to the removed @emlExtractMultipleGroups
   51  #        and the 1-argument @eml_getGroupData call with the current
   52  #        extraction API.
   53  # v2.1: Dialog design system v2. Question-first navigation with semantic
   54  #        emoji (⚖️📈📊🎯). Fields-first column selection with teaching
   55  #        below fold. Verdict emoji (✅❌⚠️) on test selection dialogs.
   56  #        Zone separators (───── and · · · ·) replace tree notation (├──└──)
   57  #        and ═══ separators. Emoji echo in optionmenu option labels.
   58  #        📋 Table context zone on all dialogs. See DESIGN_DIALOG_SYSTEM.md.
   59  # v2.0: Renamed emlWizardMode → emlShowExplanations. Added
   60  #        emlGraphsPresetRegressionLine for regression draw path.
   61  # v1.9: Wizard mode flag. Sets emlShowExplanations = 1 after includes,
   62  #        enabling third-column value-anchored explanations in all
   63  #        @emlReport* procedures. "Why:" lines now wizard-only.
   64  #
   65  # v1.8: Dialog restructuring. Normality check and column selection on
   66  #        separate upstream page; test config page shows column summary,
   67  #        normality result, and pre-selects parametric/nonparametric.
   68  #        Column index preservation across Back navigation. New procedure

   81  #        "single"). Per-group testing for k-group path (was pooled).

   83  #        Result carry-forward pre-selects parametric/nonparametric on
   84  #        loop-back via normDefault. Table preparation refactored into
   85  #        @wizardPrepareTable (replaces 8 inline blocks).
   86  #        Depends on eml-core-descriptive v1.1 (@emlShapiroWilk).
   87  # v1.6: Column defaults via @emlGuessColumnRoles (weighted keyword
   88  #        matching + type detection) replacing positional guessing.
   89  #        All 8 dialog locations wired: data+group (3), data-only (2),
   90  #        two-factor (1), paired/correlation col1+col2 (2).
   91  #        Fixed ANOVA post-hoc double dispatch: doTukey now depends on
   92  #        corrApproach; pairwise orchestrator only called for Scheffe
   93  #        and Welch+BH paths. Eliminated "Unknown pairwise test: tukey".
```

```
   11  # v1.7: AUTHOR RULING, 16 August 2026: REGISTER BATCH VOICE ANALYSIS. Two menu
   12  #       lines added — the "-- eml batch --" separator and "Batch voice
   13  #       analysis..." — in the position the tabling note's own restore lines
   14  #       named, after "EML Graphs...". No action button on any object type: the
   15  #       module reads a folder off disk and ignores the selection entirely, so a
   16  #       button that appeared because a Sound was selected would be telling the
   17  #       user something untrue. Nothing else is added, removed or re-chained.
   18  #       Two things about this file turned out to be wrong and are corrected in
   19  #       comment: after$ does NOT decide the rendered order once an anchor has
   20  #       been used (source order does), and a "-- label --" separator renders as
   21  #       a rule with no text, so none of the nine labels is ever seen by a user.
   22  #       Both were photographed under Xvfb, not reasoned: harness/batchgui.
   23  #       Coverage: validate/v72_batch_registration.R.

   27  #       added for "Describe, Compare, Correlate, Regression, Wizard" and did
```

## plugin/stats/eml-analysis.praat @ b4ca5e6

Lines 8-65 of plugin/stats/eml-analysis.praat at b4ca5e6.

```
    8  # v1.3: The normality decision rule is extracted into
    9  #        @emlNormalityRecommendation and is now written down in exactly ONE
   10  #        place. It had three hand-maintained copies that disagreed: this
   11  #        file, @wizardNormDiag (scripts/eml-wizard.praat) and the per-group
   12  #        branch of scripts/eml-check-normality.praat, the last still on
   13  #        hard-coded thresholds of 1 and 3 and on the `skKurtFail or swFail`
   14  #        gate this file retired on 5 August. All three sites now call the
   15  #        one procedure. The rule and the thresholds are unchanged here;
   16  #        @emlRunNormalityAnalysis's outputs are identical. (D137)
   17  #
   18  # v1.2: Correctness + robustness fixes in the orchestrator layer.
   19  #   item 1 — emlRMPostHoc no longer renders a failed pairwise test as an
   20  #            adjusted p of 0. A test that fails (or returns an undefined p)
   21  #            now propagates "undefined" into the raw p-vector, so the
   22  #            adjustment procedures exclude it from k the way R's p.adjust
   23  #            excludes NA. Undefined p-values print as "n/a" in both the raw
   24  #            and adjusted columns, and a note names each skipped pair with
   25  #            the reason.
   26  #   item 2 — emlRMPostHoc validates .adjMethod$. An unrecognised string used
   27  #            to fall through to Holm silently while the header printed the
   28  #            requested name; the header now prints the method that actually
   29  #            ran and the substitution is disclosed.
   30  #   item 3 — emlRunCorrelationAnalysis captures each test's outputs into
   31  #            locals immediately after its own call and restores them before
   32  #            reporting, so a nested/re-entrant call cannot overwrite them.
   33  #            Previously "both" reported Spearman's rank-based r, t, df and p
   34  #            under the Pearson heading, in the Info report AND the CSV.
   35  #            The same capture/restore is applied to the Mann-Whitney outputs
   36  #            in emlRunTwoGroupAnalysis, which @emlRankBiserialR re-enters.
   37  #   item 4 — emlRunTwoGroupAnalysis checks the error$ of every test it runs.
   38  #            The reporter performs no error checks, so a failed test used to
   39  #            be printed as undefined results and written to the CSV. Failed
   40  #            branches are now dropped from the reported test type and the
   41  #            reason is surfaced. emlRunCorrelationAnalysis likewise surfaces
   42  #            a failed Spearman test, which the reporter swallowed silently.
   43  #   item 5 — emlReportPairwiseComparison header said "Pairwise holm (holm
   44  #            adjustment)": it used emlPairwiseT.method$, which is the
   45  #            ADJUSTMENT method, as the test name. Now derived from .test$.
   46  #   item 6 — emlRunRegressionAnalysis and emlRunNormalityAnalysis guard
   47  #            their column names with "Get column index:" instead of aborting
   48  #            the whole script with a raw Praat error.
   49  #   item 7 — Documented the reserved-but-unread parameters
   50  #            (emlRunNormalityAnalysis.testType$,
   51  #            emlRunRepeatedMeasuresAnalysis.subjectCol$,
   52  #            emlRunFriedmanAnalysis.subjectCol$) and the unimplemented
   53  #            emlRunReliabilityAnalysis stub. Parameter lists are unchanged
   54  #            because callers pass arguments positionally.
   55  #   item 8 — Friedman tie correction verified against R's friedman.test on
   56  #            tied data; formula and clamp are correct. No change.
   57  #
   58  # v1.1: Missing-data fix (correctness). emlRunPairedAnalysis and
   59  #        emlRunCorrelationAnalysis now extract their two columns with
   60  #        row-wise complete-case deletion (@emlExtractPairedColumns)
   61  #        instead of two independent per-column @emlExtractColumn calls,
   62  #        which misaligned the pairs when cells were missing in different
   63  #        rows. Analyzed n is now the complete-pair count and a
   64  #        "rows excluded for missing data" note is emitted when any row
   65  #        is dropped.
```

```
    8  # v1.2: Audit fixes (items 5, 6, 7).
    9  #        Item 5 — @emlShapiroWilk: the second pair-weight coefficient is
   10  #          now applied from n >= 6, not n >= 7, matching Royston AS R94 and
   11  #          R's swilk.c (which branches on n > 5). n = 6 was the only sample
   12  #          size in 3..12 that disagreed with R; it now agrees to 1e-6.
   13  #        Item 6 — @emlKurtosis returned a spurious finite value (e.g. -13.5
   14  #          at n = 4) when the standard deviation is zero; the standardised
   15  #          moment is 0/0 there. It now returns undefined with .error$ set.
   16  #          @emlSkewness had the analogous defect (returned 0) and is fixed
   17  #          the same way. Both procedures now expose .error$.
   18  #        Item 7 — Guards for undefined elements and out-of-range arguments.
   19  #          sort# raises a hard error on a vector containing undefined, which
   20  #          aborted the whole calling script from @emlPercentile,
   21  #          @emlTrimmedMean, @emlWinsorizedMean and @emlMAD. New helper
   22  #          @eml_hasUndefined lets those procedures return undefined instead.
   23  #          @emlCI now rejects confidenceLevel outside (0, 1): a level of 1
   24  #          made invStudentQ(0, df) loop forever.
   25  #
   26  # v1.1: Added @emlShapiroWilk (Royston 1995, AS R94) and helper
   27  #        @eml_swPoly. Shapiro-Wilk W test for normality, n = 3–5000.
   28  #        Verified against scipy.stats.shapiro and R shapiro.test.
```

```
    8  # v1.5: Numeric-column integrity and group-label normalisation.
    9  #        (1) @eml_getGroupData / @eml_getGroupPairedData now verify that
   10  #        "Get all numbers in column:" will return VALUES and not Praat's
   11  #        alphabetical RANK substitution, and abort with a clear message
   12  #        when it would not. (2) @emlValidateNumericColumn hardened:
   13  #        per-cell check across every row, strict-numeric verdict, first
   14  #        offending row and its literal contents, and a coercion-hazard
   15  #        warning. It is now actually called (previously zero call sites).
   16  #        (3) number() coercion hazards are detected and reported; the
   17  #        European decimal comma is listed first because it is the only
   18  #        one that yields a plausible wrong number rather than a dropped
   19  #        row. (4) @emlCountGroups normalises group labels (case and
   20  #        leading/trailing whitespace) and warns when normalisation
   21  #        merged distinct spellings; the same normalisation is applied
   22  #        by the group extractors so counting and extraction agree.
   23  #        New helpers: @eml_normalizeLabel, @eml_strictNumericColumn.
   24  #
   25  # v1.4: @eml_getGroupPairedData — row-wise complete-case extraction of
   26  #        two numeric columns within one group, for grouped correlation
   27  #        (keeps X and Y aligned; per-column extraction misaligned pairs
   28  #        on missing data).
   29  # v1.3: @emlGuessColumnRoles — infers column roles (group, data,
   30  #        subject, time, factor) from column names via weighted keyword
   31  #        matching + type detection. Replaces positional guessing in
   32  #        wizard and graph form dialogs. Helper: @eml_kwScan.
   33  # v1.2: Group sort order — @emlCountGroups optionally sorts labels
   34  #        alphabetically when emlGroupSortAlphabetical = 1. Default 0
   35  #        (table/discovery order). Global initialized in this file.
```

```
    8  # v1.4: D125 — @emlPairwiseT and @emlPairwiseWilcoxon now call
    9  #        @emlRequireNumericColumn (.strict = 0) straight after the
   10  #        @emlRequireColumnPresent check D116 gave them. An all-blank data
   11  #        column used to leave both with an empty error$ and a matrix of
   12  #        undefined: no number was produced, so nothing could be misread as
   13  #        a result, but a direct caller could not tell a refusal from a
   14  #        computation and was told nothing about why. They now refuse in
   15  #        @emlAuditColumn's words, the same sentence @emlTwoWayAnova gives
   16  #        for the same column. Not reachable from the menus, where
   17  #        @emlRunPairwiseAnalysis asks both questions first; reachable from
   18  #        eml-lib-stats.praat, which is the supported direct-call path.
   19  #        No change on any column that holds numbers.
   20  #
   21  # v1.3: Statistical-correctness fixes (audit items 1-13).
   22  #   item 1  - @emlSpearmanCorrelation no longer clobbers
   23  #             @emlPearsonCorrelation's outputs: the shared computation
   24  #             moved into the internal @eml_pearsonCore, and both public
   25  #             procedures are now thin wrappers over it.
   26  #   item 2  - @emlPearsonCorrelation: a perfect correlation
   27  #             (.rSquared >= 1) gave an undefined t with p = 0, which the
   28  #             report layer rendered as "p < .001". .p is now 0 with new
   29  #             .perfect and .warning$ outputs disclosing it; n < 3 and
   30  #             zero-variance inputs are also guarded.
   31  #   item 3  - @emlTukeyHSD no longer fails open: an undefined q (zero SE
   32  #             or undefined MS-within) used to report p = 1.000 for every
   33  #             comparison. Per-comparison p is now undefined, counted in
   34  #             .nUndefined and disclosed in .warning$.
   35  #   item 4  - @emlOneWayAnova guards the eta-squared denominator
   36  #             (zero/undefined SS-total) instead of reporting eta^2 = 1.
   37  #   item 5  - @emlTwoWayAnova: SS_Error and SS_Total are now computed
   38  #             from within-cell deviations rather than parsed from
   39  #             Praat's Type I report, F and p re-derived from the
   40  #             corrected MS_Error, design balance checked, negative SS
   41  #             clamped, and the SS type stated in the output header.
   42  #             (Type III effect SS were already correct; untouched.)
   43  #   item 6  - @eml_parseAnovaLine matched row labels by first substring
   44  #             occurrence, so it returned factor1's row when factor2's
   45  #             name was a substring of it (and Error/Total collided the
   46  #             same way). Now matches the label field exactly.
   47  #   item 7  - @emlMannWhitneyU used a combined-N exact/approximate
   48  #             threshold and ignored ties; it now uses R's rule
   49  #             (n1 < 50 AND n2 < 50 AND no ties).
   50  #             @emlWilcoxonSignedRank keeps its n < 50 threshold (which
   51  #             already matched R) and adds the ties/zeroes conditions.
   52  #             One-tailed p-values in both returned the smaller tail
   53  #             regardless of direction; the alternative is now fixed as
   54  #             H1: group1 > group2 (matching R), with .pGreater/.pLess
   55  #             exposed. .tails is validated in both, and in
   56  #             @emlRankBiserialR / @emlMatchedPairsR.
   57  #   item 8  - @emlBonferroni, @emlHolm and @emlBenjaminiHochberg counted
   58  #             undefined elements in the comparison count. All three are
   59  #             now NA-safe (undefined in, undefined out; k excludes
   60  #             undefined), matching R's p.adjust.
   61  #   item 9  - @emlPairwiseT, @emlDunnTest and @emlPairwiseWilcoxon
   62  #             substituted rawP = 1 for a failed comparison, which reads
   63  #             as a computed non-significant result. All three now
   64  #             propagate undefined and report .nSkipped / .skipReason$.
   65  #   item 10 - @emlPairwiseT.method$ echoed the ADJUSTMENT argument, so
   66  #             report headers read "Pairwise holm". It now names the test
   67  #             ("Welch t-test" / "Student t-test"); the adjustment moved
   68  #             to .adjustMethod$.
   69  #   item 11 - @emlEpsilonSquared capped at 1 with .capped / .warning$,
   70  #             and guarded for .n <= 1.
   71  #   item 12 - @emlCohenD Hedges' J documentation corrected (worst-case
   72  #             error is 1.28% at df = 2, not 0.27%). Formula unchanged.
   73  #   item 13 - @emlOneWayAnova sums of squares computed by a two-pass
   74  #             centred algorithm instead of the raw-score Hays formulas,
   75  #             removing catastrophic cancellation on large-offset data.
   76  #
   77  # v1.2: @emlTukeyHSD internal sort removed — group order now controlled
   78  #        by @emlCountGroups via emlGroupSortAlphabetical global.
   79  #        .sortMap set to identity. No computation change.
   80  # v1.1: 10-group limit removal: deleted @eml_getGroupData dispatcher,
   81  #        all 7 consumers rewritten to use @emlCountGroups + on-demand
   82  #        @eml_getGroupData from eml-extract.praat.
```

```
    6  # v2.4: THE TWO GUARDS THAT KEEP A SAVE FROM KILLING THE SESSION, plus the
    7  #       receipt that could not draw what it was given, plus a probe that
    8  #       assumed instead of classifying. Four defects, all confirmed live at
    9  #       HEAD before a line was changed (audit of 14 Aug 2026, §3 S4 and §6).
   10  #
   11  #       NEW-G2-1, sev 2 -- a "/" in the Base name field reached writeFile:
   12  #         verbatim. Praat stopped the script inside @emlSavePanel, so the
   13  #         receipt never drew, the panel never returned, and the caller's
   14  #         Done | Save | Draw | New loop was gone with the analysis behind it.
   15  #         @eml_saveSafeBaseName now makes the typed name into a file name
   16  #         ONCE, before the collision walk, so every file of one press still
   17  #         shares one base name and one stamp.
   18  #       NEW-G12-5, sev 2 -- an unwritable target folder did the same thing
   19  #         one step later, with "unexpected error 30" for a message, and an
   20  #         unwritable PARENT did it one step earlier on the panel's own bare
   21  #         `createFolder:`. @eml_saveFolderWritable asks both questions once,
   22  #         with `nocheck` so the asking cannot itself abort, and the panel now
   23  #         RETURNS on a no instead of dying on one.
   24  #       SAVED-OVERPRINT, sev 4, five sightings -- the "Saved" receipt reserved
   25  #         one line per comment: and the toolkit drew several when a path was
   26  #         longer than the dialog, so each long path printed its tail over the
   27  #         path below. Every line now goes through @emlWrapText at 62
   28  #         characters, the width measured on 6.6.30 and the one
   29  #         @emlErrorDialog already uses.
   30  #       NEW-G12-1, sev 2 (this file's half) -- author ruling, 15 Aug 2026:
   31  #         "probe should classify column types, never assume numeric."
   32  #         @emlWrapperInit's coercion arms manufacture a "row" column out of
   33  #         row labels that a Matrix never has and a TableOfReal may not have.
   34  #         Praat renders those undefined cells as "?", which is neither of the
   35  #         two forms @eml_strictNumericColumn's scan recognises, so the
   36  #         numericiser behind it raised before the wrapper's dialog opened --
   37  #         on EVERY Matrix, every unlabelled TableOfReal and every partially
   38  #         labelled one. @eml_auditLabelColumn classifies the column and makes
   39  #         it readable; it does not invent default labels, so it composes with
   40  #         whatever the conversion side decides to put there.
   41  # v2.3: @emlWrapperExportCSV DELETED (author ruling, 14 Aug 2026: "We can
   42  #       retire the superseded csv wrapper code if we use it nowhere now").
   43  #       It had no callers in any tree. A tombstone stands where it was,
   44  #       because its folder-seed line is the reason every non-graphing Save
   45  #       button broke on 13 Aug; the seed now sits at file scope above
   46  #       @emlSavePanel.
   47  # v2.2: @emlSavePanel -- one save, one folder, one name. The figure, the
   48  #       result frames and the Info window report are written together under a
   49  #       shared stem. Before this the figure and the CSV were two journeys with
   50  #       two folder memories and two naming rules, and the report could not be
   51  #       saved at all. @emlWrapperExportCSV is superseded and now has no
   52  #       callers.
   53  # v2.1: Both arms of @emlExportResultFiles are now non-destructive. The
   54  #       declared arm could not be until @emlGenerateUniquePath moved to
   55  #       eml-core-utilities.praat -- it lived in the graphs form, which is
   56  #       included after this file. The BASE is uniqued once against the tidy
   57  #       frame and every frame in the set carries the walked suffix, so a set
   58  #       never half-overwrites an older set.
   59  # v2.0: THE MIGRATION FORK IS NOW A PROCEDURE, @emlExportResultFiles, and the
   60  #       graphs form's Exp CSV button goes through it. The fork lived inline in
   61  #       @emlWrapperExportCSV, so the plugin's OTHER export -- the post-draw
   62  #       "Exp CSV" button in eml-graphs-form.praat -- could not reach it and
   63  #       called @emlExportStatsCSV directly. The same analysis therefore wrote
   64  #       three broom-shaped files from the stats menu and one legacy long-format
   65  #       file from the graphs form. v20/v21 enumerate the stats-menu
   66  #       orchestrators, so neither could see a second exporter, and no harness
   67  #       had pressed that button until 13 Aug 2026. Writing only -- the two
   68  #       callers report differently and a shared procedure cannot open a dialog
   69  #       from inside another one.
   70  # v1.9: COMMENTS ONLY — no executable line changed. Five statements that the
   71  #       code under them contradicts, corrected.
   72  #       (1) The Provides list named @emlCSVAddRow. No procedure of that name
   73  #         has ever existed anywhere in the plugin. The list is now the real
   74  #         CSV primitives (@emlCSVInit / @emlCSVSetTable / @emlCSVTermType /
   75  #         @emlCSVAdd / @emlCSVAddStr / @emlCSVAddDescriptives /
   76  #         @emlExportStatsCSV), the rest of the public surface by family, and
   77  #         a counting rule so the whole thing can be checked rather than
   78  #         believed.
   79  #       (2) @emlWrapText's header cited "@comment:", which reads as a
   80  #         procedure call in this file's notation and is not one — `comment:`
   81  #         is a Praat dialog command. It also described @emlErrorDialog as the
   82  #         only consumer; @emlReportNote and, since D124,
   83  #         @emlDrawAnnotationBlock also wrap through it.
   84  #       (3) The D27 provenance note said the CSV self-documents the
   85  #         adjustment "in its `test` column". The long-format schema
   86  #         (emlCSV_header$) has no `test` column; the adjustment is a row
   87  #         whose `field` is "adjustment".
   88  #       (4) An "END OF MODULE" banner sat above @emlReportDescriptiveAnalysis
   89  #         rather than at the end of the module.
   90  #       (5) That procedure's note cited a bare "line 9434" for a
   91  #         "Procedure not found" error. Praat counts that line in the
   92  #         FLATTENED script, after include expansion, so it names no line of
   93  #         any file on disk. Replaced with the anchor and the reason.
   94  # v1.8: Audit fixes (items 8, 9).
   95  #       Item 8 — @emlFormatEffectLabel labelled R-squared values with
   96  #         Cohen's d thresholds. It now recognises "r_squared" (and the
   97  #         aliases "R2" and "r2") and applies Cohen's R-squared benchmarks
   98  #         0.01 / 0.09 / 0.25. The token string "r_squared" is unchanged and
   99  #         still accepted. An unrecognised effect type no longer silently
  100  #         falls back to d thresholds: .label$ is now "" and the new
  101  #         .recognized flag is 0, so callers can detect the condition.
  102  #       Item 9 — @emlFormatP reported "p = 1.000" for any p in
  103  #         [0.9995, 1), overstating the result as an exact 1. Such values now
  104  #         format as "p > .999". p = 1 exactly still formats as "p = 1.000".
  105  # v1.7: @emlWrapperInit accepts Table, TableOfReal, and Matrix objects.
  106  #       Auto-converts TableOfReal/Matrix to Table with Info window notification.
  107  #       Outputs .converted flag for caller awareness.
```

# CAPTURE: THE TREE THIS SWEEP EDITS — what is still in the shipped headers (49ce40b)

## plugin/graphs/eml-annotation-procedures.praat @ 49ce40b

Lines 8-90 of plugin/graphs/eml-annotation-procedures.praat at 49ce40b.

```
    8  # v3.22: THE BRACKET LAYOUT DISCLOSES ITS POST-HOC AND ITS CORRECTION, as the
    9  #        MATRIX layout already does with its sub-line naming the post-hoc test
   10  #        and, on the parametric arm, where its family-wise control comes from.
   11  #        The brackets are the layout that puts the p-values and the stars
   12  #        directly ON the picture, so they are the one a reader is most likely
   13  #        to take a claim off: three stars over a pair have to say which test
   14  #        produced them and whether anything was corrected.
   15  #        WHAT THE AUTHOR ASKED, AND THE ANSWER THAT SET THE SHAPE. SPSS states
   16  #        "Adjustment for multiple comparisons: Bonferroni" beneath its
   17  #        pairwise-comparisons display. Base R states nothing, but every R
   18  #        package that DRAWS significance brackets discloses:
   19  #        ggpubr::stat_compare_means prints the method on the plot by default,
   20  #        and ggstatsplot carries the pairwise test AND the adjustment method
   21  #        in a caption on every figure. The caption is the closest precedent
   22  #        and it is what this is.
   23  #        [1] Two new globals, @emlBridgeGroupComparison writes both:
   24  #            annotBracketPosthoc$ names the pairwise test, annotBracketAdjust$
   25  #            says what was done about multiplicity. THEY ARE STORED AS TWO
   26  #            HALVES, not as one sentence, and that is not tidiness — it is
   27  #            what lets @emlDrawBracketCaption break the caption at its seam on
   28  #            a narrow figure without doing string surgery on a sentence, and
   29  #            it is what makes "the two arms say different things" a property
   30  #            a validator can read rather than infer.
   31  #        [2] THE TWO ARMS SAY DIFFERENT THINGS, and the asymmetry is the
   32  #            content. Tukey's p comes from the studentized range distribution
   33  #            and is already family-wise over the set of pairwise comparisons,
   34  #            so the parametric arm STATES that and claims no further
   35  #            adjustment. Dunn's z-tests carry no family-wise control of their
   36  #            own, the form still offers the menu there and @emlDunnTest still
   37  #            honours it, so the nonparametric arm NAMES the method the user
   38  #            chose — the same token the Info-window report prints — and
   39  #            claims nothing about what it achieves. One sentence covering both
   40  #            would be false on one of them whichever way it was written.
   41  #        [3] PLACEMENT IS A CAPTION UNDER THE FRAME, not a second box inside
   42  #            it. annotTextX/annotTextY is a single slot with an alpha
   43  #            background already carrying the omnibus line, and a second box
   44  #            inside the frame would collide with the very brackets it
   45  #            describes — the brackets occupy the top of the plot by
   46  #            construction and @emlPlaceElements penalises the top quadrants
   47  #            for exactly that reason. The caption takes its own band below
   48  #            emlSetAdaptiveTheme.outerBottom and reports that band to
   49  #            @emlExpandDrawnExtent, the mechanism @emlDrawMatrixPanel already
   50  #            uses, so @emlAssertFullViewport widens the saved image to include
   51  #            it instead of cropping it off.
   52  #        [4] IT FITS OR IT SHRINKS, AND IF IT STILL DOES NOT FIT IT WRAPS. The
   53  #            caption is measured with Text width (mm) against the band it has,
   54  #            scaled down to a 5 pt floor, and split onto two lines at the seam
   55  #            between the two halves if one line still overruns. A disclosure
   56  #            that runs off the canvas is not a disclosure, and it is the
   57  #            failure mode a check anchored on where the FIRST ink falls cannot
   58  #            see: clipping takes ink off the RIGHT and leaves the left edge
   59  #            exactly where it was.
   60  #        [5] THE TWO-GROUP ARMS DISCLOSE TOO, and the invariant has no special
   61  #            case in it: EVERY BRACKET-BEARING FIGURE NAMES ITS TEST. Two
   62  #            groups is ONE comparison and nothing is corrected, so those arms
   63  #            must not borrow the Tukey sentence — that is a claim about the
   64  #            studentized range distribution and a Welch t makes no such claim
   65  #            — but they must not fall silent either. A stated absence is a
   66  #            disclosure; an empty caption cannot be told apart from a build
   67  #            that lost its caption. So they name the comparison ("Comparison:
   68  #            Welch t-test", "Comparison: Mann-Whitney U test") and then say
   69  #            "one comparison; no adjustment applied", in the voice note [2]
   70  #            describes: what produced the p-value, then what was or was not
   71  #            done about multiplicity.
   72  #        [6] AND THE CORNER BOX WITH IT. Every arm that can produce a bracket
   73  #            sets annotTextN, the two-group arms included, so
   74  #            @emlGraphsPostDispatchAnnotations always has an omnibus line to
   75  #            route into the corner block and no bracket figure leaves the
   76  #            session without a test name on it somewhere. validate/v76
   77  #            enumerates the arms and asserts each one sets it, so an arm added
   78  #            later without a test name goes red.
   79  #
   80  # v3.21: THE ANNOTATION BRIDGE NOW DECLARES. @emlReportBridgeStats ran the
   81  #        analysis, printed it and filled the legacy CSV buffer, but never
   82  #        called the @emlDeclare*Result family -- so an annotated graph's
   83  #        export could only ever be the old single long-format file, for an
   84  #        analysis the stats menu exported as tidy/glance/augment. Added the
   85  #        same declaration calls the orchestrators make, for all three families
   86  #        the bridge produces: two-group, one-way ANOVA with Tukey, and
   87  #        Kruskal-Wallis with Dunn. Extras staged FIRST in every case, because
   88  #        staging reuses the one tidy collector and the model's own tidy has to
   89  #        be what is left in it when @emlResultWrite runs. No new reporting
   90  #        code: these are calls to the procedures the stats path already uses.
```

```
   93  # v3.20: THE LEGEND IS AN ANNOTATION, AND NOW PAYS FOR ITS OWN ROOM.
   94  #        eml-draw-procedures.praat has said since 5 Aug that "any extra room
   95  #        a figure needs is a property of what is drawn on it ... and is
   96  #        supplied by @emlComputeAnnotationHeadroom at the annotation stage".
   97  #        Only the significance bracket honoured it. A legend drawn inside the
   98  #        plot is an annotation box by the same definition and contributed
   99  #        NOTHING to the axis, so the figure was never given room for it and
  100  #        it landed on the data — 13145 data pixels covered on a five-group
  101  #        line chart at 6 x 4, measured on the rendered PNG, 9 Aug 2026, by
  102  #        differencing it against the same figure on the same axis with the
  103  #        legend suppressed. The worst of the six types measured 23702.
  104  #        [1] @emlComputeAnnotationHeadroom takes two more arguments — the
  105  #            laid-out legend height in inches (from @emlMeasureLegendPanel,
  106  #            the same measurement the renderer lays itself out with, so the
  107  #            two cannot disagree) and the corner it will occupy — and returns
  108  #            .footroom beside .headroom, because a legend in a bottom corner
  109  #            is not helped by room above the data. Both bands go into ONE
  110  #            algebraic solve: the expansion is not linear in the band, so
  111  #            solving twice and adding would be wrong. With no legend the
  112  #            outputs are bit-identical to v3.19, and .overflow still means
  113  #            "the brackets do not fit" and nothing else.
  114  #        [2] The legend band is capped at emlLegendHeadroomShare (default 0.5)
  115  #            of the panel, so a 24-entry key cannot leave a figure with no
  116  #            plot. A capped band is NOT silently swallowed: .legendOverflow
  117  #            goes up and the caller names the shortfall in the Info window.
  118  #        [3] @emlPlaceElements is UNCHANGED, deliberately — see the note in
  119  #            its header for why penalising quadrants by legend size does not
  120  #            make the corner choice and the headroom agree, and what does.
  121  #
  122  # v3.19: @emlDrawAnnotationBlock wraps.
  123  #        [1] The box was sized to its longest line exactly as handed in, so
  124  #            one long disclosure line made it wide enough to sit over the
  125  #            data and, on a narrow figure, to run off the canvas. Lines are
  126  #            now wrapped through @emlWrapText to a budget taken from the
  127  #            frame (emlAnnotBlockWidthShare, 0.55 of the inner viewport less
  128  #            the box's padding and inset), never from a character constant —
  129  #            annotSize scales with the viewport, so a character is a near

  131  #            characters" has always meant "up to seven eighths of the panel".
  132  #            Measured 8 Aug 2026 on a 3.6 x 3 inch 4-bar chart: box width
  133  #            fell from 1.79 to 0.52 of the axis range, and the rightmost

  135  #            to 929, which is exactly where a figure carrying no box at all
  136  #            ends. On the real @emlDisclose path the box fell from 0.75 to
  137  #            0.49 of the frame and the data pixels it covered from 28 to 0.
  138  #        [2] Wrapping can only trade width for height, so the box is
  139  #            re-wrapped wider (to at most 0.72) when it would otherwise

  282  #        produce identical Info window output regardless of entry point
  283  #        (stats wrapper or graphs tool) and populate CSV rows via

  287  #        to annotInch/fontInch (panel's own font sizes) instead of
  288  #        bodyInch (graph's body font). Non-rotated header multiplier
  289  #        reduced from 1.8 to 1.2 (matching rotated path). Proportional
  290  #        ratios match @emlDrawTitle for visual consistency.

  297  #        "6" against its own list of four, and four is also what the code
  298  #        has. Cleared by

  300  #        "Tukey HSD · Lower: |Cohen's d|" (with effect) or just the

  306  #        by @emlDrawMatrixPanel since v1.4). .showEffect gate added to 5
  307  #        hardcoded matrix population sites (3 effect label, 2 D-value) —
  308  #        2-group NP/P matrix paths and ANOVA k-group significant path now
  309  #        respect the toggle. .showNS gate added to all 6 matrix population
  310  #        sites — upper-triangle cells show "—" when non-significant and
  311  #        showNS = 0 (lower-triangle effect sizes unaffected).

  314  #        @emlDrawMatrixPanel rendering. Matrix panel is now a pure renderer.

  317  #        vs rank-biserial r (0.5/0.3/0.1). Cohen's d stored as
  318  #        abs() in matrix (consistent with rank-biserial r). Matrix
  319  #        subtitle reads "(magnitude)" to clarify unsigned display
  320  #        while omnibus retains signed d. Removed >10 group hard
  321  #        error — stats and Info window report now run for any group
  322  #        count; matrix panel's own viewport-based display tiers
  323  #        handle visual degradation. Column header-to-data padding
  324  #        increased (0.3 → 0.8 fontInch) for non-rotated headers.
  325  # v2.3: @emlPlaceElements replaces @emlAutoPlaceLegend — universal
  326  #        quadrant-density + bracket-penalty corner selection for
  327  #        legend and annotation block placement. All graph types
  328  #        now use adaptive placement.
  329  # v2.2: annotMatrixEffectLabel$ now stores full label ("Cohen's d" or
  330  #        "rank-biserial r") instead of bare letter. The subtitle does not
  331  #        hardcodes "Cohen's" prefix — was producing "Cohen's r" for
  332  #        nonparametric tests.
  333  #
  334  # v2.0: @emlReportBridgeStats: 2-group path now includes group descriptives
  335  #        (N, Mean, SD, Median) matching the k-group report format.
  336  # v1.9: @emlFormatAnnotLabel: new .effectLabel$ parameter — bracket labels
  337  #        now show context-appropriate symbol ("d" for Cohen's d, "r" for
  338  #        rank-biserial r) instead of hardcoded "d". All 9 callsites updated.
  339  #        @emlBridgeGroupComparison: .forceMatrix replaced by .layoutMode
  340  #        (1=auto, 2=brackets, 3=matrix). Auto defaults to matrix at k>=3.
  341  #        KW omnibus string now includes epsilon-squared (e2 = H/(n-1)).
  342  #        New @emlReportBridgeStats procedure — writes full statistical
  343  #        report (omnibus + descriptives + p-value matrix + effect matrix)
  344  #        to Info window, callable from both graph dispatch and stats wrappers.
  345  # v1.8: Nonparametric k-group matrix (KW/Dunn's) now computes pairwise
  346  #        rank-biserial |r| for the lower triangle, mirroring the ANOVA
  347  #        path's per-pair Cohen's d. Bracket output also includes r when
  348  #        effect sizes are requested. Subtitle auto-selects "r" label.
  349  # v1.7: @emlDrawMatrixPanel subtitle now shows three-way conditional:
  350  #        "Upper: adjusted p-values · Lower: Cohen's d" (parametric),
  351  #        "Upper: adjusted p-values · Lower: rank-biserial r" (nonparam),
  352  #        or "Adjusted p-values" (no effect sizes). Previously subtitle
  353  #        was suppressed entirely when hasEffect = 0.
  354  # v1.6: Bracket headroom circular dependency fix — algebraic solve for
  355  #        post-expansion wpiY with overflow guard. Matrix panel top-down
  356  #        sizing (1.0–1.3× responsive zone, content minimum cap). Swatch
  357  #        squareness fix — computed in inches, converted to grid units.

  361  #        subtitle spacing increased (titleY 0.5, subtitleY 1.5, headerY
  362  #        2.2, dataTop 2.8) to prevent collision at 10 groups. Legend
  363  #        row centered on grid midpoint instead of left-aligned.
  364  # v1.3: @emlDrawAnnotationBlock and @emlDrawAnnotation: replaced charW
  365  #        estimation (length * fixedWidth) with exact text measurement via
  366  #        Text width (world coordinates): — queries the rendering engine
  367  #        for actual rendered string width in the current font/size/axes.
  368  #        Fixes box sizing for proportional fonts (the fundamental flaw in
  369  #        all prior charW-based approaches). Symmetric vertical padding.
  370  #        @emlDrawRegressionLine: white halo (width 3.5) drawn under the
  371  #        colored line for visibility against dense data.
  372  #        Semi-transparent backgrounds via alpha sprite for annotation block

  374  # v1.2: charW estimation attempts.

  377  #        with background fill and corner positioning. New @emlOppositeCorner.
```

## plugin/graphs/eml-draw-procedures.praat @ 49ce40b

```
    8  # v1.26: Comments only — no executable line changed.
    9  # v1.25: Comments only — no executable line changed.
   10  #
   11  # v1.24: MARKER SHAPES for the four chart types that draw dots and lines.
   12  #        v1.23 gave the AREA marks a second dimension (8 hues x 3 fill
   13  #        patterns) and left the point marks where they were: a scatter, a
   14  #        line chart, a spaghetti plot and a time series have no area to fill,
   15  #        so all four went on cycling eight hues in silence above eight
   16  #        groups. Each now draws through @emlDrawMarker and takes its shape
   17  #        from emlSetColorPalette.marker[] -- circle, square, triangle, on the
   18  #        same slot arithmetic, so the ceiling is 24 for both families -- and
   19  #        each sets the legend's marker key so the key shows the shape and not
   20  #        merely the hue. @emlDrawTimeSeries and @emlDrawTimeSeriesCI drew no
   21  #        point markers at all before this and now place one at every plotted
   22  #        vertex that lies on the panel (not clamped: a clamped marker would
   23  #        claim an observation at a value nobody measured).
   24  #        @emlDrawScatterPlot additionally stands its alpha-sprite path down
   25  #        above eight groups -- the sprite set is circles only, so on macOS
   26  #        and Windows, where the sprites actually render, the shape would
   27  #        never have reached the page. Its B/W point colour moves from .fill$
   28  #        to .lightLine$, because the widened grey ramp takes .fill$ to 0.94
   29  #        and a 0.94 dot with no outline is not on the page at all.
   30  #
   31  # v1.23: The sub-group ceiling goes from 10 to 24. @emlSetColorPalette now
   32  #        defines a style as (one of 8 hues) x (one of 3 fill patterns:
   33  #        solid, diagonal hatch, dots), so 24 sub-groups can be drawn
   34  #        distinguishably where 10 could not — ten fill/line pairs include
   35  #        two literal duplicates and leave eight available styles unused. @emlDrawGroupedViolin and @emlDrawGroupedBoxPlot
   36  #        pass the pattern through to the primitives, the legend carries it,
   37  #        and the over-cap disclosure names the 8 x 3 space rather than a bare
   38  #        count. The boundary is deliberately in excess of what is likely to
   39  #        be reasonable: 24 sub-violins are not readable on a default 6-inch
   40  #        figure and are perfectly readable on a wide one, and that is the
   41  #        user's call to make.
   42  #
   43  # v1.22: Three more silences — a procedure that knows it has dropped
   44  #        something says so. Same three channels and helpers as v1.21.
   45  #        (1) THE OVER-CAP SCATTER. @emlDrawScatterPlot's grouped path writes
   46  #        a Pearson line, a Spearman line and a fitted-line formula PER GROUP
   47  #        straight into annotBlockN, bypassing @emlDisclose and its 20-line
   48  #        budget. At eight groups that is 24 lines: measured, the box runs
   49  #        from the top of the panel, across the x-axis and off the bottom edge
   50  #        of the figure, and the dropped-row disclosure that follows is
   51  #        refused by the spent budget and never appears. The
   52  #        lines are now buffered and committed ALL OR NONE — a box holding six
   53  #        of eight groups is indistinguishable from a complete one — and when
   54  #        they do not fit, every one of them is printed to the Info window and
   55  #        the figure says so in one line.
   56  #        (2) ZERO IS NOT "NO DATA". @emlMeasureBarData (v3.23, in
   57  #        eml-graph-procedures.praat) seeded emlBarData_mean[] and
   58  #        emlBarData_error[] to 0 rather than undefined, so this file's
   59  #        "<> undefined" guards for the bar and the whisker could never fire:
   60  #        .nSkippedBars and .nSkippedErrors were dead code and both
   61  #        disclosures reading them were unreachable. A group with no usable
   62  #        observation drew as a bar of height zero — the same mark a measured
   63  #        zero draws — and a whisker whose error was undefined simply did not
   64  #        appear. Fixed at the root; both guards now fire, the omitted bars
   65  #        are NAMED, and the v1.21 stopgap line "N group(s) shown at zero" is
   66  #        gone with the behaviour it described.
   67  #        (3) THE ELEVENTH SUB-GROUP. @emlDrawGroupedViolin and
   68  #        @emlDrawGroupedBoxPlot drop sub-groups past the tenth because
   69  #        @emlSetColorPalette declares ten fill/line pairs over eight hues.
   70  #        THE CAP IS LEGITIMATE AND IS NOT RAISED. What it must not be is
   71  #        silent, and the
   72  #        legend still listed sub-groups that had no violin or box anywhere on
   73  #        the figure. Now disclosed by name and row count, the legend is
   74  #        capped to what was drawn, and the slot geometry divides by the drawn
   75  #        count so the remaining violins stay centred.
   76  #        Verified by validate/v29_figure_disclosure.R.
   77  # v1.21: Figure disclosure unified across the ten Table-consuming draw
   78  #        procedures, on one rule — "draw the
   79  #        image as the image unless someone asks to annotate."
   80  #        (1) Nothing in this file writes to emlSubtitle$ any more.
   81  #        @emlDrawTimeSeries appended " | Mean per time point" and
   82  #        @emlDrawBarChart appended an error-bar/truncation/omitted-bars
   83  #        caption; both saved and restored the global, so it was never
   84  #        permanently corrupted, but the DRAWN figure carried the user's own
   85  #        subtitle with a machine tail after " | ", and drew it whether or not
   86  #        Annotate was ticked. emlSubtitle$ is the user's field: the graphs
   87  #        form asks for it and persists it to config.
   88  #        (2) All ten disclose dropped rows, in one wording and one counter
   89  #        idiom taken from @emlDrawViolinPlot and @emlDrawBoxPlot, which were
   90  #        the only two that did. Silent before: histogram, bar, scatter,
   91  #        grouped violin, grouped box, spaghetti, time series; time series
   92  #        with CI had the count but in its own words and indentation.
   93  #        (3) The four procedures that plot a MEAN rather than raw values now
   94  #        all say so — @emlDrawTimeSeries already did, @emlDrawTimeSeriesCI,
   95  #        @emlDrawBarChart and the line chart (graph type 5 with CI off, which
   96  #        IS @emlDrawTimeSeries) did not.
   97  #        (4) New @emlDiscloseBegin / @emlDisclose / @emlDiscloseEnd carry all
   98  #        of the above: Info window always, figure only when the user ticked
   99  #        Annotate, through the existing annotation block. See the block
  100  #        comment on those procedures for why the gate could not simply be
  101  #        left to @emlDrawAnnotationBlock.
  102  #        Verified by validate/v29_figure_disclosure.R.
  103  # v1.20: @emlDrawLMMForest brought into line with the drawing standards.
  104  #        (1) Rule 1: the bare `Marks bottom: 5` is replaced by
  105  #        @emlDrawAlignedMarksBottom with theme-derived tick targets, so the
  106  #        12%-buffered coefficient axis gets nice-number labels instead of
  107  #        arbitrary quarter-range values. (2) Rule 2: the procedure now opens
  108  #        with the theme prologue (@emlSetAdaptiveTheme / @emlSetColorPalette)
  109  #        that the other 14 draw procedures already used — it was the only one
  110  #        without it, so its margins, and therefore its box, ticks and labels,
  111  #        previously depended on whatever ambient font size the Picture window
  112  #        happened to hold. (3) Because the LMM tool reaches this procedure
  113  #        outside the graphs form, the display-toggle globals and colorMode$
  114  #        are seeded through a variableExists guard — without it the new marks
  115  #        call aborts with "Unknown variable" on the shipped path. (4) Rule 34:
  116  #        line widths, marker size, tick colour, text colour and the series
  117  #        colour now come from the theme and palette rather than hardcoded
  118  #        constants and bare Grey/Black; the four inlined replace$ calls are
  119  #        replaced by @emlSanitizeLabel. Deliberately deferred: adopting
  120  #        @emlDrawTitle, which would require converting the figure from the
  121  #        outer-viewport layout model to the theme's inner-viewport model.
  122  #        No arguments or return variables change.
  123  # v1.19: Robustness and disclosure fixes. (1) @emlDrawHistogram ungrouped
  124  #        path does not abort on clean single-column data — the group loop
  125  #        is driven by .hasGroups instead of the always-1 .nGroups, and the
  126  #        dead else branch is removed. (2) @emlDrawScatterPlot (ungrouped and
  127  #        grouped) now draws the same estimator it reports, labels the drawn
  128  #        line, and discloses the estimator unconditionally instead of gating
  129  #        the disclosure on scatterAnalysisType. (3) @emlDrawBarChart guards
  130  #        every undefined mean/error before it reaches a drawing command — an
  131  #        unusable bar is skipped and recorded rather than aborting the
  132  #        figure — discloses the error-bar meaning (SE / SD / custom column)
  133  #        in the caption and Info window, and marks error bars truncated at
  134  #        the axis limits with outward arrowheads instead of clipping them
  135  #        silently. (4) Axis seeding in @emlDrawTimeSeries and
  136  #        @emlDrawSpaghettiPlot does not take row 1 on faith — ranges are
  137  #        folded from the first valid observation, with a 0..1 fallback and
  138  #        an Info-window note when nothing is usable. (5) Guard sweep over
  139  #        the aborting draw procedures, using @emlDrawGroupedViolin as the
  140  #        reference: @emlDrawTimeSeries, @emlDrawSpaghettiPlot,
  141  #        @emlDrawTimeSeriesCI (missing TIME cells do not create phantom
  142  #        time points), @emlDrawViolinPlot and @emlDrawBoxPlot (undefined
  143  #        values filtered at accumulation so they never reach @emlPercentile's
  144  #        sort#, empty groups skipped, unit-axis fallback). Dropped rows and
  145  #        skipped groups are reported in the Info window.
  146  # v1.18: Graph correctness. (1) Grouped scatter does not print a
  147  #        false "R² = 0.000" for Spearman/Theil-Sen fits — R² is emitted only
  148  #        for the OLS/Pearson line (guarded on annotCorrType$, mirroring the
  149  #        ungrouped path). (2) Bar chart y-range routed through
  150  #        @emlComputeAxisRange with the tracked data min, and bar baseline
  151  #        decoupled from yMin (bars emanate from 0) so all-negative means
  152  #        render. (3) Scatter axes use an adaptive @emlComputeNiceStep roundTo
  153  #        instead of a hardcoded 1, so fractional data is not over-ranged.
  154  # v1.17: Per-group scatter correlation output replaced with shared
  155  #        reporter (@emlReportCorrelationAnalysis) for rich Info window output
  156  #        including R-squared, effect size labels, and Why commentary.
```
