  159  # v1.16: Group sort unification — replaced all inline group discovery
  160  #         with @emlCountGroups calls: @emlDrawViolinPlot,
  161  #         @emlDrawBoxPlot, @emlDrawGroupedViolin, @emlDrawGroupedBoxPlot,
  162  #         @emlDrawSpaghettiPlot (conditions and subjects). Legend labels
  163  #         sanitized in @emlDrawTimeSeries, @emlDrawTimeSeriesCI, and
  164  #         @emlDrawSpaghettiPlot (underscore→subscript fix). All draw
  165  #         procedures now use single source for group ordering.
  166  #
  167  # v1.15: Bug #3 — 1-group faceted histogram forced through ungrouped
  168  #        path (hasGroups=0, displayMode=1) to prevent viewport mismatch.
  169  #        Bug #11 — semitone auto-range minimum span enforcement now uses
  170  #        direct 6-st rounding instead of re-calling @emlComputeAxisRange.
  171  #        Prevents overshoot to ~30 st for constant-pitch input.
  172  #
  173  # v1.12: @emlDrawTimeSeriesCI: CI critical value changed from hardcoded
  174  #         1.96 (normal approximation) to invStudentQ(annotAlpha/2, n-1)
  175  #         for correct t-distribution intervals at any sample size and
  176  #         confidence level. Reads global annotAlpha (default 0.05 = 95%
  177  #         CI). At n=5 and α=0.05, gives t=2.776 vs the old z=1.96.
  178  # v1.11: Faceted histogram group labels moved from right margin to left
  179  #         margin (Text left:, rotated 90°). Truncation with binary search
  180  #         when label physical width exceeds 85% of panel height. Right
  181  #         standard margin (not widened for labels).
  182  # v1.10: @emlDrawBarChart refactored to pure renderer — all data extraction
  183  #         (unique groups, aggregation, SE/SD/custom error computation) removed.
  184  #         Now reads pre-computed emlBarData_* globals from @emlMeasureBarData
  185  #         (eml-graph-procedures.praat). Signature unchanged for dispatch
  186  #         uniformity.
  187  # v1.9: @emlDrawBarChart signature change — .errorCol$ replaced by
  188  #         .errorMode (0=none, 1=SE, 2=SD, 3=custom) + .errorCol$ (for
  189  #         custom only). SE and SD computed internally from value column
  190  #         per group using single-pass variance (sum of squares). Error
  191  #         bar drawing condition uses .errorMode > 0 instead of .hasError.
  192  # v1.8: Per-axis visibility — all 8 direct axis label draws (Text left/
  193  #        Text bottom) gated by emlShowAxisNameY/emlShowAxisNameX globals.
  194  #        Axis label sanitization removed from all 6 draw procedures —
  195  #        auto-generated labels sanitized at source (@emlCapitalizeLabel),
  196  #        user-typed labels pass through raw to support Praat formatting
  197  #        codes. Title sanitization preserved. Group label sanitization
  198  #        preserved. Scatter alpha sprites always on when available —
  199  #        removed group-column condition gate so ungrouped scatters show
  200  #        density transparency.
  201  # v1.6: Faceted histogram x-axis ticks — replaced Marks bottom: with
  202  #        @emlDrawAlignedMarksBottom for nice-number alignment consistent
  203  #        with gridlines and all other axis tick drawing.
  204  # v1.5: All 12 hardcoded "Helvetica" in Text special: calls replaced
  205  #        with emlFont$ global variable (set by main script font dropdown).
  206  # v1.4: Debug lines removed. All 7 legend callers updated to
  207  #        @emlPlaceElements (adaptive corner selection). Font state
  208  #        fixes: bodySize before all Select inner viewport calls.
  209  #        Faceted histogram restructured: symmetric right margin,
  210  #        gridlines inside branch, group labels outside box.
  211  # v1.3: All Draw inner box calls replaced with @emlDrawInnerBoxIf (font
  212  #        state assertion + boolean toggle). Gridline dispatch updated:
  213  #        continuous types now 4-way (Both/Horizontal/Vertical/Off),
  214  #        categorical types now 2-way (Horizontal/Off).
  215  #
  216  # v1.1: All 6 categorical label sections now use @emlFitCategoricalLabels
  217  #        for space-aware rotation + truncation. Faceted histogram uses
  218  #        per-facet tick count from panel height instead of full canvas.
  219  # v1.0: Extracted from eml-graphs.praat v2.28 to enable shared use by
  220  #        both the graph UI (eml-graphs.praat) and stats wrapper scripts.
  221  #        Contains the drawing procedures and no standalone executable code.
  222  #        The count stated here was 14 and was never updated when
  223  #        @emlDrawLMMForest was added; it is 15 now. See Procedures
  224  #        below for the live count and the rule that derives it.

## plugin/graphs/eml-graph-procedures.praat @ 49ce40b

Lines 10-401 of plugin/graphs/eml-graph-procedures.praat at 49ce40b.

```
   10  # v3.32: Implausible-measurement warnings live with the batch analyser:
   11  #        scripts/eml-batch-process.praat carries a plausibility band per
   12  #        measure and is the code that ships that behaviour. No other line of
   13  #        code in this file changed.
   14  #
   15  # v3.31: Three changes, none of which moves a number.
   16  #
   17  #        THE Y-AXIS NAME AND ITS TICK LABELS DO NOT TOUCH.
   18  #        Praat's left-margin allocation is fixed: `Text left` anchors the
   19  #        rotated name to the inner frame at a distance set by the font size
   20  #        and by nothing else, tick numbers are right-aligned to that same
   21  #        frame, and about five characters fit between them. Six is the
   22  #        failure edge — a semitone axis reading "-33.08" leaves three pixels
   23  #        of gap at 300 dpi, and an explicit two-decimal dB axis puts "100.10"
   24  #        into "Power (dB)" so that it reads "Powe100.10".
   25  #        @emlTickLabelWidth (what a tick will read as, and how wide) and
   26  #        @emlDrawAxisNameLeft (the name, moved only when the labels have
   27  #        taken the room). @emlDrawAlignedMarksLeft publishes the widest
   28  #        label it drew; @emlDrawAxes and @emlDrawAxesSelective place the
   29  #        name through @emlDrawAxisNameLeft. WIDENING THE PLUGIN'S OWN MARGIN
   30  #        DOES NOT WORK and was measured not working — everything in the
   31  #        margin is anchored to the frame, and the frame moves with the
   32  #        margin. ORDINARY FIGURES ARE BYTE-IDENTICAL: all 39 of
   33  #        harness/stress_graphs.sh re-rendered and compared.
   34  #
   35  #        Column_k HOLDS SOURCE COLUMN k. @emlCleanConvertedTable numbers
   36  #        manufactured headers by SOURCE position rather than table position,
   37  #        because `To Table: "row"` has already put the label column in
   38  #        position 1 by then. Reached by both the graphs coercion and the
   39  #        describe wrapper.
   40  #
   41  #        ONE PRESS, ONE DERIVED SOUND. The stereo gate keeps the extracted
   42  #        channel Sound on purpose. The derived object is named with an "eml_"
   43  #        prefix, and @emlDropStaleChannelSounds collects the previous press's
   44  #        at the top of the next one, so three figures from one recording do
   45  #        not leave three Sounds sharing one name. Only ever a prefixed name:
   46  #        a channel the user extracted by hand is never touched.
   47  #
   48  # v3.30: THE LEGEND BOX IS SEMI-TRANSPARENT ON EVERY PLATFORM, NOT JUST THE
   49  #        TWO THAT HAVE ALPHA.
   50  #
   51  #        The legend's background is sprites/bg_white_a70_40.png, whose pixels
   52  #        MEASURE RGBA (255, 255, 255, 179) — a 70.2% white wash. Praat can
   53  #        only draw it on macOS and Windows, and @emlInitAlphaSprites is
   54  #        correctly gated to those two: re-confirmed 9 Aug 2026 by rendering
   55  #        the same script with and without `Insert picture from file:` on this
   56  #        Linux build, where `compare -metric AE` scores the two PNGs
   57  #        BYTE-IDENTICAL. The only other fallback available is
   58  #        `Paint rectangle: "White"` — an OPAQUE box, which deletes the data
   59  #        under the legend for every reader outside those two platforms.
   60  #
   61  #        SCREEN-DOOR TRANSPARENCY, with @emlScreenSetup, @emlPaintScreenRect
   62  #        and @emlPaintAlphaBox. Where there is no alpha the box is a white
   63  #        LATTICE at 0.027 inch pitch — 70% ink, 30% holes — through which the
   64  #        data survives instead of being erased. Measured on rendered pixels:
   65  #        ink 0.740 at 300 dpi and 0.672 at 600, against the sprite's 0.702.
   66  #        The section header carries the two rejected designs (a 45-degree
   67  #        hatch and a hex dot field), the measurements that rejected them, and
   68  #        the fact underneath all three: PRAAT DOES NOT ANTIALIAS AND ROUNDS
   69  #        RECTANGLES OUTWARD BY ABOUT ONE DEVICE PIXEL, which is why the
   70  #        geometry carries a kerf.
   71  #
   72  #        Nothing changes on macOS or Windows. @emlPaintAlphaBox's sprite
   73  #        branch issues the identical `Insert picture from file:` call with the
   74  #        identical arguments and returns .viewportDirty = 1, the viewport
   75  #        restore v3.28 added, moved verbatim behind a flag.
   76  #
   77  #        NOT SILENT, either way. @emlPaintAlphaBox publishes emlAlphaBgMode$
   78  #        ("sprite" | "screen" | "opaque") for every box it paints, and puts
   79  #        one NOTE in the Info window per session the first time a figure gets
   80  #        the screen. The sprite path stays quiet: on the platforms where the
   81  #        figure is already right, a note about it is noise.
   82  #
   83  #        NOT APPLIED to @emlDrawAnnotationBlock's background, which has the
   84  #        same gate and the same opaque fallback in
   85  #        eml-annotation-procedures.praat. @emlPaintAlphaBox is written to be
   86  #        called from there unchanged — it takes world coordinates and scales
   87  #        through emlPatWorldPerInchX/Y, which that procedure's callers set —
   88  #        but that file is not this one's to edit.
   89  #
   90  # v3.29: THE LEGEND BAND CLEARS THE COMPARISON MATRIX WHETHER OR NOT THE
   91  #        CALLER IS THE FORM.
   92  #
   93  #        Placement 3 (Below plot) has to know what is already occupying the
   94  #        page under the plot. Inside the graphs form the global
   95  #        totalCanvasHeight says so — the form sizes the comparison-matrix
   96  #        panel before it dispatches the draw and leaves
   97  #        figure_height + matrixGap + matrixPanelHeight there — and rendering
   98  #        the two together confirms it: on a 6 x 4 figure with a four-group
   99  #        Tukey matrix the panel occupies 4.130 to 6.204 inches and the legend
  100  #        band starts at 6.344, one boxInsetInches below it.
  101  #
  102  #        BUT totalCanvasHeight IS A FORM LOCAL. @emlInitDrawingDefaults, the
  103  #        documented entry point for "standalone scripts or PraatGen companion
  104  #        files", sets emlLegendPlacement and does NOT set totalCanvasHeight,
  105  #        so a caller outside the form that laid out its own matrix would get
  106  #        a band starting at the plot's own bottom edge and drawn straight
  107  #        THROUGH the panel — band 4.140 to 4.566 against a panel at 4.130 to
  108  #        6.204, 11636 dark pixels of legend ink inside the matrix band.
  109  #
  110  #        So the page bottom is settled ONCE, before the placement branch
  111  #        dispatches, from the larger of two sources: the form's
  112  #        totalCanvasHeight, and the matrix's own published measurement
  113  #        (annotMatrixN, emlMatrixLayout_suppressed, emlMatrixLayout_yMax,
  114  #        emlFitCategoricalLabels.overhangInches — all drawing-layer globals,
  115  #        and all necessarily already set, since @emlMeasureMatrixLayout is a
  116  #        precondition of @emlDrawMatrixPanel). Neither source can pull the
  117  #        band up, and a form-driven figure is unchanged because the two agree
  118  #        there. Placement 4's park reads the same page bottom, for the same
  119  #        reason.
  120  #
  121  #        Placements 1, 2 and 5 do not consult the page bottom, and a figure
  122  #        with no matrix publishes no measurement, so every render in
  123  #        harness/legend/ block 1 and block 2 is bit-identical to v3.28's.
  124  #        Driven and measured on the pixels in harness/legend/run.sh blocks 3
  125  #        and 4; asserted in validate/v32_legend_geometry.R sections 8 and 9.
  126  #
  127  # v3.28: THE LEGEND STOPS BILLING ITS RENT TO THE PLOT.
  128  #
  129  #        A user types 6 x 4 and means the DATA AREA. A legend that can only
  130  #        go inside the plot can only be given room by taking room from the
  131  #        data, and "make my figure square" is then unsatisfiable — a square
  132  #        canvas with a legend carved out of it is not a square plot. So the
  133  #        legend moves OUT and the EXPORT grows, rather than the plot
  134  #        shrinking IN. The plot rectangle is identical in all five
  135  #        placements; what changes is how much of the picture is saved.
  136  #
  137  #        NEW: @emlDrawLegendPanel and @emlMeasureLegendPanel, a renderer that
  138  #        is handed a rectangle in inches and a measurer that reports what
  139  #        would fit in one. Both work in a viewport whose world unit is one
  140  #        inch, both set the font size BEFORE selecting it, and the renderer
  141  #        lays itself out by calling the measurer, so the two cannot disagree.
  142  #        The renderer MUST NOT draw outside its rectangle and MUST NOT call
  143  #        @emlExpandDrawnExtent — the caller reports, which is what lets one
  144  #        procedure serve a corner box (must not grow the export) and a side
  145  #        panel (must). Also new: @emlEllipsizeToWidth.
  146  #
  147  #        @emlDrawLegend keeps its signature and every behaviour of an entry —
  148  #        multi-column layout, "+N more" truncation with its Info-window NOTE,
  149  #        the swatch drawn as the mark, the viewport re-selected at legend
  150  #        font size — and becomes a placement dispatcher over the new panel.
  151  #        Placement is the global emlLegendPlacement, read through
  152  #        variableExists and defaulting to 1, so a caller that sets nothing
  153  #        (the seven sites in eml-draw-procedures.praat, every stress case,
  154  #        every PraatGen companion) draws the corner box.
  155  #            1 Inside plot (DEFAULT) — export = plot rectangle
  156  #            2 Right of plot         — caller reports; export widens
  157  #            3 Below plot            — caller reports; export heightens
  158  #            4 Separate figure       — parked off-figure, saved as a 2nd file
  159  #            5 None
  160  #        The encoding, its registry and its dialog are in eml-graphs-form.
  161  #
  162  #        A LABEL WIDER THAN ONE COLUMN IS ELLIPSIZED, NOT OVERHUNG. A legend
  163  #        that computes its own box has nothing to clamp against; a legend
  164  #        that is GIVEN its bounds does. When one column of full-width labels
  165  #        will not fit the frame width, the column is clamped to the width
  166  #        that IS available and @emlEllipsizeToWidth shortens each label to
  167  #        it, so the label comes out as "Extremely long gro..." inside the box
  168  #        instead of running off the canvas — and a NOTE says so, because a
  169  #        silent ellipsis is the same defect as a silent truncation.
  170  #
  171  #        @emlMeasureGraphLayout's legend estimate delegates to
  172  #        @emlMeasureLegendPanel rather than carrying a single-column stack
  173  #        measured at bodySize. The legend is drawn at annotSize at all seven
  174  #        call sites, and measuring at the wrong size inflates every width —
  175  #        0.4967" vs 3.6229" for "Group label", measured 8 Aug 2026 on Praat
  176  #        6.6.30. New alongside the two existing globals:
  177  #        emlLayout_legendCols / Rows / Fits.
  178  #
  179  #        The alpha-sprite legend background calls `Insert picture from file:`,
  180  #        which leaves the viewport set to the image's bounding box, so the
  181  #        viewport is restored immediately rather than at the end of the
  182  #        procedure. Not observable on Linux, where @emlInitAlphaSprites is
  183  #        gated off.
  184  #
  185  # v3.27: Comments only — no executable line changed.
  186  #
  187  # v3.26: THE LEGEND IS LAID OUT TO FIT THE FRAME. The palette holds 24
  188  #        sub-group styles, so 24 entries is a figure the plugin is built to
  189  #        draw: at the annotation font on a 6 x 4 figure a row is 0.162", the
  190  #        panel is 3.11" tall, and 24 rows want 3.94". A stack of one row per
  191  #        entry, measured against nothing, overhangs the frame by 304 px at
  192  #        300 dpi and runs its last entries clean off the bottom of the PNG.
  193  #        This is a LAYOUT, not a cap: the frame is measured (rowsMax x
  194  #        colsMax = capacity) and the entries are poured down the fewest
  195  #        columns that fit the height, so 24 entries become two columns of 12
  196  #        on a 6 x 4 figure and three of 8 on a 10 x 3 one. Hue order runs
  197  #        top-to-bottom down each column. A legend that fits in one column
  198  #        gets the identical single-column geometry v3.25 drew, to the last
  199  #        decimal — verified by rendering: a 3-entry legend's pixels are
  200  #        unchanged. Only when capacity is still exceeded (a very short frame,
  201  #        or labels wide enough that one column is all that fits) is anything
  202  #        dropped, and then the last cell reads "+N more" ON THE FIGURE and a
  203  #        NOTE naming both counts goes to the Info window. Nothing is dropped
  204  #        in silence. Reports for callers and fixtures: .nCols, .rowsPerCol,
  205  #        .shown, .hidden, .capacity. A single label wider than the frame
  206  #        still overhangs to the right here; v3.28 closes that.
  207  #
  208  # v3.25: POINT MARKERS, and a greyscale ramp that uses the page.
  209  #        (1) MARKERS. The v3.24 fill pattern needs an AREA. Scatter, line
  210  #            chart, spaghetti and time series draw dots and lines, so their
  211  #            second dimension has to be the marker SHAPE. New @emlDrawMarker
  212  #            draws circle / square / triangle from NATIVE primitives (Paint
  213  #            circle, Paint rectangle, and a stack of horizontal slices for
  214  #            the triangle -- the same construction @emlDrawViolin uses for
  215  #            its body). New emlSetColorPalette.marker[] gives 8 hues x 3
  216  #            shapes = 24, the same arithmetic and the same cap as the fill
  217  #            patterns. @emlDrawLegend grew a marker key (legendMarkered,
  218  #            legendMarker[], legendMarkerLine) so the key shows the SHAPE and
  219  #            not merely the hue. Not sprites: plugin/sprites/ is real and has
  220  #            204 PNGs, but every one of them is a dot or a rectangle indexed
  221  #            by hue, and @emlInitAlphaSprites is disabled on anything that is
  222  #            not macOS or Windows because Praat has no cairo image branch.
  223  #            @emlDrawScatterPlot therefore drops the sprite path above eight
  224  #            groups, or on those two platforms the shape would never reach
  225  #            the page.
  226  #        (2) GREYSCALE. The v3.24 ramp ran 0.90 to 0.25, a span of 0.65, and
  227  #            what held it there was not the eye: the stroke was derived as
  228  #            fill - 0.30 and clamped at zero, so a fill below 0.30 collapses
  229  #            the stroke ramp -- at 0.25 the two darkest strokes are 0.043 and
  230  #            0.000 apart, which is a greyscale LINE chart with two
  231  #            indistinguishable series at eight groups. The fill and stroke
  232  #            ramps are independent: fills 0.94 to 0.10 (a span of 0.84) and
  233  #            strokes 0.63 to 0.00. New @emlMarkInk pays the cost -- a mark's
  234  #            outline, median, whiskers and pattern flip to white on a dark
  235  #            fill and black on a light one, per channel so that Okabe-Ito
  236  #            yellow (whose contrast is all in the blue channel) is not
  237  #            misjudged. New @emlParseRGB underneath it. Measured minimum
  238  #            greyscale separation on the rendered pixels: 0.1176, against
  239  #            0.090 for the derived ramp.
  240  #
  241  # v3.24: FILL PATTERNS — the palette has eight colours, not ten, and has a
  242  #        second dimension. Ten declared fill/line pairs whose slots 9 and 10
  243  #        are LITERAL DUPLICATES of 1 and 2 draw two pairs of sub-groups in
  244  #        indistinguishable colours, on the figure and in the legend, with no
  245  #        disclosure; the cycling rule above ten was `mod 8`, which says the
  246  #        palette was always eight. So the eight hues are declared once and a
  247  #        fill-pattern axis is added: 8 hues x 3 patterns (solid, diagonal
  248  #        hatch, dots) = 24 distinguishable styles, hue cycling first so
  249  #        adjacent sub-groups differ in the stronger cue. @emlDrawViolin and
  250  #        @emlDrawBox take a .pattern argument and clip the pattern to the
  251  #        mark using the scanline structure they already build; @emlDrawLegend
  252  #        draws the swatch as the mark rather than as a block of colour, so
  253  #        the key cannot say "solid" where the figure says "hatched". The B/W
  254  #        branch's ten fills, which sat between 0.82 and 0.96, are replaced by
  255  #        the eight-step 0.90-0.25 ramp @emlOptimizePaletteContrast was
  256  #        already computing, so greyscale reaches the same 24.
  257  #        @emlDrawLegend also re-selects the viewport at the legend's own font
  258  #        size before measuring and drawing. Praat maps world coordinates back
  259  #        through the font size in force at the time of the call, so a legend
  260  #        measured at one size inside a viewport selected at another is
  261  #        rendered into a rectangle that is not the .boxLeft/.boxTop it
  262  #        reports to its caller.
  263  #
  264  # v3.23: @emlMeasureBarData: zero is not "no data". emlBarData_mean[g] is
  265  #        UNDEFINED when the group has no usable observation, and
  266  #        emlBarData_error[g] UNDEFINED when there is no error to compute,
  267  #        rather than both being seeded to 0. Seeded at 0, @emlDrawBarChart's
  268  #        "<> undefined" guards for the bar and the whisker can never fire, so
  269  #        a group with nothing in it draws as a bar of height zero —
  270  #        indistinguishable from a measured zero — and an undefined error bar
  271  #        vanishes in silence. Both are suppressed by the existing guards and
  272  #        disclosed. The visible-range fold at the end of the procedure
  273  #        substitutes 0 for an undefined error so the axis range is
  274  #        unaffected. See the invariant block on @emlMeasureBarData.
  275  #
  276  # v3.22: Undefined/validation hardening.
  277  #        (1) @emlCheckNumericColumn scans the whole column (capped at
  278  #            .maxScanRows) rather than sampling the first 5 rows, and
  279  #            requires EVERY non-empty cell to be a strict numeric literal
  280  #            rather than passing a column on a single parseable cell. It
  281  #            treats empty/whitespace cells as missing rather than failures,
  282  #            and reports .nNumeric/.nMissing/.nCoerced/.nNonNumeric plus the
  283  #            first offending row and its literal text. Cells that only pass
  284  #            via number() coercion ("5,5", "30%", "1/2", "0x10", "2 3") are
  285  #            classified as coerced and REJECTED — a silent wrong number is
  286  #            worse than a rejected column. Parameter list and .isNumeric
  287  #            output name/meaning are unchanged.
  288  #        (2) @emlMeasureBarData handles undefined values. Undefined
  289  #            observations and undefined custom-error values are skipped and
  290  #            counted; means, SE/SD and the visible min/max fold are guarded
  291  #            with explicit "<> undefined" tests; single-observation groups are
  292  #            handled explicitly (no undefined error bar). New disclosure
  293  #            globals: emlBarData_valid[], emlBarData_errorDefined[],
  294  #            emlBarData_skipped[], emlBarData_errSkipped[],
  295  #            emlBarData_nSkipped, emlBarData_nErrSkipped,
  296  #            emlBarData_nInvalidGroups, emlBarData_nSingleObs,
  297  #            emlBarData_nUnmatchedRows. (v3.23 restores undefined as the "no
  298  #            measurement" sentinel and puts the burden of guarding back on
  299  #            the callers.)
  300  #        (3) The same two patterns swept through the file:
  301  #            @emlComputeAxisRange, @emlComputeNiceStep,
  302  #            @emlSetAlphaDotGeometry (relational "guards" that are FALSE for
  303  #            undefined and therefore let undefined through to Axes:/
  304  #            Paint circle:), and @emlDrawViolin, @emlDrawBox,
  305  #            @emlDrawJitteredPoints (undefined observations reaching
  306  #            Draw line:/Paint rectangle:, or silently suppressing the whole
  307  #            glyph).
  308  # v3.21: Graph correctness. (1) Box/violin quartiles use the shared R-7
  309  #        interpolated @emlQuartiles rather than nearest-rank floor(n*p),
  310  #        which biases the median low and collapses it onto the minimum at
  311  #        small n; the figure agrees with the describe table. (2) Bar
  312  #        auto-range tracks a data minimum (emlBarData_visibleMin) so
  313  #        all-negative means are not clipped at 0. (3) The B/W palette
  314  #        recomputes sprite$ greys so alpha dots/overlap match the fill/line.
  315  #        shared R-7 interpolated @emlQuartiles instead of nearest-rank
  316  #        floor(n*p), which biased the median low and collapsed it onto the
  317  #        minimum at small n (figure now agrees with the describe table).
  318  #        (2) Bar auto-range tracks a data minimum (emlBarData_visibleMin) so
  319  #        all-negative means are not clipped at 0. (3) The B/W palette
  320  #        recomputes sprite$ greys so alpha dots/overlap match the fill/line.
  321  # v3.20: Group sort unification — @emlExtractUniqueValues and
  322  #         @emlMeasureBarData now call @emlCountGroups instead of inline
  323  #         discovery. All group ordering flows through single source.
  324  #
  325  # v3.18: Stereo channel handling — @emlCheckChannels presents a user dialog
  326  #         via @emlHandleStereo rather than converting to mono silently. New @emlApplyChannelChoice for batch scripts
  327  #         (applies pre-selected channel handling without dialog).
  328  #         Three-procedure architecture: @emlApplyChannelChoice (mechanical
  329  #         core), @emlHandleStereo (single-file UI wrapper),
  330  #         @emlCheckChannels (backward-compat thin wrapper).
  331  # v3.17: New @emlExpandDrawnExtent procedure — single source of truth
  332  #         for extent tracker bounding box expansion. Replaces inline
  333  #         pattern in @emlSetAdaptiveTheme; also called by
  334  #         @emlDrawMatrixPanel (eml-annotation-procedures.praat) to
  335  #         register the matrix panel viewport with the extent tracker.
  336  # v3.16: New @emlMeasureBarData procedure — extracts and aggregates bar
  337  #         chart data (unique groups, per-group means, SE/SD/custom errors,
  338  #         visible max) into emlBarData_* globals. Called once from
  339  #         pre-dispatch; results read by both headroom computation and
  340  #         @emlDrawBarChart. Eliminates DRY violation between pre-dispatch
  341  #         aggregation and draw procedure aggregation.
  342  # v3.15: Independent per-axis control — emlShowTicks/emlShowAxisValues
  343  #         split to emlShowTicksX/Y and emlShowAxisValuesX/Y. New
  344  #         emlShowAxisNameX/Y globals gate axis label drawing. New
  345  #         @emlExpandAxisControls procedure: 3 dropdown indices → 6
  346  #         per-axis booleans. All 3 aligned mark procedures,
  347  #         @emlDrawCategoricalXAxis, @emlDrawAxes, and
  348  #         @emlDrawAxesSelective updated. Categorical group names
  349  #         always visible regardless of X tick/value settings.
  350  #         Sanitization moved to source: @emlCapitalizeLabel now calls
  351  #         @emlSanitizeLabel (auto-generated labels). @emlDrawAxes and
  352  #         @emlDrawAxesSelective do not sanitize axis labels — user-typed
  353  #         labels with Praat formatting codes (%italic, #bold, ^super,
  354  #         _sub) pass through raw.
  355  #         New .tickColor$ ("{0.35, 0.35, 0.35}") in @emlSetAdaptiveTheme
  356  #         — tick value numbers draw slightly muted vs axis name labels
  357  #         (textColor$). Applied in all 3 aligned mark procedures.
  358  # v3.14: New @emlDrawTitle procedure — centralizes title/subtitle drawing
  359  #         via Text special with 1:1 inch coordinate mapping. Title/subtitle
  360  #         anchored upward from inner box top, horizontally centered on inner
  361  #         box. Replaces 7 inline title blocks (1 in @emlDrawAxes, 6 in
  362  #         categorical procs via eml-draw-procedures). Clipping guard for
  363  #         small viewports.
  364  #         @emlSetAdaptiveTheme: symmetric margins (.marginRight = .marginLeft).
  365  #         New .bodyInch, .titleInch outputs (typography in inches). New
  366  #         .boxInsetInches output — unified physical inset for legend,
  367  #         annotation block, and comparison matrix boxes.
  368  #         @emlDrawLegend insetX/Y now use boxInsetInches for equal padding.
  369  # v3.13: @emlDrawLegend — colored line samples replaced with filled
  370  #         square swatches; legend text labels now use axis text color
  371  #         (textColor$) instead of group color. @emlDrawAxes title
  372  #         centering — asserts full outer viewport before title/subtitle
  373  #         so text centers on figure, not inner box. Subtitle support
  374  #         (global emlSubtitle$, drawn at bodySize in grey via Text top:
  375  #         "no"). Content-driven marginTop — computed from title/subtitle
  376  #         typography instead of viewport height cap.
  377  # v3.12: New @emlDrawAlignedMarksBottom procedure — mirrors Left/Right
  378  #         for bottom x-axis ticks. @emlDrawAxes and @emlDrawAxesSelective
  379  #         refactored to call aligned mark procedures (Left/Bottom) instead
  380  #         of inline loops — DRY, consistent guard logic. Show axis values
  381  #         toggle (emlShowAxisValues) added: all 3 aligned mark procedures
  382  #         now independently control writeNumber (emlShowAxisValues) and
  383  #         drawTick (emlShowTicks). @emlDrawAxesSelective per-axis params
  384  #         (.showXTicks/.showYTicks) remain as hard overrides wrapping the
  385  #         procedure calls.
  386  # v3.11: @emlSetAdaptiveTheme — bare Helvetica replaced with
  387  #         'emlFont$' global variable (set by main script font dropdown).
  388  #         Theme exposes .font$ output for documentation.
  389  # v3.10: @emlDrawLegend font restore moved before Select inner
  390  #         viewport (was after — caused margin mismatch). Show ticks
  391  #         toggle (emlShowTicks) guards added to @emlDrawAxes,
  392  #         @emlDrawAxesSelective, @emlDrawAlignedMarksLeft/Right.
  393  # v3.9: @emlDrawInnerBoxIf — wrapper for Draw inner box with boolean toggle
  394  #        and font size assertion (keeps ticks from being displaced).
  395  #        @emlDrawVerticalGridlines — vertical-only gridlines for continuous
  396  #        axes. @emlDrawLegend now restores Font size: bodySize at end
  397  #        (font state management layer 1).
  398  #
  399  # v3.7: New @emlFitCategoricalLabels procedure — space-aware x-axis labels
  400  #        with automatic truncation via Text width (world coordinates) when
  401  #        labels exceed available slot width. Used by all categorical draw procs.
```

## plugin/graphs/eml-graphs-form.praat @ 49ce40b

Lines 7-153 of plugin/graphs/eml-graphs-form.praat at 49ce40b.

```
    7  # v2.9: The post-draw dialog is three fixed buttons -- Done | Save | Redraw --
    8  #       and Save opens @emlSavePanel, which offers whichever outputs exist.
    9  #       The button count does not vary with what is available, so every
   10  #       keyboard walk of this dialog is the same walk.
   11  # v2.8: The post-draw Save path exports through @emlExportResultFiles, the
   12  #       shared migration fork, so an analysis saved from a figure lands in the
   13  #       same shape the stats menu writes it in. @emlReportToFile uniques the
   14  #       legacy file itself and the declared arm does not unique, which is the
   15  #       behaviour @emlWrapperExportCSV has.
   16  # v2.7: A WRAPPER'S ANNOTATE PRESET SURVIVES A BEGINNER DRAW. Beginner mode
   17  #       draws only what its own dialog offers, so the beginner commit sets
   18  #       annotate = 0. The preset is consumed once BEFORE the outer repeat, so
   19  #       Redraw does not re-apply it, and prev_adv_<type>_annotate is written
   20  #       in exactly one place -- the toggle to beginner -- which a preset never
   21  #       passes through. All six annotate-capable restore branches therefore
   22  #       fall back to the live preset when there is no saved advanced state,
   23  #       and carry the preset's test type into the dialog index with it. Same
   24  #       shape as v1.6's Item 22, which preserves annotTestType$ and
   25  #       annotStyle$ across a beginner Draw.
   26  # v2.6: A LEGEND INSIDE THE PLOT GETS Y-AXIS ROOM MADE FOR IT. Any extra room
   27  #       a figure needs is a property of what is drawn on it, not of the unit,
   28  #       and is supplied by @emlComputeAnnotationHeadroom at the annotation
   29  #       stage. A legend at placement 1 sits on the data area, so it earns the
   30  #       same treatment a significance bracket does. The six types that draw a
   31  #       legend are 5, 8, 10, 11, 12 and 13.
   32  #
   33  #       * TWO PASSES. The figure is drawn, the axis it resolved and the
   34  #         corner @emlPlaceElements sent the legend to are read back from the
   35  #         draw procedure, and if the legend needs room the figure is erased
   36  #         and drawn again on the widened axis. Both facts are decided inside
   37  #         the draw procedure and neither exists before it runs. The
   38  #         alternative — this file re-deriving every legend-bearing type's
   39  #         auto-range with its own copy of that type's arithmetic — would
   40  #         change the axis of every figure whether or not it had a legend the
   41  #         first time a copy drifted by one rounding step.
   42  #       * @emlGraphsDispatchDraw (the DISPATCH block, at file scope so it can
   43  #         be called twice) and @emlGraphsDrawWithLegendRoom (the loop), both
   44  #         at file scope so a probe can drive the real code rather than a
   45  #         transcription of it.
   46  #       * @emlLegendHeadroomAfterDraw — the decision. Only placement 1
   47  #         (Inside plot) earns an axis change: 2 and 3 grow the saved image
   48  #         around an unchanged plot, 4 writes a second file, 5 draws nothing,
   49  #         and none of them takes a square inch of the data area.
   50  #       * NOTHING THAT CANNOT BE AFFORDED IS SILENT. A legend band capped at
   51  #         emlLegendHeadroomShare of the panel, a bottom-corner legend on a
   52  #         histogram (whose frequency axis has a hard zero floor), a
   53  #         user-typed axis that had to be widened, and the discarded first
   54  #         pass itself are each named in the Info window.
   55  # v2.5: LEGEND PLACEMENT. The user's width and height describe the PLOT, and
   56  #       a legend has four places to go besides the inside of it, so giving a
   57  #       legend room does not mean taking room from the data and "make my
   58  #       figure square" is satisfiable. Shared setting, one canonical encoding
   59  #       for every graph type:
   60  #
   61  #           config_legendPlacement
   62  #             1 Inside plot (DEFAULT — auto-corner)
   63  #             2 Right of plot      3 Below plot
   64  #             4 Separate figure    5 None
   65  #
   66  #       THE PLOT IS THE SAME SIZE IN ALL FIVE. Placements 2 and 3 grow the
   67  #       SAVED IMAGE around an unchanged plot rectangle; placement 4 writes
   68  #       the legend as a second file. figure_width and figure_height are not
   69  #       adjusted anywhere for a legend, which is the whole point. The
   70  #       geometry lives in @emlDrawLegend / @emlDrawLegendPanel in
   71  #       eml-graph-procedures.praat v3.28; this file owns the encoding, the
   72  #       registry, the dialog and the second file.
   73  #
   74  #       * REGISTRY. legendPlacementStyle[1..nGraphTypes], 5 = offers the
   75  #         menu / 0 = this type has no legend, beside gridModeStyle[] and in
   76  #         the same shape for the same reason (see the block there: two
   77  #         encodings sharing one persisted key seed an optionmenu with an
   78  #         out-of-range index, which Praat draws blank and then refuses to
   79  #         close, and the bad value is on disk so it survives a restart). Six
   80  #         types draw a legend — 5, 8, 10, 11, 12, 13, which is exactly the
   81  #         set whose draw procedures call @emlDrawLegend. A missing or
   82  #         out-of-range entry fails at INCLUDE time from a file-scope loop,
   83  #         and @emlLegendPlacementStyle refuses an unregistered type by name
   84  #         at runtime. Adding a graph type without a legend entry cannot ship.
   85  #       * ENCODING. @emlLegendPlacementToMenu / @emlLegendPlacementFromMenu /
   86  #         @emlSeedLegendPlacement / @emlCommitLegendPlacement, mirroring the
   87  #         gridline set. There is only ONE encoding, so the translations
   88  #         are identities; they exist so that a second one, if it is ever
   89  #         needed, has one place to go instead of fourteen. The clamp is at
   90  #         @emlLoadConfig, where the file is read, so an out-of-range value in
   91  #         a hand-edited config becomes the default rather than a blank
   92  #         dropdown and a form that refuses to close.
   93  #       * DIALOG. "Legend placement" optionmenu in the Advanced block of the
   94  #         six legend-capable pages only, seeded and committed through the two
   95  #         single-token procedures.
   96  #       * SECOND FILE. Placement 4's legend is drawn on a parked patch of the
   97  #         picture and deliberately not reported to @emlExpandDrawnExtent, so
   98  #         the figure still saves at its own extent; the Save branch then
   99  #         selects the parked rectangle and writes <name>_legend.png beside
  100  #         <name>.png — same folder, same base name, same DPI, same
  101  #         non-destructive @emlGenerateUniquePath. No new path convention.
  102  #         It inherits the figure's annotSize rather than recomputing a size
  103  #         from the legend canvas, so the two files' text matches when they
  104  #         are placed side by side in a manuscript.
  105  # v2.4: The graph type registry declares 13 types, with Spaghetti Plot at 13.
  106  #       nGraphTypes, all six parallel registry arrays, menuToType[16] and
  107  #       typeToMenu agree on that count. Time series with a confidence interval
  108  #       is a "Show confidence interval" toggle on type 5 (Line Chart), and
  109  #       @emlDrawTimeSeriesCI is dispatched from there.
  110  #       CALLERS: a script setting emlGraphsPresetType for a spaghetti plot
  111  #       sets 13.
  112  # v2.3: * The Draw path's Export Results dialog proposes a name read off the
  113  #         CSV buffer that is about to be written -- @emlGraphsCSVDefaultName /
  114  #         @emlGraphsCSVRowAnalysis -- giving the same <table>_<analysis-slug>
  115  #         shape as @emlWrapperExportCSV, so the filename describes the bytes
  116  #         in the file rather than whatever object the figure was drawn from.
  117  #       * emlGraphsPresetSubgroupCol$ lets a wrapper name its actual second
  118  #         factor, consumed in the Grouped Violin and Grouped Box preset
  119  #         branches ahead of the role guesser. A preset that collides with
  120  #         Category or Value is demoted and the guesser runs instead.
  121  #       * @emlGraphsIsPercentageColumn, and the two @emlComputeAxisRange calls
  122  #         here pass it rather than a literal 0, so a percentage column gets a
  123  #         percentage axis. Detection needs BOTH a percentage-looking name and
  124  #         data inside 0-100.
  125  #       * The correction preset's string and its dialog index are both derived
  126  #         from one validated index via @emlAdjustMethodName, so they cannot
  127  #         disagree, and an unrecognised preset is reported where it was set.
  128  #         Consuming it on the parametric path is in
  129  #         eml-annotation-procedures.praat, not here.
  130  # v2.2: (a) The spaghetti-plot column-mapping dialog consumes
  131  #       emlGraphsPresetGroupCol$ through the spPresetHasGroup /
  132  #       spPresetGroupIdx sentinel, mirroring scatter and histogram, so a Draw
  133  #       from a stats wrapper carries the requested grouping. (b)
  134  #       emlGraphsPresetXCol$ and emlGraphsPresetYCol$ are cleared at workflow
  135  #       end rather than inside the scatter page, so they cannot leak into the
  136  #       next workflow call when the user chooses a non-scatter type.
  137  # v2.1: The histogram column-mapping dialog honours an incoming group-column
  138  #       preset: the histPresetHasGroup sentinel, mirroring scatterPresetHasGroup,
  139  #       ticks "Use group column" with the seeded index so histGroupCol$ commits
  140  #       and the grouped-histogram annotation route at the dispatch is reached.
  141  # v2.0: Multiple-comparison adjustment is user-facing rather than a hardcoded
  142  #       "holm". "Adjustment method" optionmenu (Bonferroni / Holm /
  143  #       Benjamini-Hochberg, default Holm) on the annotate-capable
  144  #       column-mapping dialogs, backed by the shared prev_annotAdjustIdx
  145  #       persistence variable and the @emlAdjustMethodName index-to-string
  146  #       helper. emlGraphsPresetCorrection$ carries a stats wrapper's own
  147  #       adjustment choice in.
  148  # v1.9: Bar-chart negative-mean support in the annotated (bracket) path —
  149  #       auto-range floors on emlBarData_visibleMin so all-/mixed-negative
  150  #       means are not clipped at 0 (positive data is unaffected via the axis
  151  #       procedure's non-negative guard). Companion to eml-graph-procedures
  152  #       v3.21 / eml-draw-procedures v1.18.
  153  # v1.8: Post-draw dialog "Back" renamed to "Done".
```

   12  # v1.3: The two APPENDIX_D §7 (hard) rules for bounded ranges.
   13  #       GUARD BOTH ENDS OF EVERY BOUNDED RANGE — the filtered-autocorrelation
   14  #       range, the raw-cross-correlation range and the 60-330 Hz cepstral peak
   15  #       search now each warn when a measured F0 comes within 10% of either
   16  #       limit, which is the difference between a censored number and a
   17  #       measured one and is invisible in the value itself. CHECK THE USER'S
   18  #       STATED RANGE AGAINST THE MEASUREMENT — highest expected F0 derives
   19  #       both pitch ranges, so a measurement above it is reported with the two
   20  #       parameters it set. Info-window output and the warning count only: no
   21  #       Table cell, no column and no exported value changes, and
   22  #       harness/batch's seven result CSVs are byte-identical across the edit.
   23  #       This is the module's first version to carry a registered menu entry
   24  #       (plugin/setup.praat v1.7) and a GUI drive of its real dialog
   25  #       (harness/batchgui). validate/v72_batch_registration.R.
```

## plugin/scripts/eml-check-normality.praat @ 49ce40b

```
    9  # v2.2: Per-group mode does not carry its own normality rule. It
   10  #        had hard-coded thresholds of 1 and 3 against the shared constants
   11  #        of 2 and 7, and the pre-5-August `skKurtFail or swFail` gate, while
   12  #        the SAME script's overall mode called @emlRunNormalityAnalysis,
   13  #        which had neither. One wrapper, two answers for the same data
   14  #        depending on whether a grouping column was picked: a group with
   15  #        |skew| >= 1 that Shapiro-Wilk did not reject came out nonparametric
   16  #        grouped and parametric ungrouped. Both modes now reach the one
   17  #        shared rule, @emlNormalityRecommendation (stats/eml-analysis.praat).
   18  #        This CHANGES per-group verdicts, which is the point.
   19  # v2.1: Draw button on the completion dialog — a normal Q-Q plot for ONE
   20  #        column at a time, behind an explicit column picker. The picker is
   21  #        not optional: this checker tests every numeric column in one run,
   22  #        so a figure drawn from an assumed column would be a silently wrong
   23  #        figure. In grouped mode the picker asks for the group as well,
   24  #        because in that mode a column is not what was tested — a column
   25  #        within a group is.
   26  # v2.0: Full convergence — @emlWrapperInit for Table check,
   27  #        @emlRunNormalityAnalysis orchestrator for overall mode (fixes
   28  #        double-report bug and wrong-type 4th parameter in v1.0).
   29  #        repeat/until replaces goto/label.
   30  # v1.0: Initial release.
```

## plugin/stats/eml-analysis.praat @ 49ce40b

Lines 8-61 of plugin/stats/eml-analysis.praat at 49ce40b.

```
    8  # v1.3: The normality decision rule lives in @emlNormalityRecommendation and
    9  #        is written down in exactly ONE place. This file, @wizardNormDiag
   10  #        (scripts/eml-wizard.praat) and the per-group branch of
   11  #        scripts/eml-check-normality.praat all call the one procedure, so
   12  #        three hand-maintained copies cannot disagree about thresholds or
   13  #        about the gate.
   14  #
   15  # v1.2: Correctness + robustness in the orchestrator layer.
   16  #   item 1 — emlRMPostHoc does not render a failed pairwise test as an
   17  #            adjusted p of 0. A test that fails (or returns an undefined p)
   18  #            propagates "undefined" into the raw p-vector, so the adjustment
   19  #            procedures exclude it from k the way R's p.adjust excludes NA.
   20  #            Undefined p-values print as "n/a" in both the raw and adjusted
   21  #            columns, and a note names each skipped pair with the reason.
   22  #   item 2 — emlRMPostHoc validates .adjMethod$, so an unrecognised string
   23  #            cannot fall through to Holm while the header prints the
   24  #            requested name. The header prints the method that ran and the
   25  #            substitution is disclosed.
   26  #   item 3 — emlRunCorrelationAnalysis captures each test's outputs into
   27  #            locals immediately after its own call and restores them before
   28  #            reporting, so a nested/re-entrant call cannot overwrite them.
   29  #            Previously "both" reported Spearman's rank-based r, t, df and p
   30  #            under the Pearson heading, in the Info report AND the CSV.
   31  #            The same capture/restore is applied to the Mann-Whitney outputs
   32  #            in emlRunTwoGroupAnalysis, which @emlRankBiserialR re-enters.
   33  #   item 4 — emlRunTwoGroupAnalysis checks the error$ of every test it runs.
   34  #            The reporter performs no error checks, so a failed test used to
   35  #            be printed as undefined results and written to the CSV. Failed
   36  #            branches are now dropped from the reported test type and the
   37  #            reason is surfaced. emlRunCorrelationAnalysis likewise surfaces
   38  #            a failed Spearman test, which the reporter swallowed silently.
   39  #   item 5 — emlReportPairwiseComparison header said "Pairwise holm (holm
   40  #            adjustment)": it used emlPairwiseT.method$, which is the
   41  #            ADJUSTMENT method, as the test name. Now derived from .test$.
   42  #   item 6 — emlRunRegressionAnalysis and emlRunNormalityAnalysis guard
   43  #            their column names with "Get column index:" instead of aborting
   44  #            the whole script with a raw Praat error.
   45  #   item 7 — Documented the reserved-but-unread parameters
   46  #            (emlRunNormalityAnalysis.testType$,
   47  #            emlRunRepeatedMeasuresAnalysis.subjectCol$,
   48  #            emlRunFriedmanAnalysis.subjectCol$) and the unimplemented
   49  #            emlRunReliabilityAnalysis stub. Parameter lists are unchanged
   50  #            because callers pass arguments positionally.
   51  #   item 8 — Friedman tie correction verified against R's friedman.test on
   52  #            tied data; formula and clamp are correct. No change.
   53  #
   54  # v1.1: Missing-data fix (correctness). emlRunPairedAnalysis and
   55  #        emlRunCorrelationAnalysis now extract their two columns with
   56  #        row-wise complete-case deletion (@emlExtractPairedColumns)
   57  #        instead of two independent per-column @emlExtractColumn calls,
   58  #        which misaligned the pairs when cells were missing in different
   59  #        rows. Analyzed n is now the complete-pair count and a
   60  #        "rows excluded for missing data" note is emitted when any row
   61  #        is dropped.
```

    8  # v1.2: Audit fixes (items 5, 6, 7).
    9  #        Item 5 — @emlShapiroWilk: the second pair-weight coefficient is
   10  #          now applied from n >= 6, not n >= 7, matching Royston AS R94 and
   11  #          R's swilk.c (which branches on n > 5). n = 6 was the only sample
   12  #          size in 3..12 that disagreed with R; it now agrees to 1e-6.
   13  #        Item 6 — @emlKurtosis returned a spurious finite value (e.g. -13.5
   14  #          at n = 4) when the standard deviation is zero; the standardised
   15  #          moment is 0/0 there. It now returns undefined with .error$ set.
   16  #          @emlSkewness had the analogous defect (returned 0) and is fixed
   17  #          the same way. Both procedures now expose .error$.
   18  #        Item 7 — Guards for undefined elements and out-of-range arguments.
   19  #          sort# raises a hard error on a vector containing undefined, which
   20  #          aborted the whole calling script from @emlPercentile,
   21  #          @emlTrimmedMean, @emlWinsorizedMean and @emlMAD. New helper
   22  #          @eml_hasUndefined lets those procedures return undefined instead.
   23  #          @emlCI now rejects confidenceLevel outside (0, 1): a level of 1
   24  #          made invStudentQ(0, df) loop forever.
   25  #
   26  # v1.1: Added @emlShapiroWilk (Royston 1995, AS R94) and helper
   27  #        @eml_swPoly. Shapiro-Wilk W test for normality, n = 3–5000.

    8  # v1.5: Numeric-column integrity and group-label normalisation.

   10  #        "Get all numbers in column:" will return VALUES and not Praat's
   11  #        alphabetical RANK substitution, and abort with a clear message
   12  #        when it would not. (2) @emlValidateNumericColumn hardened:
   13  #        per-cell check across every row, strict-numeric verdict, first
   14  #        offending row and its literal contents, and a coercion-hazard
   15  #        warning. It is now actually called (previously zero call sites).

   17  #        European decimal comma is listed first because it is the only
   18  #        one that yields a plausible wrong number rather than a dropped
   19  #        row. (4) @emlCountGroups normalises group labels (case and
   20  #        leading/trailing whitespace) and warns when normalisation
   21  #        merged distinct spellings; the same normalisation is applied

   21  # v1.3: Statistical-correctness fixes (audit items 1-13).
   22  #   item 1  - @emlSpearmanCorrelation does not clobber
   23  #             @emlPearsonCorrelation's outputs: the shared computation
   24  #             moved into the internal @eml_pearsonCore, and both public
   25  #             procedures are now thin wrappers over it.
   26  #   item 2  - @emlPearsonCorrelation: a perfect correlation
   27  #             (.rSquared >= 1) gave an undefined t with p = 0, which the
   28  #             report layer rendered as "p < .001". .p is now 0 with new
   29  #             .perfect and .warning$ outputs disclosing it; n < 3 and
   30  #             zero-variance inputs are also guarded.
   31  #   item 3  - @emlTukeyHSD does not fail open: an undefined q (zero SE
   32  #             or undefined MS-within) would report p = 1.000 for every
   33  #             comparison. Per-comparison p is now undefined, counted in
   34  #             .nUndefined and disclosed in .warning$.
   35  #   item 4  - @emlOneWayAnova guards the eta-squared denominator
   36  #             (zero/undefined SS-total) instead of reporting eta^2 = 1.
   37  #   item 5  - @emlTwoWayAnova: SS_Error and SS_Total are now computed
   38  #             from within-cell deviations rather than parsed from
   39  #             Praat's Type I report, F and p re-derived from the
   40  #             corrected MS_Error, design balance checked, negative SS
   41  #             clamped, and the SS type stated in the output header.
   42  #             (Type III effect SS were already correct; untouched.)
   43  #   item 6  - @eml_parseAnovaLine matched row labels by first substring
   44  #             occurrence, so it returned factor1's row when factor2's
   45  #             name was a substring of it (and Error/Total collided the
   46  #             same way). Now matches the label field exactly.
   47  #   item 7  - @emlMannWhitneyU used a combined-N exact/approximate
   48  #             threshold and ignored ties; it now uses R's rule
   49  #             (n1 < 50 AND n2 < 50 AND no ties).
   50  #             @emlWilcoxonSignedRank keeps its n < 50 threshold (which
   51  #             already matched R) and adds the ties/zeroes conditions.
   52  #             One-tailed p-values in both returned the smaller tail
   53  #             regardless of direction; the alternative is now fixed as
   54  #             H1: group1 > group2 (matching R), with .pGreater/.pLess
   55  #             exposed. .tails is validated in both, and in
   56  #             @emlRankBiserialR / @emlMatchedPairsR.
   57  #   item 8  - @emlBonferroni, @emlHolm and @emlBenjaminiHochberg counted
   58  #             undefined elements in the comparison count. All three are
   59  #             now NA-safe (undefined in, undefined out; k excludes
   60  #             undefined), matching R's p.adjust.
   61  #   item 9  - @emlPairwiseT, @emlDunnTest and @emlPairwiseWilcoxon
   62  #             substituted rawP = 1 for a failed comparison, which reads
   63  #             as a computed non-significant result. All three now
   64  #             propagate undefined and report .nSkipped / .skipReason$.
   65  #   item 10 - @emlPairwiseT.method$ echoed the ADJUSTMENT argument, so
   66  #             report headers read "Pairwise holm". It now names the test
   67  #             ("Welch t-test" / "Student t-test"); the adjustment moved
   68  #             to .adjustMethod$.
   69  #   item 11 - @emlEpsilonSquared capped at 1 with .capped / .warning$,
   70  #             and guarded for .n <= 1.
   71  #   item 12 - @emlCohenD Hedges' J documentation corrected (worst-case
   72  #             error is 1.28% at df = 2, not 0.27%). Formula unchanged.
   73  #   item 13 - @emlOneWayAnova sums of squares computed by a two-pass
   74  #             centred algorithm instead of the raw-score Hays formulas,
   75  #             removing catastrophic cancellation on large-offset data.
   76  #
   77  # v1.2: @emlTukeyHSD internal sort removed — group order now controlled
   78  #        by @emlCountGroups via emlGroupSortAlphabetical global.

## plugin/stats/eml-output.praat @ 49ce40b

Lines 6-65 of plugin/stats/eml-output.praat at 49ce40b.

```
    6  # v2.4: TWO ENTRY GUARDS ON @emlSavePanel, a receipt that wraps, and a probe
    7  #       that classifies rather than assumes.
    8  #
    9  #       @eml_saveSafeBaseName makes the typed Base name into a file name
   10  #         ONCE, before the collision walk, so a "/" cannot reach writeFile:
   11  #         verbatim -- which stops the script inside @emlSavePanel, so that the
   12  #         receipt never draws, the panel never returns, and the caller's
   13  #         Done | Save | Draw | New loop goes with the analysis behind it. One
   14  #         pass, before the walk, so every file of one press still shares one
   15  #         base name and one stamp.
   16  #       @eml_saveFolderWritable asks, once and with `nocheck` so the asking
   17  #         cannot itself abort, whether the target folder and its PARENT can
   18  #         be written. An unwritable target reports "unexpected error 30" and
   19  #         kills the session the same way; the panel RETURNS on a no.
   20  #       THE "Saved" RECEIPT WRAPS. It reserves one line per comment:, and the
   21  #         toolkit draws several when a path is longer than the dialog, so an
   22  #         unwrapped long path prints its tail over the path below. Every line
   23  #         goes through @emlWrapText at 62 characters, the width measured on
   24  #         6.6.30 and the one @emlErrorDialog uses.
   25  #       @eml_auditLabelColumn CLASSIFIES THE COLUMN, never assumes numeric.
   26  #         @emlWrapperInit's coercion arms manufacture a "row" column out of
   27  #         row labels that a Matrix never has and a TableOfReal may not have.
   28  #         Praat renders those undefined cells as "?", which is neither of the
   29  #         two forms @eml_strictNumericColumn's scan recognises, so the
   30  #         numericiser behind it would raise before the wrapper's dialog opened
   31  #         -- on EVERY Matrix, every unlabelled TableOfReal and every partially
   32  #         labelled one. It does not invent default labels, so it composes with
   33  #         whatever the conversion side decides to put there.
   34  # v2.3: The one CSV export journey is @emlSavePanel plus
   35  #       @emlExportResultFiles. The folder seed a Save button needs sits at
   36  #       file scope above @emlSavePanel, where every caller reaches it.
   37  # v2.2: @emlSavePanel -- one save, one folder, one name. The figure, the
   38  #       result frames and the Info window report are written together under a
   39  #       shared stem, rather than the figure and the CSV being two journeys
   40  #       with two folder memories and two naming rules and the report not being
   41  #       savable at all.
   42  # v2.1: Both arms of @emlExportResultFiles are non-destructive.
   43  #       @emlGenerateUniquePath lives in eml-core-utilities.praat, which is
   44  #       included before this file, so the declared arm can reach it. The BASE
   45  #       is uniqued once against the tidy frame and every frame in the set
   46  #       carries the walked suffix, so a set never half-overwrites an older
   47  #       set.
   48  # v2.0: THE MIGRATION FORK IS A PROCEDURE, @emlExportResultFiles, and the
   49  #       graphs form's export goes through it, so one analysis cannot write
   50  #       three broom-shaped files from the stats menu and one legacy
   51  #       long-format file from the graphs form. Writing only -- the two
   52  #       callers report differently, and a shared procedure cannot open a
   53  #       dialog from inside another one.
   54  # v1.9: Comments only — no executable line changed.
   55  # v1.8: @emlFormatEffectLabel recognises "r_squared" (and the aliases "R2"
   56  #         and "r2") and applies Cohen's R-squared benchmarks 0.01 / 0.09 /
   57  #         0.25 rather than Cohen's d thresholds. An unrecognised effect type
   58  #         does not fall back to d thresholds: .label$ is "" and the
   59  #         .recognized flag is 0, so callers can detect the condition.
   60  #       @emlFormatP reports "p > .999" for p in [0.9995, 1) rather than
   61  #         "p = 1.000", which would overstate the result as an exact 1.
   62  #         p = 1 exactly still formats as "p = 1.000".
   63  # v1.7: @emlWrapperInit accepts Table, TableOfReal, and Matrix objects.
   64  #       Auto-converts TableOfReal/Matrix to Table with Info window notification.
   65  #       Outputs .converted flag for caller awareness.
```

    1  # EML Praat Tools — Correctness Fix Bundle
    2  Date: 2026-07-21 · Applied by: Claude (Anthropic), for Ian Howell / EML
    3  All fixes verified with Praat 6.6.30 (barren) against numpy/scipy references; graph edits additionally
    4  reviewed by an independent adversarial pass (one regression caught and reverted — see L).
    5  
    6  ## A. Missing-data fix (audit C1/C2)
    7  - stats/eml-analysis.praat (1.0→1.1): emlRunPairedAnalysis + emlRunCorrelationAnalysis now use
    8    row-wise complete-case extraction (@emlExtractPairedColumns), preserving pairing; analyzed n =
    9    complete pairs; "rows excluded for missing data" note added.
   10  - stats/eml-extract.praat (1.3→1.4): new @eml_getGroupPairedData (complete-case within a group).
   11  - scripts/eml-correlate.praat (3.2→3.3): per-group correlation uses @eml_getGroupPairedData.
   12    Verified: paired n=4/t=1.6977/p=0.1881, correlation n=6/r=0.9938 — match scipy.
   13  
   14  ## B. Graph fixes (audit H1, H3, M3, M4, L)
   15  - graphs/eml-graph-procedures.praat (3.20→3.21):
   16    * H1 — box & violin quartiles now call the shared R-7 @emlQuartiles instead of nearest-rank
   17      floor(n*p) (which biased the median low and collapsed it onto the minimum at small n; figure
   18      now agrees with the describe table). Verified: emlQuartiles == numpy R-7 for n=3/4/5.
   19    * M3 — bar auto-range now tracks emlBarData_visibleMin so negative means aren't clipped at 0.
   20    * L — REVERTED (see below).
   21  - graphs/eml-draw-procedures.praat (1.17→1.18):
   22    * H3 — grouped scatter no longer prints a false "R² = 0.000" for Spearman/Theil-Sen; R² emitted
   23      only for the OLS/Pearson line (guarded on annotCorrType$, mirroring the ungrouped path).
   24    * M3 — bar y-range routed through @emlComputeAxisRange with the tracked min; bar baseline
   25      decoupled from yMin (bars emanate from 0). Verified: neg bars axis -10..10; pos bars keep 0.
   26    * M4 — scatter axes use adaptive @emlComputeNiceStep roundTo instead of hardcoded 1. Verified:
   27      fractional data 0.45..0.55 now maps to axis 0.44..0.58 (was 0..1).
   28  - graphs/eml-graphs-form.praat (1.8→1.9):
   29    * M3 — closes the annotated/bracket sub-case so negative-mean bars with brackets also floor
   30      correctly (positive data unchanged via the axis procedure's non-negative guard).
   31  
   32  ## L — B/W sprite (NOT fixed; reverted)
   33  An initial attempt set sprite$ to an RGB grey in B/W mode. Adversarial review found sprite$ holds
   34  PNG-filename STEMS (bw01..bw10), not colors, so the RGB string produced a non-existent filename and
   35  silently disabled alpha transparency. The change was reverted. Properly fixing the cosmetic
   36  grey-ordering mismatch requires mapping each computed grey to the nearest bwNN sprite (needs the
   37  sprite grey-level table) — deferred, low priority.
   38  
   39  ---
   40  
   41  ## 5 August 2026 — D88, adaptive axis rounding
   42  
   43  **Closes finding D88.** Verified by GUI drive on Praat 6.6.30, not by inspection.
   44  
   45  `@emlComputeAxisRange` takes a `roundTo` argument that sets the granularity
   46  the axis bounds snap to. Twelve of its seventeen call sites passed a literal
   47  `10`, which fixes that granularity at ten units regardless of the data's
   48  magnitude. Any measure whose full range is small relative to 10 was
   49  compressed into the bottom of the panel.
   50  
   51  Ten sites now derive `roundTo` from the data via `@emlComputeNiceStep`,
   52  which is the same nice-number logic the gridlines already use:
   53  
   54  - `graphs/eml-draw-procedures.praat` — `@emlDrawTimeSeries`,
   55    `@emlDrawTimeSeriesCI`, `@emlDrawSpaghettiPlot`, `@emlDrawBarChart`,
   56    `@emlDrawViolinPlot`, `@emlDrawBoxPlot`, `@emlDrawGroupedViolin`,
   57    `@emlDrawGroupedBoxPlot`
   58  - `graphs/eml-graphs-form.praat` — both auto-range branches in
   59    `@emlGraphsWorkflow` (bar, and violin/box)
   60  
   61  This is the same change made to the scatter path on 21 July under M4 in
   62  section B above, which was applied there and not propagated. The comment at
   63  the scatter site already named the failing case — "fractional data
   64  (proportions, reaction times, jitter %)".
   65  
   66  **Follow-up, same day: the propagation was completed to 15 of 17 sites.**
   67  The two `@emlDrawF0Contour` sites and the waveform amplitude site
   68  (`roundTo = 0.05`) are now adaptive too. The concern that adaptive rounding
   69  would defeat the F0 minimum-span logic was unfounded: the 50 Hz and
   70  12-semitone corrections run *after* the axis call and widen the result when
   71  it is too tight, so a narrower derived range is corrected upward exactly as
   72  before. On the 50 Hz span the derived granularity reproduces the previous
   73  10 Hz.
   74  
   75  **The two histogram sites keep their literal `5`.** Making them adaptive was
   76  attempted twice and backed out both times, with the failures evidenced —
   77  see finding D91. Frequency is a count, and while the derived bounds are
   78  correct, the tick *labels* come from `@emlDrawAxes`, which takes no tick
   79  constraint, so the axis ends up labelled in halves. Fixing it properly means
   80  changing a procedure every draw path calls.
   81  
   82  **Verification — two cases driven end to end through the GUI:**
   83  
   84  | Input | Data range | Axis before | Axis after |
   85  |---|---|---|---|
   86  | `evidence/csv/demo_paired_input.csv`, jitter | 0.528 – 4.191 | 0 – 10 | 0 – 5 |
   87  | `validate/redpath/r7_small_range_measure.csv`, contact quotient | 0.401 – 0.548 | 0 – 10 | ≈0.38 – 0.56, ticks every 0.05 |
   88  
   89  Figures: `evidence/figures/d88_FIXED_spaghetti_jitter.png` and
   90  `d88_FIXED_spaghetti_CQ.png`, against the pre-fix
   91  `d88_spaghetti_axis_0to10.png`.
   92  
   93  The contact-quotient case is the one that matters: on a 0–10 axis that data
   94  occupied under 2% of the panel height.
   95  
   96  
   97  ## 5 August 2026 — F0 axis: minimum-span floors removed
   98  
   99  The F0 contour axis enforced a minimum visible span: 50 Hz in hertz, 12
  100  semitones rounded to 6 in semitones. Both are gone. The axis now follows the
  101  data, and any extra room a figure needs comes from
  102  `@emlComputeAnnotationHeadroom` at the annotation stage, which is where it
  103  belongs — headroom is a property of what is drawn on the figure, not of the
  104  unit on the axis.
  105  
  106  **These floors were a workaround for the D88 granularity bug, not a
  107  considered design.** The v1.15 note above records Bug #11 as "semitone
  108  auto-range minimum span enforcement now uses direct 6-st rounding instead of
  109  re-calling @emlComputeAxisRange. Prevents overshoot to ~30 st for
  110  constant-pitch input." That overshoot was `roundTo = 10` applied to a
  111  semitone axis: a near-constant pitch produced a tiny range that snapped out
  112  to a 10-unit grid. With the granularity now derived from the data, the
  113  overshoot cannot occur and the workaround has nothing left to prevent.
  114  
  115  **Verified by driving `@emlDrawF0Contour` on a 220 Hz tone with 2.9 Hz of
  116  drift:**
  117  
  118  | Unit | Measured span | Axis | Ticks |
  119  |---|---|---|---|
  120  | Hertz | 220.040 – 222.960 (2.9 Hz) | 219.5 – 223.5 | 0.5 Hz |
  121  | Semitones | same signal, ≈0.23 st | −12.05 – −11.70 | 0.05 st |
  122  
  123  Under the old floors the hertz figure would have spanned roughly 195–245 and
  124  the drift would have read as a flat line. The semitone figure shows no trace
  125  of the Bug #11 overshoot.
  126  
  127  Figures: `evidence/figures/d88_f0_steady_hz_2.9Hz_span.png`,
  128  `d88_f0_steady_semitones.png`.
  129  
  130  **One hard-coded pair remains in this procedure and is not a defect.** When
  131  `Get minimum`/`Get maximum` return undefined — no voiced frames anywhere —
  132  the axis falls back to 75–500 Hz or −36 to +6 st. There is no data to derive
  133  a range from in that case, and an empty figure still needs an axis.
  134  
  135  ## 5 August 2026 — D91: the count-axis constraint
  136  
  137  Closes D91 and completes the D88 propagation. **All 16 `@emlComputeAxisRange`
  138  call sites now derive their granularity from the data.** No literal remains.
  139  
  140  New global `emlYAxisMinStep`: 0 means unconstrained, a positive value is the
  141  smallest step the y-axis may take. Declared in `@emlInitDrawingDefaults`,
  142  guarded in `@emlSetAdaptiveTheme`, honoured by the four procedures that turn
  143  a range into a step — `@emlDrawGridlines` (y only),
  144  `@emlDrawHorizontalGridlines`, `@emlDrawAlignedMarksLeft` and
  145  `@emlDrawAlignedMarksRight`. `@emlDrawHistogram` sets it to 1 and releases it
  146  before returning, so a frequency axis stays on whole counts while its range
  147  comes from the data.
  148  
  149  A global rather than a parameter: the constraint has to reach axis bounds,
  150  gridlines and tick labels alike, and threading it would have changed five
  151  signatures across roughly thirty call sites. The plugin already carries
  152  cross-cutting display state this way.
  153  
  154  **Two hazards, both commented at the site because neither is visible from the
  155  code:**
  156  
  157  - The reset must not go in `@emlSetAdaptiveTheme` unconditionally.
  158    `@emlDrawAxes` calls that procedure again partway through a figure, so an
  159    unconditional reset clears the constraint immediately before the ticks are
  160    drawn. The figure then comes out unchanged with no clue why.
  161  - The global must be guarded with `variableExists`. A draw procedure can be
  162    entered without `@emlInitDrawingDefaults` having run, and Praat aborts the
  163    whole figure at the first comparison against an undefined global.
  164  
  165  Both were caught by driving, not by reading the diff.
  166  
  167  **Verified:** histogram at max count 3 → 0–4 with ticks 0 1 2 3 4 (the old
  168  literal `5` gave 0–5); at max count 5 → 0–6 integral, x-axis still fractional
  169  at 1.5, 2, 2.5; a continuous figure first in a fresh session draws clean with
  170  0.05 ticks; the same figure drawn immediately after a histogram is identical,
  171  so the constraint does not leak.
  172  
  173  ## 5 August 2026 — D92: the annotated path pinned violin and box axes to zero
  174  
  175  Found by verifying that annotation headroom, not a minimum span, supplies the
  176  extra y-axis room a figure needs — the check the F0 span removal implied.
  177  
  178  `@emlGraphsWorkflow`'s auto-range branch, which runs only when brackets are
  179  drawn, passed a literal `0` as the data minimum for every non-bar graph type
  180  and never assigned `valueMin`. The same violin plot therefore drew on 70–105
  181  unannotated and **0–170** annotated, with the data in the bottom 14% of the
  182  panel.
  183  
  184  Worse than wasteful: `@emlComputeAnnotationHeadroom` scales its expansion by
  185  `yDataRange`, so a range inflated from 30 dB to 100 dB by the zero floor
  186  produced about three times the headroom actually needed.
  187  
  188  Fixed by tracking `visibleDataMin` alongside `visibleDataMax` and passing it.
  189  `@emlComputeAxisRange`'s non-negative guard still holds the floor at 0 for
  190  data that never goes below it.
  191  
  192  **Verified:** annotated violin, SPL 75.6–99.3 dB with three brackets, went
  193  from 0–170 to **75–110** — data filling the panel, brackets at 100–106,
  194  headroom carrying the rest. The unannotated figure is unchanged at 70–105.
  195  The annotated **bar chart is unchanged at 0–170** and must be: bars emanate
  196  from the origin, and that branch already passed `emlBarData_visibleMin`.
  197  
  198  ## 5 August 2026 — D4: kurtosis labels
  199  
  200  Three wrappers printed `Kurtosis` for a value that is **excess** kurtosis
  201  (Fisher's *g*₂, normal = 0). `@emlKurtosis` computes it correctly and
  202  documents it correctly; only the display labels were short.
  203  
  204  - `scripts/eml-describe-table.praat` → `Kurtosis (excess)`
  205  - `scripts/eml-check-normality.praat` → `Kurtosis (excess) = `
  206  - `scripts/eml-wizard.praat` → `Kurtosis (excess):`
  207  
  208  `stats/eml-analysis.praat` already qualified it and is unchanged. Verified by
  209  driving *Describe Table column*: `Kurtosis (excess)   -0.5555`.
  210  
  211  **The threshold is a separate question and is deliberately untouched.** Three
  212  sites disagree — the shape flag uses `|excess| >= 3`, the wizard's classifier
  213  uses `|excess| < 1`, and the sentence that classifier prints tells the reader
  214  `|excess| < 3` is typical. The second and third contradict each other inside
  215  one procedure. Which convention the plugin should teach is an author ruling,
  216  recorded as D10.
  217  
  218  ## 5 August 2026 — D15: paired effect sizes matched to their tests
  219  
  220  Author ruling: match the effect size to the test, show both when the user
  221  asks for both.
  222  
  223  `@emlMatchedPairsR` was called unconditionally, so a paired *t*-test printed
  224  the Wilcoxon rank-biserial correlation under the heading **Effect Size**,
  225  directly beneath the *t*, df and *p* of a test it does not belong to.
  226  
  227  - New `@emlCohenDz` in `stats/eml-inferential.praat`: *d*z = mean(v1−v2) /
  228    sd(v1−v2), plus *r* = *t* / √(*t*² + df).
  229  - `@emlRunPairedAnalysis` calls `@emlCohenDz` on the parametric branch and
  230    `@emlMatchedPairsR` on the nonparametric branch.
  231  - The paired-*t* block reports Cohen's *d*z and *r* (from *t*); the Wilcoxon
  232    block still reports matched-pairs *r* under **Nonparametric Effect Size**,
  233    where it was always correct. CSV `effect_type` for a parametric run is now
  234    `Cohen's dz`.
  235  
  236  Verified by drive on `demo_paired`: *d*z 1.728, *r* from *t* 0.871, and in
  237  "Both" mode the Wilcoxon section adds matched-pairs *r* 0.971. All three
  238  match R.
  239  
  240  `validate/v06` rewritten from pinning the defect to asserting the fix, with
  241  a guard that the two effect sizes stay numerically distinct.
```
